# Android / iOS feature parity

Goal: implement all user-facing iOS capabilities on Android, using Android-native equivalents for system integrations. Source baseline: current working copies of this repository and ../gitsync.md as of 2026-10-28. Existing uncommitted changes are preserved.

A feature is complete only when its production path is connected and appropriate automated and device evidence proves it. Backend-only methods and visible no-op controls do not count. The full goal remains active until every category below is implemented and verified.

| Requirement | Status / acceptance evidence needed |
| --- | --- |
| Safe pull, fresh planning, explicit divergence handling | In progress: plans and pulls follow the configured upstream remote/branch, pin branch/HEAD/config/folder/credential/account identity across fetch, verify fresh remote advertisement and preserve ignored/untracked checkout collisions. Pulls remain fast-forward-only under repository serialization. Reviewed configured-upstream merging now offers local-only or acknowledged merge-and-push, persistent conflicts and same-commit push retry. Real Git and native production editor evidence is recorded below. Broader external-tool/transport/device qualification remains. |
| Selected tag publishing with verified remote acknowledgement | Implemented for the current configured-origin path: exact tag refspec and pinned object, rejected missing/failed acknowledgement, fresh selected-row identity, and production Android annotated-tag creation/publication against a real local remote. Other local tags and the branch remain unpublished. Wider provider/endpoint-change/device qualification remains. |
| Push existing commits / retry without making another commit | Implemented for the current remote/credential path: explicit branch push and verified acknowledgement, new-branch/idempotent core tests, production Android push after local commit and failed-push retry without another commit. Broader authentication/remote support remains below. |
| Manual conflict editor / complete files / binary / rename conflicts | In progress: editor connected and seeded with ours. Complete index-stage file content and binary/large-file edit guards are implemented and tested. Rename/rename, rename/delete and detected rename/edit merge paths are connected with real-Git and production Android evidence below. Single-parent replay rename grouping and the rebase filename editor are now connected below. Clean/conflicting text virtual bases now retain verified split-rename grouping for app-created merges. Broader ambiguous/overlapping/binary/deletion/device qualification remains. |

Duplicate submissions and stale branch commits are rejected. Status includes deletions and counts paths once. The Git status card now reads full HEAD and persisted completion metadata, showing a short SHA and locale-formatted time; unknown/unborn states are explicit. Core and private/media/production UI evidence is below. Repository-list/vault metadata is connected with lightweight lifecycle-owned reads and shared formatting; production evidence is below. App-operation invalidation now refreshes foreground list/vault/Git views, and headings use saved catalog names/aliases and remote owners. Evidence is below. Foreground external-tool changes now refresh visible cards, vault/Git, browser and diff views; evidence and bounded fallback are below. Broader completion paths and wider filesystem/API/cold-process/device qualification remain. Reviewed discard now pins single/all scope, repository/index/checkout identity and byte revisions; matches iOS untracked-file and unborn-branch behavior; protects ignored files; and holds the real index lock through private-index publication and operation cleanup. All 690 core tests pass, including 18 discard cases; production dialogs and six native regressions compile. ## Verification log

- Initial offline build could not resolve uncached Compose debug dependencies; online dependency resolution succeeded.
- First online build compiled Kotlin but failed Android's JDK image transform under the existing Java 21 Gradle daemon. Retrying with Java 17.
- Java 17 build passed: `:core:test :app:assembleDebug`. Core results: 139 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped. Integration suites cover selected-tag isolation/rejection, all-tag rejection propagation, new-branch/idempotent pushes, divergent/fast-forward pulls, full conflict-side preservation, binary edit protection, and Stage All including deletions/ignore rules.
- No app store publication or external Git pushes are performed by these tests.

- Emulator smoke check (emulator-5554, isolated `md.gitsync.md.debug` installation): opened sample → Git screen → Stage All. Screen changed to `1 STAGED` / `UNSTAGE`. Tapped Push Current Branch without entering a commit message; `adb shell run-as` confirmed local and bare-remote main refs both `d468eb6d5537948d7a262c26e9f8710a1e24317d`. This is manual/runtime evidence, not a complete automated E2E pass.
- Debug builds now use `.debug` application ID suffix, preserving release installations/data. Initial attempt to replace release failed due to signature mismatch; no release installation was removed.
- Argent accessibility helper returned stale trees across navigation; scoped helper resets restored discovery. Recorder also captured a role-only repository target. Saved the unfinished trace under `.argent/recordings/parity-git-controls.incomplete.yaml` for diagnosis. It is not a passing E2E test and must be repaired/re-recorded and replayed before counting automated UI verification.
- Next work: safe vault relocation and recovery, history branch/account clone defaults, remaining Git recovery workflows, SSH, rebase, LFS, publishing, background/push sync, diagnostics and automation. Device/cloud-provider and release qualification remain in scope alongside every other pending matrix requirement.

### File browser/editor and operation coordination (2026-10-27)

- Added `RepositoryOperationCoordinator`, shared by all JGit repositories and `RepositoryFiles`, with canonical alias locking, sequential reentrancy, cancellation-safe release and fail-fast child/cross-repository nesting. Compound commit-and-push now retains the same lease.
- Added production routes from Vault → Browse files → recursive folders → File editor. The browser lists real status badges and supports creation/rename. Editor highlights Swift, Markdown, JSON, YAML, JavaScript/TypeScript, Python, shell, HTML/XML and CSS; highlighting leaves character offsets unchanged and is debounced/capped.
- File writes stream to a sibling temp file, fsync, preserve POSIX permissions when supported, check original content revision twice, and replace atomically (with a platform fallback). Traversal, metadata access and symlinks are rejected. External modifications retain the user's draft and newer disk content. Binary, non-UTF-8 and >2 MB documents cannot be overwritten through the text editor.
- Core result: **158 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips (`:core:test`). Debug app and Android test APK builds passed using Java 17. A transient disk-full error interrupted Kotlin incremental-cache writes during one rebuild; available space recovered and a non-incremental rebuild succeeded. No user files/caches were removed.
- Native Android result: **8 tests passed** on GitSync-specific API 35 emulator `emulator-5556` (`FilesScreenTest`, `FileNavigationTest`). Includes Android/media atomic saves, create/rename/open, saved/unsaved Back, exact Unicode content, external-edit protection, binary deletion confirmation, light/dark/160% font layouts, dirty rename with path restoration, and production vault → browser → editor → Git diff navigation. Test fixtures and temporary preference changes are restored/removed by the tests.
- The first production-navigation run exposed a real immediate hardware-Back timing gap. The editor now keeps its handler installed and reads the current ViewModel state at dispatch; the same test passes without adding delays or weakening its expectation.
- Visual screenshots were retrieved to `/tmp/syncmd-file-evidence/parity-evidence/` and inspected. Screen recordings are local untracked diagnostics under `.argent/recordings/`; they include initial/failing runs and are **not** a final motion/performance acceptance artifact. No 60 fps or complete motion audit is claimed.
- Native replay command after installing the current debug + AndroidTest APKs: `adb -s emulator-5556 shell am instrument -w -e class com.bontecou.syncmd.FileNavigationTest,com.bontecou.syncmd.FilesScreenTest md.gitsync.md.debug.test/androidx.test.runner.AndroidJUnitRunner`.

### Repository lifecycle and safe removal (2026-10-27)

- Added Local repositories from the repository list. It scans app-managed roots without following symlinks, validates exact working-tree roots, shows branch/change/remote summaries, and reconnects explicitly selected copies in a batch. Scans are bounded and expose warnings. This does **not** provide arbitrary SAF folder import or custom clone destinations.
- Removing an entry now keeps its working files and local Git history by default. A separate confirmed action deletes only validated app-managed clones. Removed repositories retain reconnect history, remote and branch information. Legacy path aliases are canonicalized; settings mutations are serialized across ViewModels and committed before success callbacks. The former implicit folder-copy migration was removed.
- Clones use a unique owned staging directory and publish only into an absent destination. Existing destinations, including ones created during cloning, are never cleaned up. Git symlinks use regular pointer files with the correct index mode on Android storage, preserving a clean checkout.
- Remove Git tracking requires metadata review and a final confirmation. The default backup verifies a full metadata copy plus a ZIP, then retains the original metadata in the backup before disconnecting the working tree. The optional no-backup action explicitly warns about permanent history loss. Shared metadata, locks, symlinks, nested repositories, changed review state and unsafe backup locations are rejected. Working files stay in place.
- Tracking removal writes a recovery journal before detachment. Startup reconciles interrupted actions and commits repository history plus journal cleanup together. A repeated device run exposed a publication race in the earlier two-step recovery; the final implementation fixes it without adding test delays or weakening expectations.
- Android uses native `Os.stat`/`Os.rename` for same-volume metadata moves because `Files.getFileStore` attempts a blocked mount-table read on API 35. Backup sharing uses a restricted, read-only FileProvider grant for backup directories.
- Core result: **172 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips. Java 17 debug and AndroidTest APK builds passed. Native result: **16 tests passed** on `emulator-5556`, covering all repository lifecycle and prior browser/editor/navigation tests. The eight repository lifecycle tests include real Android/media clone/backup operations, retained-file reconnect after reload, confirmation/cancellation, deletion without following links, metadata ZIP sharing, interrupted removal before/after detachment and legacy path aliases.
- The eight-test repository lifecycle suite passed again after the recovery fix, including both interrupted-removal cases. `git diff --check` passed.
- Final local-repository screenshot was retrieved to `/tmp/syncmd-repository-evidence/repositories-local.png` and visually inspected. No external Git pushes or store publication occurred. Full motion, accessibility and arbitrary-storage acceptance remain open.

### Accurate index/worktree diffs and local commits (2026-10-27)

- Audited the current iOS `LocalGitService.commitLocal` implementation: it commits the prepared index and supports an unborn HEAD. Android now exposes **Commit locally**, alongside explicit **Commit & Push** and **Push Current Branch**. Local commits work without a remote or signed-in account, preserve unstaged work and reject blank/no-change commits or an active Git operation.
- Replaced HEAD-to-worktree status guessing with separate HEAD-to-index and index-to-worktree diffs. A partially staged path has two clearly labelled rows, and each diff route carries its layer. A staged change remains visible even when the working file is restored to HEAD. Initial commits and unstage-all on unborn branches preserve the intended working files. Rename stage/unstage acts on both paths; paths and modes come from JGit entries rather than patch-header guesses, including quoted/non-ASCII pure renames and binary files.
- Unified the two production status readers. Deletions and conflicts are included, staged/unstaged flags coexist, and one path counts once in the overview. The vault distinguishes partially staged files and staged/unstaged deletion. Git status, branch and history summaries refresh after index/commit/push actions.
- DiffViewModel gates mutations synchronously before coroutine launch, invalidates stale reads and selection when the repository changes, preserves drafts typed during an operation, and keeps the same repository lease through commit/push/status refresh. It checks that the displayed branch is still current before commit-and-push or branch push. A failed push leaves the local commit visible and allows retry without another commit. Commit-message/file-content debug prints were removed from this path.
- Core result: **181 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips. Added real JGit tests for two-layer partial staging, restored-to-HEAD worktree, staged deletion plus recreated file, unborn unstage and initial commit, blank/empty commit rejection, exact binary/rename metadata, rename actions, path guards, and distinct status/deletion counts. Java 17 debug and AndroidTest APK builds passed; `git diff --check` passed.
- Native Android result: **21 tests passed** on API 35 `emulator-5556`, including all earlier repository/file tests. Production navigation now verifies both partial-file diff layers, a local-only commit whose blob is the staged version, retained later working edits, untouched remote before explicit push, push without another commit, and a first local commit with no remote. Three ViewModel/device tests separately verify duplicate submission suppression, failed-push retry, and stale-branch rejection before history is written. The final full native run passed again after status refresh and navigation-inset changes.
- Inspected `/tmp/syncmd-repository-evidence/git-local-commit.png`; the final Git and diff screens reserve Android navigation-bar space. The initial new navigation-test run stopped at case-mismatched labels from the existing uppercase button component; the corrected checks passed with the actual labels. No test expectations for Git contents or refs were weakened. No external Git pushes or store publication occurred.
- Remaining scope in this category includes destructive discard/merge/recovery edge cases, other ViewModels' asynchronous staleness guards, and full dark/large-font/long-content/accessibility/motion acceptance. No complete motion/performance audit is claimed. All other pending parity categories remain in scope.

### HTTPS remote connections and scoped credentials (2026-10-27)

- Added a production **Clone from URL** route directly from the repository list. The native form accepts a repository display name, HTTPS URL, optional branch, and either explicit public access or username plus token/password. Public access suppresses implicit GitHub credentials. URLs with embedded credentials, query tokens, fragments, invalid ports or path traversal are rejected before networking. Secrets are held only in memory until submission and are never placed in a navigation URL or normal repository preferences.
- Manual clones retain the staged-publication/absent-destination protections, select the requested branch and set its upstream. Branch errors clean up only the owned staging directory. A credential policy is saved before publication; the repository is registered only after cloning and credential persistence succeed. Display names never determine the filesystem path. Custom folder selection and SSH remain separate pending requirements.
- Added `RepositoryCredentialStore` using device-bound Android Keystore AES-256-GCM. Each repository record has a fresh IV and canonical-path-derived authenticated data. Explicit anonymous policies survive restart, changed remote addresses fail closed, and ciphertext transplanted between repository records is rejected. The encrypted preferences are excluded from cloud backup and device transfer. That batch left GitHub OAuth/PAT preferences unmigrated; the verified session migration is recorded below. Multi-account support remains open.
- All production clone, remote fetch/push/tag-push and pull transports now use scoped credential providers. The legacy GitHub token is not even requested for another host, insecure scheme or nonstandard port. Repository policies override that fallback. Push credentials use the configured push URL, and multiple ambiguous remote URLs are rejected for review.
- Added an HTTP connection factory guard to check every request against the selected HTTPS host, port and repository path. This also blocks redirects after an Authorization header has already been cached. New HTTPS clones retain certificate checks; the connection form provides no trust-all or invalid-certificate acceptance path. A redirected repository requires its final HTTPS URL.
- Core result: **188 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips. A real loopback HTTPS server backed by `git http-backend` verifies authenticated clone of a non-default branch, push, fetch, fast-forward pull, anonymous clone without Authorization, untrusted-certificate rejection before credentials reach the server, and authenticated same-host redirect rejection before any request reaches the other repository. The generated certificate is trusted only temporarily in that test JVM and trust is restored afterward. Additional tests cover URL/credential scoping, explicit anonymous override, push URL selection and missing-branch cleanup. The HTTPS fixture was repaired to decompress Git request bodies and provide an explicit local fetch refspec; security/content expectations were retained.
- Android result: **26 native tests passed** on API 35 `emulator-5556`, including all prior file/lifecycle/Git workflow tests. Four connection tests verify real Android Keystore encryption/reload/address binding, anonymous policy and swapped-record rejection, full form submission with selected-branch checkout and registration, and pre-network URL validation. The form's Android clone fixture substitutes an on-device file remote; real HTTPS transport proof is supplied by the separate core integration test. Production home → URL form → Back navigation also passes. Java 17 app and AndroidTest builds passed; `git diff --check` passed.
- Light/dark form screenshots were captured with the keyboard dismissed at `/tmp/syncmd-repository-evidence/remote-clone-light.png` and `remote-clone-dark.png` and visually inspected. Passwords are masked and controls remain above Android navigation space. URL normalization preserves percent-escaped repository paths and encodes Unicode without changing the selected repository. The four connection tests passed again during this visual check.
- Current remaining scope: connection editing and credential refresh/removal UI; full saved branch/auth restoration when cloning from history; multi-account and GitHub credential migration; arbitrary storage; SSH; and all other pending categories in the matrix. Full motion/performance/accessibility acceptance is still open. No external repositories were pushed and no store publication occurred.

Implementation references: [Android Keystore key-generation API](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/security/keystore/KeyGenParameterSpec), [JGit 5.13 HTTP transport and redirect handling](https://archive.eclipse.org/jgit/site/5.13.0.202109080827-r/xref/org/eclipse/jgit/transport/TransportHttp.html).

### Existing-repository connection editing (2026-10-27)

- Added Local repositories → Edit connection → Repository connection. Existing working copies can keep their saved token without displaying it, replace username/token, explicitly select public access, or select the currently signed-in GitHub account for a standard github.com HTTPS URL. Changing the address disables keeping a saved secret; public access, a replacement token or GitHub account selection remains explicit. Saving explicitly uses that address for fetch and push and shows any prior separate push address. The repository catalog refreshes only after config and credential persistence succeed.
- Added `RepositoryConnection` with exact-root validation, config content revisions, metadata identity checks, Git's config lock and a fsynced prepared write. Concurrent config edits and pre-existing lock files are rejected. Writes preserve working files, staged index contents, HEAD/local refs and unrelated remote settings; a previously unconfigured origin receives its fetch/upstream mapping without creating a commit.
- Credential writes check an encrypted-record revision so stale editor forms cannot overwrite a newer token. They occur before config publication under the same repository lease. A failed config commit restores the exact previous encrypted record. Preference-write errors are also rolled back, including Android's case where an unsuccessful disk commit has already updated memory.
- GitHub-account selection is stored as an encrypted endpoint-bound reference, without copying the OAuth token into a repository record. If a process stops between policy persistence and address publication, the new secret/account policy rejects the old endpoint; the editor can repair the connection. This is fail-closed manual recovery, not an automatic transaction journal. The later GitHub-session batch below migrates global token preferences. Multi-account support remains open.
- Removing saved credentials requires confirmation, leaves fetch/push URLs untouched and selects explicit public access. That policy refuses app credentials for all of the repository's remotes, including a separate push URL, so it does not silently fall back to the signed-in GitHub account. Re-entering a credential later remains available through the editor.
- A changed-host history entry now trusts its saved remote instead of falling back to an old GitHub display alias. It cannot offer to clone a former GitHub repository merely because the working copy retained its old label. General non-GitHub history cloning and full branch/auth restoration remain open.
- Core result: **193 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips. Five new tests cover config/index/ref preservation, stale review and lock rejection, credential persistence failure, noncooperating config changes during a save, and origin/upstream setup on an unborn branch. The real smart-HTTPS integration test also rejects an expired password, refreshes authentication through the connection-save path, and fetches successfully without cloning again or changing HEAD.
- Native result: **37 tests passed** on API 35 `emulator-5556`, including all previous connection/file/lifecycle/Git workflow tests and the new production editor route and changed-host history guard. Editor scenarios cover: address/token replacement with identical index/HEAD/working contents, keeping a saved token without exposing/retyping it, confirmation/cancellation of token removal with separate push URLs retained, credential-record stale edits, config-commit rollback, preference-commit failure rollback, interrupted secret/account policy repair, signed-in GitHub selection and a checked Android/media config save. Targeted production-route/history/write-failure tests also passed together before the final full run. Java 17 core, debug app and AndroidTest builds passed, as did `git diff --check`. No external Git pushes or store publication occurred.
- The initial route test attempted to scroll a fixed footer and then looked for a repository before the catalog was ready. It now waits for the actual fixture repository, uses canonical path identity and scrolls the lazy list. No waits were added to weaken Git contents, credential or recovery assertions. The editor light-theme capture was visually inspected at `/tmp/syncmd-repository-evidence/connection-editor-light.png`; the replacement token is masked and controls stay above navigation space. Full motion, large-font/dark-editor/accessibility acceptance remains open.

### Encrypted GitHub sessions, OAuth verification and account-owned data (2026-10-27)

- Added `GitHubSessionStore` using Android Keystore AES-256-GCM for the active OAuth/PAT token and pending OAuth state. Encrypted writes are verified by decrypting the resulting record before success is published. Failed writes restore the previous ciphertext, including preference-commit failures that already changed memory. The new session preferences are excluded from cloud backup and device transfer.
- Legacy `github_token` preferences migrate only after encrypted persistence is verified. Secure records take precedence over stale legacy values. Signed-out tombstones prevent old restored preferences from resurrecting a signed-out account. Failed migration retains the legacy value and reports a recoverable storage error instead of using an unverified session. Old profile/repo caches without proven ownership are re-fetched; local repository records and settings are preserved.
- Matched the current iOS OAuth state-validation contract and the sibling OAuth server's state-echo response. Android now generates a persisted random state before opening the browser, accepts it once within ten minutes, and validates scheme/authority/path/fragment and single decoded state/token parameters. Wrong, missing, duplicate (including encoded duplicate names), future, expired, superseded and replayed callbacks are rejected without replacing the existing session. PAT sign-in and sign-out cancel older pending browser callbacks. The token-bearing URI is removed from MainActivity's launch intent after handling.
- Explicit browser sign-in can replace an unreadable encrypted record, and errors are connected to the login screen or a native sign-in error dialog. Successful encrypted sessions also report failed cleanup of old ordinary preferences rather than silently claiming cleanup succeeded. No live browser authorization or real GitHub account was used in these tests; callback protocol behavior is verified with isolated fixture sessions and the checked-in server/iOS sources.
- Bound ordinary profile/repository caches to a token fingerprint, and require an expected token for async cache writes. A replaced token clears the previous identity. Added an injectable `GitHubClient` and request/session guards in GitHubViewModel so delayed old-account responses cannot repopulate current data, a sign-out cannot be undone by late PAT validation, and a new PAT session can finish while an old account request remains blocked. GitHub API responses close connections, and bearer-authenticated API calls do not automatically follow redirects.
- Targeted Android result: **13 tests passed** on API 35 `emulator-5556`. Nine session tests cover verified plaintext migration, failed migration/retry, secure-record/tombstone precedence, encrypted cold-start one-use callbacks, malformed/duplicate callbacks, expiration/future/supersession/sign-out, failed replacement/sign-out, cache ownership and unreadable-session recovery. Four ViewModel tests use controlled API responses to exercise late account data, sign-out during validation, identity clearing before new email loading, and new-session completion without waiting for an old request. All preferences and tokens are fake, isolated from the actual account/session files and removed after testing.
- Core result remains **193 passing tests**, no failures/errors/skips. Java 17 debug and AndroidTest builds and `git diff --check` pass. The final full native run passed **50 tests** on API 35 `emulator-5556`, including all earlier connection, file, Git workflow and lifecycle regressions. Multi-account storage/UI and stable per-repository account references are still incomplete; the session store currently represents one active account. Custom per-repository author name/email fields, present in iOS SettingsView, are explicitly retained in the remaining scope.

- Final recovery cases also verify that starting browser sign-in after a failed migration preserves a usable legacy token in verified encrypted storage, and that an unreadable encrypted record plus a legacy token retains that legacy value until a new login succeeds. No pending null-token callback session is allowed to silently discard unverified legacy credentials.
- One verification attempt started before APK installation had finished and was interrupted by package replacement. It produced no passing test result and is not counted. Both installs were then confirmed successful before the final complete **50-test pass** (154.722 seconds). No live OAuth authorization, external Git pushes or store publication occurred.

### Multiple GitHub accounts and repository author identity (2026-10-27)

- Extended encrypted GitHub sessions to schema v2 while retaining the existing Keystore key/AAD and decrypting the actual v1 format. Account tokens and profiles remain encrypted and backup-excluded. An immutable original-account default preserves legacy repository ownership; token-hash references migrate to verified numeric GitHub IDs through aliases. Reauthentication updates that account instead of creating a duplicate, including a changed login name. Previously owned profile caches migrate only when their token fingerprint matches; provisional accounts refresh their API identity even when repository lists are cached.
- Added Settings / repository picker / connection editor → GitHub accounts. Users can add an account without removing the existing sign-in, choose a browsing account, and confirm removal of a sign-in. Removing an account retains its local author profile, invalidates pending callbacks and leaves assigned repositories disconnected. It never chooses another connected account automatically. Active browsing data retains the existing async session guards. Per-repository account chips show the saved assignment, including original-account defaults for older repositories; disconnected assignments require an explicit choice before saving.
- GitHub picker clones capture the account and token before starting. Both picker and URL clones save an encrypted account reference under the destination lease immediately before staged clone publication; a failed publication rolls the reference back. URL cloning now offers a saved GitHub account alongside public access and username/token. Fetch/pull/push resolve the repository reference instead of the browsing account. Explicit public access continues to suppress all app credentials.
- Recovery from an unreadable encrypted session keeps original ownership unassigned. A new browser login or API-validated PAT can recover access, but older unbound repositories require account selection. A new login cannot silently become their default. Failed account selection/removal writes preserve both the previous encrypted record and published session.
- Added Local repositories → Edit connection → Edit commit author. Name/email are validated and written with a reviewed config revision, metadata identity checks, Git config lock and fsync. Working files, index and refs are preserved. Explicit repository config wins; otherwise the repository's account profile supplies the author independently of browsing. JGit merge/completion, revert, stash and annotated-tag operations use the same resolved identity in memory without rewriting user config. Local commits receive the resolved author/committer explicitly.
- Core result: **197 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips. New tests verify author config preservation and stale/locked/invalid edits, and actual merge, merge-completion, revert, stash and annotated-tag signatures with unchanged config. Java 17 core/debug/AndroidTest builds passed, and `git diff --check` passed.
- Native result before final recovery additions: **64 tests passed** on API 35 `emulator-5556` (120.752 seconds), covering all earlier suites plus account migration, renewal, switch/removal/restart, clone publication rollback, account-owned author resolution, add-account PAT flow, explicit repository reassignment, URL clone account selection, disconnected legacy assignments and author editing. The final recovery/migration build then passed **all 65 native tests** (128.011 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-accounts-migration-final-regression.log`), including OAuth/PAT unreadable-session recovery and token-owned legacy profile migration. All fixture account preferences and repositories were cleaned up by the tests.
- Fixed a real lazy-list semantics duplication after account removal by assigning stable keys to all rows. A new connection test initially omitted repository registration and then compared an Android path alias literally; it now registers the fixture and checks canonical identity. Large-text checks use the lazy list's scroll-to-node action and still require the target control to be displayed. The new identity cache assertion requires the newly API-validated account's profile rather than an empty profile. Git/credential assertions were retained.
- Light and dark/160%-font account and author captures were generated and reviewed. Long account handles/display names wrap, and primary controls remain reachable. A five-test UI capture passed separately (`/tmp/syncmd-accounts-motion-check.log`); raw recordings are untracked under `.argent/recordings/`. Recordings include test-activity creation/teardown and are diagnostic interaction evidence, not full production-navigation or 60-fps release acceptance. Complete motion, landscape and accessibility audits remain open.
- Browser sign-in requests ephemeral cookies using the [documented Custom Tabs protocol](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/browser/customtabs/CustomTabsIntent#EXTRA_ENABLE_EPHEMERAL_BROWSING). AndroidX Browser 1.9 required AGP 8.9.1, so the app retains Browser 1.8 and sets the documented intent extra directly; supported browsers honor it and unsupported browsers retain ordinary cookie behavior. PAT account addition is fixture-tested. No live browser authorization, real account mutation, external Git push, store publication or production deployment occurred.

- Final UI recording: `.argent/recordings/screen-recording-emulator-5556-1793109111567.mp4`; a compact 20-second diagnostic copy is `/tmp/syncmd-accounts-author-ui-verified.mp4`. Captured frames were reviewed; test-window transitions and existing main-activity frames are included. The recording predates the final recovery-only changes, which are covered by the final 65-test run.

### Device-folder storage, clone location review and repository import (2026-10-27)

- Added Settings → Choose clone storage. The saved default applies to new clones; existing repositories keep their path. Both GitHub and URL clones allow a per-clone folder or explicit app-storage override. GitHub cloning now shows a location review and starts only after confirmation. Storage resolution and Git work run off the main thread. Failed/revoked selected locations report an error instead of silently cloning elsewhere. Retry retains the chosen location and allows it to be changed.
- Added a native shared-storage permission explanation/settings handoff, followed by Android's folder picker. The current JGit filesystem requires ordinary writable paths, so shared device storage uses `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` on Android 11+, plus a user-picked external-storage-provider tree with persisted read/write access. Android 9/10 use runtime storage permission and the Android 10 legacy-storage flag; these older-device branches are not yet device-qualified. URI document IDs are resolved through mounted OS storage volumes, checked for traversal/symbolic links/Git metadata and verified by owned create/fsync/rename/read/delete probes. Cloud document providers are explicitly rejected; no copied shadow working tree is substituted for an in-place repository.
- Saved folder records/defaults are excluded from cloud/device-transfer backups. The app retains grants across restarts and validates URI ownership, volume/path identity and shared-storage access before resolving a custom destination. A platform access checker is installed in `RepositoryOperationCoordinator`, covering existing working copies and files as well as new clones. App-owned storage remains available without shared-storage permission. Removing a URI grant prevents further serialized work even while Android's broad storage permission remains enabled.
- Added Local repositories → Scan a device folder. The user-selected tree is scanned with the existing depth/directory bounds, and working copies are added explicitly in a batch. Source working files, staged index, local refs and author config remain in place. Imported/custom clones are protected from the app-managed delete action. Safe relocation and explicit custom-clone deletion qualification are still pending; this batch does not claim complete storage parity.
- Interrupted tracking-removal recovery now retains its journal and catalog entry while custom-folder access is unavailable. Regranting the folder allows recovery to finish. Missing access is no longer treated as evidence that Git metadata disappeared. Tracking backups select an app-owned directory on the source filesystem using Android native device IDs, preserving verified metadata retention for shared-storage working copies. App-owned root enumeration includes mounted secondary volumes; removable-volume sharing still requires physical qualification.
- Java 17 core/debug/AndroidTest builds passed; **197 core tests** pass with zero failures/errors/skips. A targeted **nine-test native run passed** (52.915 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-storage-nine-device.log`) using real Android folder-picker grants, saved defaults/overrides, URI revocation, provider/traversal guards, an external working copy with edit/stage/commit, batch import/restart, deferred recovery, GitHub clone location review and light/dark 160%-font layouts. The full final regression then passed **all 75 native tests** (164.968 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-storage-final-full-regression.log`), including an additional same-volume backup/share test. The separate fresh-process denied-permission check also passed (0.143 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-storage-denied-verified.log`): no shared-folder action runs without access, while app storage and app cache remain usable. This totals **76 distinct passing native tests** across the granted and denied permission runs.
- Android terminates a running app when its `MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` app-op changes. The initial in-process permission setup therefore interrupted instrumentation and is not counted as a pass. Tests now receive that permission before process launch; a separate denied-permission run follows restoration. Android also correctly rejected synthetic self-grants under shell identity, so every storage test now uses the real picker/consent flow. Corrected the picker initial URI to a document URI derived from the saved tree URI; native confirmation then passed (6.475 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-storage-picker-focused.log`). No permission assertions or Git contents were relaxed.
- The manifest's all-files permission supports the app's document/Git management path and is requested only after a storage action. [Android documents its direct-filesystem access](https://developer.android.com/training/data-storage/manage-all-files), and [Google Play requires a qualifying use and permissions declaration/approval](https://support.google.com/googleplay/android-developer/answer/10467955) before publishing an app that declares it. No store submission, permission declaration or production deployment was performed. Full motion, production permission-return navigation, landscape, removable-storage and older-Android qualification remain open.

- The first full storage regression exposed six file-editor/browser failures because the new platform policy omitted app-owned cache paths. Added internal/external cache and no-backup roots to the app-owned scope; all seven file UI tests then passed (`/tmp/syncmd-storage-cache-files-verified.log`), followed by the complete 75-test pass. Shared folders still require a selected persisted grant, and an explicit unselected-folder regression proves broad permission alone does not authorize repository work there.
- Visual review caught clone-location labels inheriting black text on the custom dark background. They now use semantic on-surface colors. The focused final UI pass rechecks storage/default selection and the GitHub clone review after that color-only correction. This change does not alter filesystem, account or navigation behavior.

The selected clone path is now visible on the dark custom background, and the review header describes cloning as a confirmation step.

Captured frames were reviewed at `/tmp/syncmd-storage-ui-motion-sheet.png`; the native picker, grant confirmation, default selection, clone review and clone completion appear in the recording, along with test-activity transitions and idle frames. ### Whole-vault relocation, encrypted metadata transfer and recovery (2026-10-27)

- Added Local repositories → Move repository with destination review and explicit confirmation. The entire working tree, staged index, Git metadata, refs, untracked files and author config move together. Sorted multi-path leases serialize both locations and retained copies; cancellation releases partially acquired leases. Source changes, active Git operations, shared metadata, nested repositories, occupied destinations and stale credential/catalog reviews prevent publication.
- Same-filesystem moves use Android native rename and retain directory identity. Cross-filesystem moves copy to a uniquely owned staging folder, verify full content snapshots, retain the original in an app-owned same-volume recovery folder, and publish the prepared working copy. Device tests exercise the real private-data/shared-storage boundary and a user-granted Documents working copy on shared storage. Revoking the persisted URI grant after review stops mutation before any journal or folder move.
- Added durable, fsynced move journals for prepared, copied, retained-source, published, committed and recovery-deletion phases. Startup resumes filesystem/catalog work, recognizes publication interrupted before a journal update, and retains recovery state on errors. Published destination edits survive recovery. A same-filesystem move removes its journal after application completion; cross-filesystem records remain visible until the retained original is restored or explicitly removed.
- Repository credentials are decrypted and re-encrypted for destination-path authenticated data in one verified preference commit, with an idempotent receipt. Basic credentials, explicit anonymous policies and stable GitHub account references preserve their ownership; browsing-account switches never select a replacement. Failed credential commits restore the exact prior ciphertexts. A catalog failure after credential publication rolls forward on restart without changing account assignment. Journal payloads contain catalog intent and credential-record revisions, never tokens/passwords.
- Added Move recoveries in repository management: finish interrupted work, cancel unpublished work, restore a retained original as a separate repository, and confirm removal of an unchanged retained copy. A restoration consumes the previous recovery record only after the replacement catalog/credential update commits. Current working-copy edits and account assignment remain intact. Cleanup refuses changed recovery contents or replaced destination identities.
- Core verification: **209 tests pass**, zero failures/errors, including 12 relocation/concurrency cases and all earlier Git/files/storage regressions. The relocation suite injects process stops at every durable phase, after physical rename, before catalog completion and during authorized cleanup. The shared recovery-root restore test exposed an overly broad overlap check; comparison now uses actual retained-copy paths, allowing sibling copies while rejecting moves inside a working tree.
- Targeted native verification: **13 tests passed** on API 35 emulator-5556 (37.097 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-moves-supported-native.log`). Coverage includes real cross-volume contents/index preservation, local author and pinned account ownership, startup before catalog completion, failed catalog/credential persistence, stale credentials, separate recovery restoration, cleanup, existing-destination protection, actual folder-picker grants/revocation, same-volume inode preservation, Unicode paths, dangling links, and light/dark 160%-font confirmation/cancellation UI. Two initial asynchronous assertions observed catalog publication before recovery finalization; checks now await the final recovery state without changing data/account expectations.
- Filesystem limitation remains explicit: Android shared-storage FUSE rejected Linux RENAME_NOREPLACE with EINVAL in real native tests. The unsupported JNI helper/build integration was removed; final APKs contain no native libraries. Supported Os.rename now checks destination absence again immediately before the syscall, including dangling links. Nonempty destination directories cannot be replaced by rename; an unrelated empty directory created in the final check/syscall window can still be replaced. This is not an atomic no-replace guarantee. Confirmation asks users to keep other apps from changing the folders during a move. A stronger portable publication protocol, arbitrary external-writer races, power-loss durability, physical/removable storage and older-Android qualification remain open.

### Clone-history remote, branch and authentication restoration (2026-10-27)

- Local repositories → Previously added → Clone again now opens a dedicated production history route using only the original local path as its navigation argument. The review form restores the saved HTTPS remote, display name and branch for GitHub or self-hosted hosts. A former GitHub display alias never replaces a recorded self-hosted address. No GitHub login is required for a public/self-hosted history clone.
- Restores repository-bound GitHub account references rather than the current browsing account, including canonical account aliases and the frozen original account for older unassigned GitHub working copies. A disconnected or missing assigned account leaves Clone disabled until the user explicitly selects a connected account or another method. Explicit public policies remain public even with connected GitHub accounts.
- Saved username/token connections can be reused through a selected Use saved connection option. Secrets are decrypted only for the transport and re-encrypted under the new working-copy path; they are never prefilled into fields or placed in route arguments. The remote must match the reviewed endpoint and the encrypted-record revision must match before use and before publication. A changed endpoint/credential rejects automatic reuse, and credential changes during transport prevent publication while retaining the original history/connection.
- History with no recorded URL can use an authoritative saved connection URL. An alias-only GitHub suggestion requires explicit URL/authentication review and receives no automatic credential selection. Unreadable/incompatible saved connections show a recoverable review message; the nonsecret catalog fields remain editable so an explicit replacement is possible. Non-HTTPS URL support remains pending with SSH/transport parity.
- Successful registration validates the completed working copy, preserves the previous display alias, and adds the new catalog row while removing the old history row in one checked preference commit. Failed clones keep the history and original encrypted connection for retry. Canonical comparisons accept old /data/user/0 history paths as the same location as /data/data; the native fixtures now exercise that older spelling.

The production route test verifies the exact self-hosted URL/branch and explicit authentication gate. These use fake identities and real on-device bare Git remotes; no external Git server is contacted by the fixture transport.

### Reviewed pull with rebase and persistent conflict recovery (2026-10-27)

- Added Git control → Pull with rebase / Resume rebase → a dedicated production route bound to the selected working-copy path. Review fetches the actual configured upstream and shows local/upstream commit counts and the exact target's abbreviated ID. Starting requires confirmation that local IDs can change. Execution refetches and revalidates branch, local commit, metadata identity, config, credential-policy revision, tracking ref and live advertised upstream before rewriting. A deleted upstream cannot use an old cached tracking ref. Detached/unborn, dirty, active-operation and shared/linked-metadata inputs are rejected before beginning.
- Uses JGit's merge-preserving rebase, retaining local merge topology. Original commit authors remain intact; committer identity comes from the configured repository author/account resolver in memory. Automatic stashing is disabled for the command without changing saved config; local changes must be committed/stashed explicitly. Fetch uses the existing scoped credential/redirect/TLS policy and a transport timeout. Rebase performs no push.
- Git's own rebase metadata supplies restart-persistent sessions. The new screen resumes stopped sessions, identifies upstream as Git's ours side and the replayed local commit as theirs, supports manual text resolution or exact whole-side blobs, and explicitly identifies side deletions. Resolving a missing side stages deletion instead of trying to check out a nonexistent blob. Binary/large conflicts cannot enter the text editor. Continuation requires staged resolutions, and an empty replay requires a separately confirmed Skip current commit action rather than silently discarding work.
- Session revisions cover HEAD, config, index, original branch ref, rebase metadata and changed working files. Stale resolve/continue/abort operations preserve later edits and ask for refresh. Even a refreshed session refuses to overwrite an original branch moved by an external tool. Abort requires confirmation describing discarded tracked edits/resolutions, restores the original branch/commit, and needs no remote access. Unsaved resolution drafts require an explicit discard choice; duplicate mutations are gated before coroutine launch and app operations share canonical repository leases.
- Core result: **221 tests passed**, zero failures/errors. Twelve new rebase integration tests cover clean replay/no push, conflict resolution/restart, offline abort, dirty/changed branch and changed upstream rejection, stale edits, preserved merge topology, externally moved branch refusal, empty-commit skip, delete/modify resolution, credential-policy changes, deleted advertised branches and command-local auto-stash disabling/config preservation. The existing real smart-HTTPS fixture now also creates divergence, authenticates rebase fetches on a non-default branch, verifies both resulting files and proves the server ref was not pushed. No live GitHub account or external Git server is involved.
- Initial native rebase checks passed, then the broader navigation run exposed two issues in its verification: Back returned to the Git list's preserved lower scroll position, so the route test now checks the reachable rebase control instead of waiting for an offscreen header; an existing diff refresh emitted UI state from repository I/O and triggered a Compose test-clock wrong-thread dialog update. Diff/index refresh remains inside the same lease, with observable UI publication and commit/push messages now delivered on the ViewModel main context. The corrected targeted run passed **17 tests** (36.804 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-rebase-native-final.log`), including all commit/push ViewModel and production navigation regressions at that checkpoint. Final current-build full native and cold-process evidence follows.
- Java 17 core/debug/AndroidTest and release builds plus release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-rebase-candidate-build.log`, 33 seconds). The runtime remains pure Java/JGit with no device shell Git dependency or new native library. Rebase currently shares the app's supported HTTPS/real-working-copy transport/storage scope; SSH, LFS and the previously recorded storage publication/qualification items remain open. The full parity goal is still active.

A later conflict-reader review found that invalid UTF-8 without NUL bytes could pass binary detection and enter a replacement-character editor. The reader now strictly decodes every index side; malformed text requires whole-side selection. Core and native regressions verify that manual editing is disabled and the chosen blob's exact bytes survive resolution and rebase.

### SSH transport, encrypted keys and server trust (2026-10-27)

- Added maintained JSch transport and Bouncy Castle support, configured per repository transport. Keys are parsed in memory; the app does not load system SSH configuration, default private keys or an agent. The factory checks the exact configured address before opening credentials and accepts only standard upload-pack/receive-pack commands for that repository. Explicit usernames, custom ports, SCP/SSH paths, IPv6 normalization and Git home-relative paths are handled without losing path scope.
- Added private-key, optional passphrase and optional matching public-key fields to URL cloning and connection editing. Secret drafts remain in memory, are masked by default, and clear after success or changing authentication methods. RSA keys below 2048 bits and mismatched public keys are rejected. The existing Android Keystore/AES-GCM repository credential store encrypts SSH keys and binds records to the working-copy path and exact remote address. Vault credential relocation and failed-config rollback retain SSH policies.
- Unknown or changed server keys require an explicit foreground fingerprint review before public-key authentication. Verified host/port pins persist on this device; changed keys show the previous fingerprint. Background unknown keys fail closed, leaving the foreground cancels pending reviews, and concurrent connections share one challenge. Settings now lists trusted fingerprints and provides confirmed per-server forgetting or reset/repair. Failed trust-removal persistence restores the previous preferences before reporting failure. Failure-injection coverage for this last persistence guard remains open.
- SSH Clone again restores the original saved key and branch through the review form without exposing secrets or borrowing a signed-in GitHub browsing account, including SSH remotes hosted on github.com. HTTPS/account connection and history regressions continue to pass.
- Core result: **232 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips, including eight new SSH authentication/transport tests. A real loopback SSH server with generated host/client identities verifies encrypted Ed25519 clone, fetch, fast-forward pull, push and unapproved-host rejection. Tests also cover RSA, wrong passphrases, weak keys, public-key mismatch, path/host/user/port credential isolation, command injection rejection and standard SHA256 fingerprints. The server permits only Git pack commands against its temporary fixture repository. No system host keys, personal private keys or external Git repositories are used.
- Final Java 17 core/debug/AndroidTest/release builds and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-ssh-final-build.log`, 48 seconds). Explicit retention rules preserve JSch algorithm implementations and Bouncy Castle provider classes selected reflectively; the release mapping retains Ed25519, AES-CTR and bcrypt implementations. Unused desktop LDAP certificate-store references are excluded from missing-API warnings. Release build success and mapping inspection do not constitute release-device runtime qualification.
- Final native connection/history/trust regression result: **31 tests passed** on the dedicated API 35 GitSync emulator `emulator-5556` (`/tmp/syncmd-ssh-final-regression.log`, 84.192 seconds). Covers masked SSH clone/edit forms, encrypted key reload/path and endpoint isolation, unchanged index/worktree during connection editing, config-failure rollback, vault credential relocation, saved SSH history cloning, trust approval/persistence/change/rejection/background cancellation/concurrent review/port isolation and confirmed forgetting, alongside existing HTTPS and GitHub-account history behavior.

### Git LFS filters, transfers and tracking review (2026-10-27)

- Added bounded v1 pointer parsing, streaming SHA256/size verification and content-addressed storage under `.git/lfs/objects`. Objects are published from fsynced temporary files only after verification. Existing cache entries are reverified before use; corrupt cache entries can be replaced by independently verified downloads or exact hydrated worktree content. Cache paths reject pre-existing symbolic links. Empty LFS files stay empty Git blobs. Unknown/malformed pointer extensions fail staging instead of silently wrapping or dropping them.
- Integrated in-process JGit clean/smudge filters with the production repository-opening seam. Staging writes pointers to the index while keeping hydrated bytes in the working tree; status and diffs compare canonical contents. Checkout expands verified cached objects. Regular Git clients can continue using their own git-lfs filters; the standard `useJGitBuiltin` flag selects the Android implementation. No device shell Git executable or new native library is required.
- Added HTTPS batch, download, upload and optional post-upload verification. Requests stream object bytes, retain platform certificate checks, reject redirects and bound JSON size/depth/header counts. Batch replies must acknowledge exactly the requested OIDs and sizes with a supported transfer/hash. Server errors and malformed signed URLs are not echoed into user-facing errors. Repository credentials stay scoped to the original derived endpoint; foreign action URLs receive only explicit action headers, and a repository-controlled `.lfsconfig` cannot redirect Git credentials to a different endpoint. Dedicated credential/consent UX for separate private LFS endpoints remains open.
- Added host-verified SSH `git-lfs-authenticate` for download/upload with encrypted key/passphrase support and custom ports. Each session is restricted to its exact repository and one typed LFS operation; ordinary Git SSH sessions retain their pack-command-only restriction. Authorization responses and stderr are bounded, expired access fails closed, and no arbitrary shell-command entry point is exposed. Unknown/changed SSH host keys use the existing foreground trust workflow.
- Clone and fast-forward pull hydrate unchanged index-backed pointers. Downloads are copied beside the destination, hashed again, and replace only the exact observed pointer after checking branch/HEAD/index identity and path safety. Failed downloads keep pointers, and external drafts are retained while verified caches remain reusable. A completed Git pull with failed hydration now reports a separate LFS attention message and provides a Git LFS retry route; it does not claim the fast-forward was rolled back.
- Branch/tag publication uploads reachable LFS objects and completes required server verification before the Git push. Failed verification keeps the local commit and leaves remote refs unchanged. Explicit ref pushes pin the captured source OIDs while LFS transfers run. Remaining branch-switch/rebase/conflict hydration/merge semantics and additional historical-object qualification are not considered complete by these clone/pull tests.
- Added reviewed tracking decisions for known binary extensions and large binary files. Before staging/committing, undecided files require an explicit LFS or regular-Git choice. Review checks the exact file identities, HEAD, index and `.gitattributes` revision; applying a choice changes only exact-path attribute rules. It preserves staged content and working files until explicit staging. Explicit opt-outs are remembered in `.gitattributes`. A staged regular-Git blob above 100 MiB cannot be committed; its index and working file are preserved. Git → Git LFS now exposes tracking review, object state, verified-cache state, download/retry and local-edit preservation.
- JGit 5.13.5 and the official stable-6.10 parser both split quoted attribute paths incorrectly. Added a small independently authored, public-API-compatible `AttributesNode` parser with C-style quoted paths; a cached Gradle transform omits only the upstream class and obsolete jar signatures, retaining the rest of JGit and its license resources. An upstream parser-class SHA256 guard forces review before a changed JGit parser is substituted. Tests compare spaces, Unicode, quotes, tabs, macros and overrides with native Git; exact tracking of literal wildcard characters preserves sibling-file decisions. This adapter needs review/removal during future JGit upgrades.
- Core result: **260 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-compatibility-tests.log`, 22 seconds), including real smart-HTTPS Git plus LFS clone/hydration, pointer staging, upload+verify before ref publication, pull hydration and completed-pull attention/retry. Additional tests cover corrupt downloads/cache repair, edits during download, omitted/duplicate batch replies, redirect refusal, credential/header/path isolation, bounded/duplicate JSON, expiry, explicit tracking consent/opt-out, stale reviews, unusual filenames, empty files and the real staged >100 MiB commit guard. The real SSH fixture also authorizes both LFS operations with encrypted Ed25519 keys and the exact server pin.
- Debug, AndroidTest, release APK and release vital lint builds pass (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-final-build.log`, 74 seconds). Final release mapping retains all **113** required named JSch algorithm/authentication implementations and includes the compatibility attributes parser. Subsequent changes add tests only; production debug/release code is unchanged. Release-device runtime and complete performance/motion qualification remain open.
- Real API 35 Android transport proof: **1 test passed** (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-device-final-tests.log`, 10.152 seconds) with SSH Git cloning into app-owned Android/media, SSH LFS download/upload authorization, verified upload-before-push, a second clone over smart HTTPS and clean canonical status. The temporary servers recorded one upload, one successful verify and two downloads, with zero Git credentials sent to object-storage actions. Independently checked the remote pointer and all 16,387 uploaded payload bytes; SSH command records contain exactly `download`, `upload`. Both generated loopback servers and exact adb reverse rules were stopped/removed. This run predates only error-redaction/prose and build-guard refinements; a final-build replay is recorded below when complete.

### Custom LFS endpoint approvals and separate credentials (2026-10-27)

- Added device-local custom endpoint reviews to the production root trust gate, with exact repository path, Git remote, HTTPS LFS address and transfer direction. Unknown background endpoints fail closed; approved background operations can reuse the saved grant. A foreground decision resumes the waiting operation, concurrent matching requests share a review, and cancellation/backgrounding releases waiters without granting access. SSH fingerprint review takes priority without discarding queued LFS reviews.
- Added an Android Keystore/AES-GCM store that encrypts the full approval/credential record, uses exact-scope authenticated data, and excludes its preferences from backup/device transfer. Download and upload each have an independent public/private policy. Separate endpoint credentials never inherit the Git sign-in. Users can explicitly reuse previously approved same-endpoint credentials when approving the other direction. Error messages and public record models do not expose saved secrets.
- Settings → LFS endpoint approvals now lists scopes and public/private directions, with confirmed per-direction revocation and reset/repair. Revoking one direction preserves the other's approval and credential. Revision checks reject stale approval/revocation decisions; failed writes restore prior preferences, including when a failing commit already changed the preferences in memory/disk. Corrupt/swapped encrypted records cannot grant access and can be reset explicitly. Cancellation appears as an LFS notice instead of opening a second error modal behind the global review.
- The core authorization boundary reads custom credentials only after approval and validates their HTTP headers. Core tests pass **264 cases**, zero failures/errors/skips. Initial focused Android verification passes **16 tests** (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-endpoints-native-first.log`, 29.404 seconds): eight initial store/consent checks plus the four existing LFS screen and four SSH trust cases. Two additional store cases and production Settings/back navigation are included in the following full regression.
- A real private-endpoint Android flow passes (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-private-endpoint-flow-first.log`, 13.628 seconds). It clones through authenticated HTTPS Git, cancels an LFS download review with exact pointer/index preservation, then approves a private download through the production dialog and restores the object. A subsequent real Git push separately reviews private uploads, uploads/verifies the new object before publishing the Git ref, and uses different reader/writer credentials. Confirmed download revocation preserves upload access across store reload. Fake credentials remain encrypted and masked. It uses only UUID-scoped test preferences/app-owned files and restores TLS/global endpoint callbacks afterward.
- Independently read the bare remote pointer and verified all **16,388 bytes**, SHA256 `d1183c623589db8d8b28d8e5b2de65183037d9df578297d224ec8bc9373ef65b`, one custom download batch, one custom upload batch, one object upload/verify and **zero escaped Git credentials**. Proof: `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-private-endpoint-proof.json`. The exact fixture launcher exited, both listeners closed and its reverse rule was removed.
- Debug/AndroidTest/release builds and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-endpoints-final-build.log`, 31 seconds). Release mapping retains all **113** required named SSH implementations and the quoted-path attributes parser; proof `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-endpoints-release-mapping-proof.json`. No release APK was installed or published. Light approval and dark/160%-font approval/management captures were retrieved and visually inspected; no full release performance/accessibility/landscape or physical-device acceptance is claimed.
- Saved the disposable SSH/HTTPS LFS server in `tools/testing/lfs_device_fixture.py` with replay/cleanup instructions. It uses fake fixture credentials, a temporary bare Git remote, strict custom-endpoint reader/writer checks, action-specific object credentials, counters and independent remote-object proof. It does not contact external repositories or use personal keys/tokens.
- Approvals are deliberately bound to the canonical vault path and do not silently follow relocation; explicit reapproval after a move, move/reconnect UX and broader credential-transport/device failure qualification remain in scope. LFS locking, branch/rebase/conflict hydration, advanced tracking and every other pending parity matrix category remain unfinished.

### LFS lock verification before publishing (2026-10-27)

- Audited iOS's actual call sites: commit-and-push and existing-commit branch push verify lock ownership; create/list/unlock are service APIs without a dedicated iOS management screen. Added equivalent Android lock APIs and connected automatic checks to production push paths. Dedicated manual Android lock management remains an explicit pending capability; the APIs alone do not count as that UI.
- Added standard create/list/verify/unlock requests, optional ref/path/id/cursor/limit filters, opaque-ID URL encoding, and explicit force-unlock semantics. Lock requests use the existing scoped HTTPS/SSH authentication and custom-endpoint approval/credential boundary. Only explicit HTTP 404/501 mean unsupported locking; authentication failures, redirects, server errors and malformed responses stop the operation. Server error bodies are not displayed. JSON/UTF-8/text/list limits, duplicate ownership/paths/IDs, repeated cursors, excess pages and changing support during pagination fail closed. Normal foreign-owner unlock remains rejected; no automatic force-unlock is performed by app pushes.
- Push preflight reads the actual receive-pack advertisement with the same remote/credentials, supporting write-only Git endpoints without requiring upload-pack/fetch permission. It pins source OIDs and attaches destination ref leases, including zero-OID expectations for new refs. A destination appearing or changing during lock review is rejected even for a force push. Cached tracking refs cannot determine which changes are checked; an existing advertised destination whose objects are missing locally requires fetching first.
- Changed-path review uses committed tree attributes on both old/new sides, covers rename/deletion/force rewind and intermediate unpushed edits later reverted, and follows incoming commit history. Current worktree, info and global attribute overrides cannot bypass the committed-rule check. Already-published history is excluded for new branches/tags, so merely creating a ref from existing content or publishing a notes-only commit does not claim unchanged locked assets. Existing branch changes still check the net tree difference even if their source commit already exists on another remote ref. Unicode/C-quoted/tab paths and explicit committed opt-outs are tested.
- All user-repository push entry points now share the same checks and verified acknowledgement path. The legacy current-branch push also uses an explicit pinned branch refspec instead of allowing an implicit push default to publish unchecked refs. Foreign locks stop before object upload or Git publication and retain the local commit/index/working files. Git displays a specific modal naming locked paths and explaining retry. Commit-and-push failures now replace the stale “Pushing…” notice with “Committed locally. Push stopped; retry when ready.” Lock-modal state is cleared on a new action, repository change, dismissal or a later read error.
- **278 core tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips, adding 14 committed-tree/API/HTTPS/race regressions. Coverage includes a foreign lock on a later page, owned locks, exact retained-commit retry, unsupported vs unauthorized/error servers, no redirect escape, missing/duplicate ownership and cursor cycles, create/list/unlock semantics, write-only Git transport, destination creation during verification, and new-ref exclusion of unchanged published locks. The new tag test initially omitted its required explicit remote argument; it was corrected before the passing run.
- Core/debug/AndroidTest/release builds and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-published-range-final-build.log`, 62 seconds); the final status-message/UI build also passes (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-status-final-build.log`, 35 seconds). Release mapping retains all **113** required named SSH implementations, the compatibility attributes parser and the lock exception used by the UI (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-release-proof.json`). No release APK was installed or published.
- Initial production Android navigation passes (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-production-light.log`, **7.46 seconds**), using real DI, encrypted per-repository fake Git credentials and HTTPS Git/LFS services. The server returns a foreign lock on page two. The test checks the visible dialog, zero uploads while blocked, unchanged remote HEAD and exact retained local HEAD/index/working bytes, then rejects a normal foreign-owner unlock, explicitly removes only the fake fixture lock and retries through Push Current Branch. The same commit is published with one upload/verify. Independent remote proof is `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-production-light-proof.json` (all **16,389 bytes** and SHA256 verified).
- Dark/160%-text production replay passes on the final production APK (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-production-dark-xl-final.log`, **7.371 seconds**), with the same Git/lock/credential/byte assertions and independent remote proof (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-production-dark-xl-proof.json`). The first large-text run incorrectly waited for a below-fold vault action; the test now scrolls the actual lazy list, without weakening state assertions. Screenshot capture now waits for stable pixels after native dialog-window animation rather than treating Compose idleness as platform-window idleness. The settled dark/large-text capture `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-lock-blocked-dark-xl.png` is visually inspected. Original system font scale **1.0** is restored; storage remains **default**. Full motion/performance/landscape/accessibility and physical-device acceptance remain open.
- Protocol source: [Git LFS locking API](https://github.com/git-lfs/git-lfs/blob/main/docs/api/locking.md). Existing unresolved intermittent emulator upload EOF qualification, remaining LFS workflows and every other pending parity category remain in scope.
- Final production light-theme replay passes (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-production-light-ready.log`, **6.508 seconds**), with a settled opaque dialog capture `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-lock-blocked-light-final.png` and independently verified **16,389-byte** remote object (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-production-light-final-proof.json`). A preceding recorded replay clicked the push control while it was disabled during initial branch/diff loading; tracing confirms no push or lock request occurred. The test now waits for the real enabled state before its single click, rather than retrying taps or weakening any remote/commit/index/byte assertion. The failed recording remains diagnostic only.
- Full final-build native regression passes **134 tests** (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-locks-full-native.log`, **288.091 seconds**), including the new stopped-push status assertion and all qualified rebase/account/connection/history/storage/move/file/navigation/SSH trust/LFS/endpoint-store suites.
