### LFS hydration after checkout, merge, rebase and conflict resolution (2026-10-27)

- Branch checkout and successful/conflicted merges now run verified LFS hydration after Git updates the working tree. Rebase begin/continue/skip/abort also hydrate stage-zero pointer files, including nonconflicting paths while a rebase is paused. Returned rebase sessions are inspected after hydration so their revisions describe the actual files. Downloads use the selected branch's upstream remote (including a named remote other than origin), the repository-bound connection and the existing endpoint/SSH trust gates. Cached content remains available offline without resolving credentials or requiring a configured remote.
- Download failure preserves the completed Git result. Branch switching and merge completion/abort report a typed completed-action notice; merge/rebase/conflict outcomes retain their successful or paused result and explain the missing contents. Production UI refreshes the actual branch/status and offers the existing Git LFS download retry instead of implying checkout/history rewriting must be repeated. Git outcome dialogs use neutral titles. Conflict pointer metadata cannot be edited as ordinary text: users choose an entire side, its pointer is staged, and verified contents download afterward. A failed download keeps that conflict resolution staged.
- The new clean-rebase regression initially failed: committed and indexed pointer IDs matched, but the index retained the 129-byte pointer length after expansion to 8,193 bytes. JGit's metadata comparison reported a worktree edit before checking filtered content. Hydration now acquires the index lock before replacement and invalidates only the selected entry's length hint; pointer object ID, mode, stage and flags remain intact. Tests require a clean resulting worktree and detection of a subsequent same-size edit. A separate regression holds an external index.lock and proves hydration preserves the exact pointer/index/foreign lock rather than replacing the working file. Diagnostic instrumentation was removed.
- Branch ViewModel work now runs on Dispatchers.IO, gates duplicate mutations before launch, ignores stale results after repository changes, retains completed-checkout notices through refresh and triggers status/diff/conflict/history refresh after actions. Native tests control an in-flight switch/read to prove duplicate rejection, work off the main thread, correct branch after partial success and isolation from a later repository selection.
- Full core verification: **289 tests pass**, no failures/errors/skips (11 additional LFS checkout/rebase/conflict/index-lock cases). Debug, AndroidTest and release APK builds plus release vital lint pass with Java 17 (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-verified-build.log`, 26 seconds; core test results were reused after the final dialog-title-only change). Current release mapping retains the checkout notice/helper, rebase service and branch ViewModel; `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-release-proof.json` records its SHA256. No release APK was installed or published.
- Targeted native regression: **18 tests pass** in 44.251 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-native-regression.log`): nine rebase, three commit/push, two branch ViewModel and four LFS screen tests. Additional SSH/HTTPS transport plus production navigation: **11 tests pass** in 35.008 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-transport-navigation.log`). The transport's independently inspected bare main pointer references the expected 16,387-byte object; one upload, one verification, two downloads and separate SSH download/upload authentication operations are confirmed in `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-transport-proof.json`. No Git credentials escaped to object action URLs.
- Production HTTPS checkout/retry flow passes against separate fresh fixtures in light mode (10.199 seconds, `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-native-light-stable.log`) and dark mode at 160% system text (10.056 seconds, `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-native-dark-xl.log`). It publishes a synthetic assets branch, evicts its cached object, switches via the actual app/DI and verifies downloaded contents. A later switch with rejected credentials retains that branch/pointer and exposes the completed-Git notice; restoring its encrypted connection and using Git LFS downloads the exact contents without another commit/checkout. Independent Git/object inspection confirms unchanged main, the exact 16,390-byte assets object, one upload/verification, three downloads and zero escaped credentials in each fixture (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-light-proof.json`, `/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-dark-proof.json`). Screenshots `/tmp/lfs-checkout-attention-light.png`, `/tmp/lfs-checkout-attention-dark-xl.png` and both retry captures were inspected: the notice wraps, OK is visible at 160%, and LFS contents are available after retry. The large-text download page scrolls normally.
- The first production replay attempted to locate a branch before asynchronous branch loading completed; it is not counted as a pass. The final test first locates the Branches card and waits for its row/action. Early screenshots captured a native dialog entrance frame; final captures wait for stable frames. Test-only changes are not represented as production bug fixes. Fixture workspaces/preferences/credential entries are cleaned by teardown, each owned server was stopped and all ten owned listener ports checked closed. Exact adb forwarding rules and copied remote screenshots were removed; original font scale 1.0 and default all-files-storage permission were preserved/restored. See tools/testing/README.md for replay details.
- This closes the missing hydration wiring for these paths, with JVM transport and production Android branch-retry evidence. Native LFS-specific rebase/conflict failure UX, other providers/devices, very large files, external-writer/power-loss races and full release/accessibility/motion qualification remain open. Manual lock UI, advanced tracking, folder publishing, automation, push/background sync, widgets, diagnostics, onboarding/localization and every other pending parity requirement remain part of the full active goal.
- Final installed-build verification after the neutral dialog-title change: **12 tests pass** in 29.946 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-lfs-checkout-final-native.log`), covering all ten production navigation checks and both controlled branch ViewModel regressions. This turn verifies **30 distinct native scenarios**, with the HTTPS checkout scenario additionally repeated in light/dark large-text configurations. Final device checks confirm font scale 1.0, MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE default and no adb reverse rules. Final `git diff --check` passes.

### Persistent diagnostics, safe export and recovery (2026-10-27)

- Added Settings → Diagnostics with live newest-first entries, All/Info/Warnings/Errors filters, visible/stored counts, selectable entry text, Copy visible, the native Android share chooser and confirmed Clear all. Copy/share use the selected severity filter; clear includes hidden entries and explains that later operations can record new events. Empty, filtered-empty, loading, storage-error and corrupt-log recovery states are connected. Native Back returns to Settings.
- Diagnostics stores typed operation/outcome/reason fields, UUID and timestamp only. It has no free-text channel for exception messages, Git output, repository/remote addresses, paths, authors, commit messages, file content or credentials. A real-Git core test supplies distinctive private names/content and proves neither the file nor export contains them. The log records clone, fetch, pull, push, stage/unstage/discard, commit, branch, merge, rebase/conflict, LFS, stash, tag and revert outcomes. Completed Git work with missing LFS contents is attention rather than a failed checkout; paused rebases and unsuccessful result objects are classified explicitly. Diagnostic persistence failure cannot change the Git action's result or exception.
- The single-process store writes an atomic fsynced file in Android no_backup/diagnostics, bounded independently by 500 entries and 256 KiB. Writes and initial loading run off the UI thread. Loading malformed/oversized data preserves the original and exposes a clear-to-recover state. Failed persistence preserves the last committed memory state. Symlink log paths cannot read/replace another file. Startup removes regular files in the store's owned temporary namespace after an interrupted write, retaining the committed log, unrelated files and symlinks. Core tests cover restart/clear durability, byte/entry bounds, concurrent producers, failure classification, export filtering, storage failure isolation and interrupted-write cleanup.
- Full core result: **299 tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips, including ten diagnostics tests. Debug, AndroidTest and release builds plus release vital lint pass with Java 17 (`/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-orphan-final-build.log`, 71 seconds). The release mapping retains the diagnostic store, entry and typed schema; `/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-release-proof.json` records the mapping hash. No release app was installed or published.
- Native UI: **4 tests pass** in 17.094 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-native-ui.log`), covering live filtering and exact filtered clipboard contents, native share-chooser cancellation, clear cancellation/confirmation including hidden records, reload persistence and corrupt-log recovery. These use UUID-scoped stores and never clear the production log. The chooser assertion resolves the actual system package rather than assuming Android's old package name.
- Actual process restart: prepare and recovery phases pass in different debug-app processes with an explicit host force-stop between them. The final test additionally leaves an interrupted-write file, verifies its removal without losing the two committed records, checks filtered export, verifies durable clear and deletes only its isolated store/marker (`/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-cold-final-prepare.log`, 0.061 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-cold-final-recover.log`, 0.063 seconds). The installed app's original diagnostics are retained.
- Production/native regression with logging enabled: **29 tests pass** in 63.792 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-native-regression.log`): all eleven file/navigation cases, both branch ViewModel cases, nine rebase, three commit/push and four LFS screen tests. This includes actual Settings → Diagnostics → hardware Back through production navigation and DI. After the interrupted-write correction, the final installed build passes all four diagnostics UI tests plus the production route again: **5 tests pass** in 16.304 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-final-native.log`). Together with the two cold phases, this turn verifies **35 distinct native scenarios**; repeated configurations are not counted as additional distinct scenarios.
- Visual evidence: inspected `/tmp/diagnostics-filter-light.png`, `/tmp/diagnostics-clear-dark-xl.png` and `/tmp/diagnostics-empty-dark-xl.png`. Filter/action rows wrap, entries remain readable and the clear dialog's actions remain visible. Local Compose density does not scale a separate platform dialog window; therefore the clear/cancel/confirm flow was additionally run with actual system font scale 1.6, passed in 5.715 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-native-system-xl.log`), and the settled dialog was recaptured. Original system scale 1.0 was restored afterward.
- Motion diagnostics: `/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-ui-flow.mp4` contains 324 frames (18.429 seconds) covering the UI test flows, with contact sheet `/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-contact.png`. The actual-system-large-text confirmation recording `/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-dark-xl.mp4` contains 157 frames (7.641 seconds), with `/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-xl-contact.png`. Sampled frames and the settled screenshots were inspected. Recordings include test-host activity transitions and the pre-existing Git screen; these are not a full production transition acceptance pass or a measured 60-fps release certification. Landscape, screen-reader/release-device performance and complete motion/accessibility qualification remain open.
- An independent host read of the real installed app's production diagnostics verified the exact allowed fields and enum values, 82 entries / 10,356 bytes at the final checkpoint, and no free-text/credential fields (`/tmp/syncmd-diagnostics-production-proof.json`). No logs are automatically uploaded; sharing invokes the system chooser and the test cancels it. Temporary stores, cold-start marker and copied device evidence are cleaned after verification; original preferences, font scale and default all-files-storage permission are preserved/restored.

### First-run tour, replay, help and release notes (2026-10-27)

- Added an Android-native four-page tour covering repositories, local editing, reviewed Git work and setup. First-run progress is committed after page changes and resumes after recreation. Horizontal paging, explicit Continue, previous-page Back and Skip are connected. Setup offers GitHub sign-in, URL cloning, the offline sample, storage selection and continuing without an account, using the existing production screens/services. Completion removes the tour from the startup path; Back from a setup destination returns to repositories. Settings replay always starts at page zero and returns to Settings without changing first-run completion.
- Established installations are detected from existing repository/account/storage data when adopting the guide preferences and retain their startup flow. New installations see the tour. Guide preferences contain only initialization/completion flags, a bounded page number, a typed pending setup target and the acknowledged build revision. The target remains available until navigation reaches a resumed entry and consumes it, so activity restoration cannot silently lose a chosen destination. Target enum names are explicitly retained by R8.
- Added Settings → Release notes, a current-build overview with the actual BuildConfig version/code, and a once-per-later-build acknowledgement dialog. First adoption establishes the current revision as its baseline; subsequent higher build revisions show notes until acknowledged. Fresh tour completion marks the current notes seen. Settings' former hardcoded 1.0.2 version now displays the installed version. No app version number or store publication was changed by this work.
- Added Send feedback, Join our Discord and Copy support email, using the support address/community link already configured by iOS. Feedback invokes ACTION_SENDTO with a mailto draft containing app/build, Android API and model metadata. It includes no repository, account or credential information and never sends automatically. Missing/blocked handlers expose a recoverable message and a copyable address. Native monitors intercept email/browser intents during tests; no feedback was sent and Discord was not opened externally. New tour/help/note UI copy uses Android string resources; full app translation parity remains open.
- The initial recreation regression failed: the pager restored an older saved position and wrote it over newer committed tour progress. The fix synchronizes it to the authoritative progress before accepting settled-page events. The same test now verifies the saved page after activity recreation, previous-page hardware Back, completion into URL cloning and no Back path into the completed tour. Failed progress writes allow in-memory advancement/completion, with an explicit notice that the tour may return next time. Later successful target/note bookkeeping cannot reopen the tour within that completed session, even if the original completion write failed.
- Fixed an existing sample entry-point issue exposed by the guide: opening an already registered sample could retain another selected vault. Sample entry now selects its canonical working copy after registration and before navigating. Its native test starts with a different selected vault, verifies the sample becomes selected and reaches the actual vault screen, and restores the catalog. An isolated incomplete-workspace test proves the original file is kept and both the sample and global repository error states report failure, rather than silently returning home from onboarding.
- Final Java 17 verification: **299 core tests pass**, no failures/errors/skips; debug, AndroidTest and release builds plus release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-guide-final-verified-build.log`, final incremental run 3 seconds; earlier full release verification 29 seconds). Release mapping and hash proof are in `/tmp/syncmd-guide-release-proof.json`. Only the debug package was installed, on dedicated emulator-5556. No release APK was installed or published.
- Final native verification: **23 tests pass** in 47.487 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-guide-verified-native.log`): five guide/store/support-contract tests, seven production guide/help/setup tests and all eleven existing file/Git/LFS/rebase/navigation tests. Cases cover fresh and established-install state, pending-target persistence, future-build note acknowledgement, storage-write failure/recovery, every setup choice, replay, exact public email copying, intercepted support intents, correct sample selection and intact incomplete-sample data. Temporary preferences/directories are UUID-scoped; the production guide/catalog preferences are restored where a test changes them.
- Actual system large-text verification: setup in dark mode and the native release-note acknowledgement pass with font scale 1.6, **2 tests pass** in 6.824 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-guide-native-xl.log`). The original font scale is restored in a finally block (`/tmp/syncmd-guide-xl-device-proof.json`). Inspected `/tmp/app-tour-setup-dark-xl.png`: all setup actions wrap, scroll and remain reachable, including Continue without an account and Skip. `/tmp/release-notes.png` shows the current build label and readable feature sections. Initial light captures included Android's public-email clipboard overlay; a separate replay refreshes that evidence after the overlay expires.
- Interaction diagnostics: `/tmp/syncmd-app-guide-final.mp4` contains 363 frames / 27.021 seconds, with `/tmp/syncmd-guide-final-contact.png`; `/tmp/syncmd-guide-xl.mp4` contains 138 frames / 9.058 seconds, with `/tmp/syncmd-guide-xl-contact.png`. Contact sheets and settled screenshots were inspected. Recordings include test-host transitions and pre-existing Git snapshots; they are not a measured 60-fps release certification or complete production motion acceptance. The first, failing restoration recording is retained separately and is not counted as a pass.
- Broader live OAuth/mail/browser-provider qualification, translations, screen-reader/landscape/physical-device coverage and complete release motion/accessibility acceptance remain open. Publishing local folders, manual LFS lock UI/advanced tracking, callbacks/automation, widgets, background/push sync and all other pending matrix requirements remain in the full active goal.
- Final clean light-mode replay passes in 5.718 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-guide-clean-light.log`); `/tmp/app-tour-light.png` was recaptured and inspected with both footer actions unobscured. `/tmp/syncmd-guide-final-device-proof.json` confirms that persisted guide metadata contains only the allowed guide keys, scoped test directories/preferences were removed, font scale is 1.0 and no adb reverse rules remain. The all-files-storage app-op remains default. Copied device screenshots and owned remote recordings were removed; local evidence is retained. Final `git diff --check` passes. The full goal remains active.


### Reviewed manual LFS lock management (2026-10-27)

- Connected the Git → Git LFS screen to explicit read-only listing, paginated ownership verification, lock creation for indexed lockable files, ordinary release of owned locks and force release of foreign locks. Read-only results never grant mutation controls. The force dialog starts with an unchecked acknowledgement, carries the reviewed file/branch/endpoint/owner, supports scrolling at large text sizes and exposes the entire acknowledgement as the checkbox's accessible label. Cancelling the dialog sends no write.
- Every mutation checks the frozen local review, freshly verifies complete server ownership, and checks again after authentication/endpoint consent before sending a write. Branch/HEAD, metadata, local/global attributes, resolved Git credentials, encrypted connection record and separately scoped custom endpoint credentials participate in freshness checks. Only digests of credential data enter review state. A normal release cannot silently become a force release after ownership changes. Creating a lock rechecks that the candidate still exists as an ordinary indexed lockable file.
- Mutation responses distinguish an unconfirmed request that may have applied from a confirmed write whose subsequent refresh failed. Either case requires refreshing before another action. Duplicate UI submissions are gated synchronously; mutations finish under the repository lease before Back is enabled. Read-only checks permit leaving the screen, and coroutine cancellation now stops before fetching another page or resuming a request after consent. Local worktree/index/refs are never changed by these lock controls.
- Fixed the failing global-attributes test fixture by using its canonical path: macOS aliases `/var` to `/private/var`. Production symlink rejection was preserved. A separate real HTTPS regression first demonstrated that cancellation still requested the next lock page; the fix checks the current operation's cancellation state between pages and before requests. No delays or weakened expectations were used to hide this issue.
- Core verification: **307 tests pass**, no failures/errors/skips (`/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-verified-build.log`). Eight added reviewed-lock tests cover successful create/release and unchanged local state, read-only access, changed branch/attributes/resolved credentials/connection revision, changed ownership, confirmed versus unconfirmed replies, custom endpoint credentials/approval-time changes and cancelled pagination. Debug/AndroidTest/release assembly and release vital lint pass; final production build log is `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-final-build.log` (30 seconds), followed by a test-only isolation build. Release mapping retains the lock DTOs/manager; APK/mapping hashes are recorded in `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-release-proof.json`. No release APK was installed or published.
- Native production verification on dedicated API 35 emulator-5556: **18 passing executions across 17 distinct tests**. The new lock flow passes in light mode (17.978 seconds, `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-native-light.log`) and actual system font scale 1.6/dark mode (17.192 seconds, `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-native-dark-xl.log`). It tests read-only results, force acknowledgement and cancellation, rejection of credentials changed during confirmation, fresh verification, force release, a twice-submitted create confirmation, owned release, empty results and hardware Back. It compares exact working bytes, index, local HEAD and remote HEAD. Each run uses its own disposable HTTPS fixture and records exactly one create, two releases, one clone download, no uploads/verifications and no escaped Git credentials. The final test explicitly preserves/restores onboarding preferences as well as the repository catalog and encrypted fixture connection.
- Existing regression coverage: all **15 LFS-screen/file-navigation tests pass** in 38.828 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-native-regression.log`). The existing blocked-push/retry flow also passes in 8.083 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-push-regression.log`) against a fresh fixture: two-page foreign ownership blocks the push, explicit fixture release permits publishing the same local commit, and counters show three ownership checks, one release and one upload/verification. Independent host verification hashes the remote's exact 16,389-byte object and confirms the expected fixture bytes. These are loopback-only Git operations; no real account or external repository is used.
- Visual evidence: settled light and dark/large-text force-dialog, stale-review and owned-lock screenshots were retrieved to `/tmp/lfs-manual-*.png` and inspected. The final large-text force capture shows the complete acknowledgement after scrolling, with both actions reachable. Light interaction recording `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-light.mp4` contains 220 frames / 18.857 seconds; dark/large-text recording `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-dark-xl.mp4` contains 327 frames / 17.970 seconds. Contact sheets at `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-*-contact.png` were inspected. They include the pre-existing activity/test-host transitions and are debug-emulator diagnostics, not measured 60-fps release-device acceptance. Screen-reader, landscape, physical-device and complete release-motion qualification remain open.
- Passing fixture counters and local/remote preservation evidence are recorded in `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-light-proof.json`, `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-dark-xl-proof.json` and `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-push-proof.json`. Fixture processes and SSH children are stopped after checking exact process identity; owned forwarding rules and copied device media are removed. `/tmp/syncmd-manual-locks-device-proof.json` confirms font scale restored to 1.0, no adb reverse rules, no owned test repositories and unchanged default all-files-storage permission. Local evidence is retained. `git diff --check` passes.
- Source-parity clarification: the current iOS source exposes LFS create/list/verify/unlock service APIs but no separate manual-lock UI caller was found. The Android controls fulfill the previously retained implementation scope; they should not be described as an already-enabled iOS UI. Advanced extension-wide tracking, publishing local folders, push/background sync, widgets/system actions, callbacks/Obsidian, localization and every other open matrix requirement remain active.


### Extension-wide LFS tracking and frozen tracking reviews (2026-10-27)

- Added Git → Git LFS → Review matching extensions. The confirmation shows repository/branch, exact case-sensitive patterns, current affected files (including unselected and unchanged committed siblings), retained explicit choices and the effect on future matching files. It changes only .gitattributes; converting contents to pointers still happens when the user stages affected files. Existing exact-file LFS and regular-Git choices remain available. The extension button is disabled if no selected file has a recognized binary extension.
- Matched the current iOS auto-tracking policy: the same known binary extensions and 10 MiB unknown-binary threshold; rules include lower-case, upper-case and selected-case variants. Selected large unknown types keep escaped exact paths alongside extension rules. A future unselected mixed-case variant not named by those patterns still needs its own choice. Ignored files stay ignored.
- Preview resolves a proposed root attributes node entirely in memory using JGit's attribute engine; it does not temporarily modify the working tree. Existing root text stays later than extension defaults, nested/info attributes keep their precedence, and exact exceptions preserve current explicit filter/diff/merge/text choices, including inherited global choices. Higher-priority rules that prevent a selected choice fail before writing. Limits bound paths and generated rule bytes; cancellation is checked between review operations.
- Applying rebuilds the complete review and compares file identities, selected files, proposed contents, affected/retained paths and rule metadata. Changed siblings, new matching files, changed local/global rules, index, HEAD or branch require another review. Same-OID branch changes now invalidate exact-file choices too. Exact confirmations freeze their plan and selected set rather than reading a later selection at submission. Duplicate confirmation is gated before coroutine launch; successful tracking mutations invalidate previous server-lock reviews.
- Both exact and extension updates now use the existing revision-checked, fsynced atomic text writer. For a previously absent attributes file, exclusive creation reserves an empty file before atomic replacement; a failed save may leave an empty file but cannot expose partially written rules or overwrite a concurrently created file. Working contents, staged index and refs stay intact while applying a tracking decision.
- Core verification: **314 tests pass**, no failures/errors/skips. Seven new integration tests cover reviewed case variants, unchanged tracked siblings, future files, ignored files, preserved root/nested/global choices and custom attributes, new/changed siblings, changed global/nested rules, same-commit branch switches and escaped large unknown-file fallbacks. Actual stage operations produce pointers after consent while retaining working bytes. Full core/build log is `/tmp/syncmd-extensions-navigation-probe-build.log`; final clean debug/AndroidTest/release assembly and vital lint pass in 30 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-extensions-clean-build.log`). Final release class retention and APK/mapping hashes are in `/tmp/syncmd-extensions-build-proof.json`. No release APK was installed or published.
- Native verification: **18 distinct LFS-screen and production file/navigation tests pass** in 48.516 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-extensions-native-regression.log`). New cases exercise cancel without a write; a stale unselected sibling; fresh review; duplicate confirmation; unchanged index, HEAD and contents; pointer staging for selected/unselected/unchanged files; retained regular-Git bytes; a clean final commit; unknown-extension exact tracking with the extension action disabled; and the actual repositories → Git → extension review → hardware Back route. All fixtures use UUID-scoped app-owned storage and no external account or Git host.
- The initial production-route test ran ahead of asynchronous Git loading and LFS inspection. Scoped probes captured target-position changes and confirmed resumed, correctly scoped callbacks when invoked; a later failure queried the extension action before its initial inspection existed. The test now waits for the actual loaded file count/branches, fully positions the target and waits for tracking candidates. It retains ordinary pointer taps and the original navigation assertions, with no sleeps, retaps or weakened expectations. **Eight consecutive independent clean runs pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-extensions-navigation-verified.json`). All temporary lifecycle/position/scroll probes were removed; production navigation behavior was preserved. Initial failing logs are retained separately.
- Focused light-mode flow passes in 12.712 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-extensions-native-light.log`); final dark-mode flow at actual system font scale 1.6 passes in 8.946 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-extensions-native-dark-xl.log`). Settled screenshots `/tmp/lfs-extensions-light.png` and `/tmp/lfs-extensions-dark-xl.png` were inspected. Native plural resources fix the one-file wording; shorter copy/actions and a scrollable body keep patterns, file names and confirmation controls readable/reachable at large text sizes. Candidate checkboxes now expose their filename/size labels through one toggleable row. New extension dialog copy uses Android strings/plurals; full language parity remains open.
- Interaction diagnostics: `/tmp/syncmd-extensions-light.mp4` contains 111 frames / 12.754 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-extensions-dark-xl.mp4` contains 140 frames / 9.481 seconds. Contact sheets `/tmp/syncmd-extensions-*-contact.png` were inspected. They include pre-existing activity/test-host transitions and are not measured 60-fps release-device acceptance. Screen-reader, landscape, physical-device, large-repository and broader LFS failure/performance qualification remain open.
- `/tmp/syncmd-extensions-device-proof.json` confirms restored system font scale 1.0, no adb reverse rules, no remaining owned LFS-screen/navigation repositories and unchanged default all-files-storage permission. Copied device screenshots/recordings were removed, local evidence retained, and `git diff --check` passes. Publishing local folders, push/background sync, widgets/system actions, callbacks/Obsidian, localization and every other open requirement in the matrix remain active.


### Folder publication: Git preparation and pinned transport foundation (2026-10-27)

- Added the first-publication Git layer under services/publication. Inspection retains exact paths, SHA256 identities, byte sizes, owner execute bits, ignore decisions and unsupported-attribute reasons. Suggested selection excludes ignored files, sensitive filenames/keys, files above 10 MiB, valid LFS pointers and files governed by LFS/custom/content-conversion attributes. Hidden files remain reviewable. It rejects existing/broken/enclosing/nested Git metadata, symlinks, special files and normalized Unicode name collisions. Ordinary files named HEAD/config and ordinary objects/refs directories are not mistaken for Git metadata without a HEAD signature.
- Matched the iOS first-publication limits: 50,000 inspected files, at most 10,000 selected files / 256 MiB total, and 10 MiB per selected file. The storage preflight estimates three times the payload plus per-file and fixed metadata overhead. Hashing streams with cancellation checks, selected-file and pointer reads are bounded, and byte/mode/ignore/attribute changes anywhere in the reviewed folder invalidate initial preparation. Empty regular files and executable modes are preserved; Git does not preserve empty folders.
- Preparation exclusively reserves .git for a UUID workflow and writes a fsynced, locked ownership marker before initialization. All initializer files are created only when absent or exactly matching owned progress. A deterministic timestamp/signature, reviewed raw blobs, complete tree validation and an expected-absent main-ref update pin the initial commit. No ordinary Git staging command or user content-conversion filter is run by preparation. Excluded paths get local literal info/exclude rules so later Stage All does not silently add them. Working files are never written.
- Recovery validates the exact initial commit's tree, file set, bytes, modes, author/committer, message, timestamp and parent count. A fresh service can recognize a commit persisted before its marker update, retain the same OID and preserve newer working/staged content. Foreign workflow IDs, changed history, malformed markers, incompatible saved metadata and held index locks fail without deleting or replacing that state. The owned pending-publication config marker remains set; clearing it belongs to the still-pending guarded app registration workflow.
- Added publication of only the saved OID to main. Production accepts canonical credential-free GitHub HTTPS destinations; scoped authentication checks every HTTP request against that repository. The live receive-pack advertisement must be empty for a first update, and the actual command requires the old main OID to be zero, without force. An already matching destination reconciles without another push. Missing/failed acknowledgements are explicitly uncertain, and finish verifies the live main OID before adding local tracking. Origin identity is pinned in the marker before network work and cannot silently switch destinations. Newer worktree/index content is never restaged or committed by retry.
- The initial read-only test caught JGit create(false) writing a .git pointer into its configured worktree despite external metadata. Only disposable fixtures were affected; no app entry point uses this service yet. Inspection now initializes entirely inside an owned scratch workspace, closes it, and reopens that metadata against the selected folder without initialization. The original no-metadata and exact-folder-content assertions pass. Scratch metadata, including temporary alternate-object access for saved-tree checks, is removed afterward.
- Core verification: **332 tests pass**, no failures/errors/skips (`/tmp/syncmd-publication-preparation-build.log`). The 18 new publication tests cover inspection/exclusions, exact CRLF/Unicode/empty/executable content, stale unselected/new files and rule/mode edits, existing metadata/symlinks, capacity and quota limits, deterministic recovery with staged newer data, index-lock resumption, marker/header corruption, only-pinned-commit publication, idempotent retry, a lost acknowledgement after application, nonempty destinations, a competing main created after advertisement, saved-tree versus newer-worktree attributes, destination pinning and rejected credential-bearing/noncanonical URLs. The transport tests use real owned bare repositories via an injected transport; their logical GitHub URLs never contact GitHub.
- Java 17 debug/AndroidTest/release assembly and release vital lint pass (1 minute 20 seconds, same build log). Release mapping retains publication DTOs and the private recovery marker; hashes are recorded in `/tmp/syncmd-publication-git-build-proof.json`. Only the debug app/test APKs were installed on dedicated API 35 emulator-5556. No release APK was installed/published and no external GitHub account, API creation or Git push was used.
- Android verification: **4 native tests pass** in 0.593 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-publication-native.log`). They exercise read-only inspection, raw-byte preparation and sensitive/ignored exclusions, Android/media preparation with the all-files-storage app-op still default, recovery from a missing post-commit marker update with newer staged bytes, and a real local Git push whose successful acknowledgement is lost. Retry performs only one push, retains the same local/remote commit and exact newer index/working bytes, then establishes tracking. No UI or end-to-end GitHub creation claim is made by these backend tests.
- `/tmp/syncmd-publication-git-device-proof.json` confirms no remaining UUID fixture directories in app files or Android/media, no owned inspection scratch directories in cache, system font scale 1.0, no adb reverse rules and unchanged default all-files-storage permission. `git diff --check` passes.
- The folder publication requirement remains incomplete. Next work must persist the app workflow before changing the selected folder or starting publication network effects; pin the verified GitHub account ID; implement public/private creation and ambiguous-creation reconciliation with explicit destination adoption; protect unfinished folders from ordinary discovery/import, tracking removal, moves and automatic synchronization; connect saved folder grants/reconnection, selection refresh, UI confirmations and idempotent credential/catalog registration. These are required before exposing the feature or marking parity complete. Every other open matrix category remains active.

### Folder publication: API, private journal and recovery coordinator (2026-10-27)

- Verified the previously written GitHub API client against its saved 12-test result, then reran it with this milestone. The client pins the fixed HTTPS API origin, verified stable account ID, repository identity and requested visibility. Creation explicitly sends private/public and auto_init=false. Redirects are disabled and fixed-length POST streaming prevents automatic HTTP-stack replay after a dropped response. Malformed/mismatched acknowledgements, unexpected success statuses and uncertain transport outcomes require reconciliation; a failed GET never proves that a repository does not exist. Only known rejection responses permit another explicit creation attempt.
- Matching refs are bounded, paginated across all namespaces and checked for duplicates. Pagination cannot change the origin/repository, repeat a page or supply unexpected query parameters. Branch checks require exact ref names, including Unicode paths. A recognized empty-repository response still requires readable matching metadata; denied access is never interpreted as an empty destination. Strict UTF-8/JSON input rejects duplicate fields, type coercions, excessive depth/nodes and oversized responses. API error messages are static and never include response bodies or credentials. Tests use real loopback HTTP I/O through a trusted logical-URL adapter; no GitHub API mutation occurs. Android HTTP/TLS qualification remains pending.
- Added a bounded schema-versioned private journal with OS/process locking, exclusive temporary files, file fsync, atomic replacement and directory synchronization. The Android host must place it in noBackupFilesDir and supply native directory fsync where required. Records contain reviews, URI bindings, verified account/destination identities, selected paths, commit/tree IDs and typed problems; tokens are absent. Strict read/encode validation rejects missing/unknown/duplicate/coerced fields and incompatible phases. Corruption is preserved and blocks writes. Expected-record replacement rejects stale screens, while unfinished ancestor/descendant folders overlap. Snapshot, account, visibility and destination choices freeze when preparation starts; later commit/remote identities cannot change.
- Added the core coordinator, persisting PREPARING before owned Git changes, CREATING_REMOTE before POST, and PUSHING before ref updates. Recovery only normalizes uncertain phases and performs no network/Git mutation. Ambiguous creation requires a GET-only candidate review followed by explicit adoption and another fresh empty-all-refs check. A lost push acknowledgement can reconcile the same saved OID without another push or commit. Cancellation persists uncertainty, and coordinators sharing a store reject concurrent publication/recovery/removal operations. Platform folder access is a required injected boundary, not a fallback to an arbitrary saved filesystem path.
- Added 9 journal and 7 coordinator tests. They cover actual disk persistence across instances, concurrent stale writers, corrupt/unknown/coerced JSON, duplicate selected paths, symlink journals/locks, overlapping folders and aliases, frozen destinations, private-store placement, mismatched remote metadata, and an injected failure after atomic rename. Coordinator tests assert durable intent inside mutation seams, explicit fresh adoption, changed accounts/permissions/history, cancellation, concurrent coordinators, known-rejection retry and lost-push reconciliation. Two initial test methods inferred a non-void Truth return type; explicit Unit returns fixed JUnit discovery, and all original assertions pass.
- Full core verification: **360 tests pass**, no failures/errors/skips, including 46 publication/API tests. Java 17 debug, AndroidTest and release assembly plus vital release lint pass in 1 minute 37 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-publication-journal-build.log`). Release mapping retains the journal envelope, records, phases, API client and coordinator. Counts and release APK/mapping hashes are recorded in `/tmp/syncmd-publication-journal-build-proof.json`. No APK was installed/published and no native test was run for this new journal/coordinator milestone; earlier Git-only device evidence is not substituted for these paths.
- This remains an unexposed backend workflow. Still required: Android private-store/native-fsync integration and native/API transport evidence; saved URI grant/identity verification and reconnection; persistent review choices and refresh; connected import/move/removal/ordinary-Git availability guards; encrypted account assignment and idempotent catalog registration; native file/account/visibility/unknown-outcome review UI; and production navigation/device/accessibility/motion evidence. Registration must become durable, then the journal must become COMPLETED, and only then may pending-publication Git configuration be cleared. PUBLISHED currently remains protected pending that integration. All other parity requirements remain active.

### Folder publication: Android access, ordinary-operation guards and registration (2026-10-27)

- Added AndroidFolderPublications, using noBackupFilesDir and native Os.fsync for the journal's containing directory. Begin verifies the explicitly selected stable GitHub account, derives its author, defaults to private visibility and persists the read-only review. It rejects registered-folder overlaps, including stale catalog ownership, before changing Git state or contacting the publication API. It rejects symlink selection before canonicalization. Browsing another account does not change the publication's token source. No user-facing route is exposed yet.
- Folder access now checks the original filesystem directory key, canonical path and retained tree URI. A missing URI grant never falls back to an accessible saved path. RepositoryLocations.requireFolderBinding is read-only and verifies the exact provider-resolved saved folder. Grant rebinding can restore access at the same path after access verification and prepared-commit validation; it cannot replace the directory or rewrite the frozen snapshot/destination. Moved-folder reconnection is still pending, as is native publication coverage using an actual granted device-folder URI.
- Added durable review-update and refresh operations. Refresh preserves prior exclusions, removes files that became ineligible and suggests only new eligible nonsensitive files. A changed folder identity fails without replacing saved progress. Choices remain editable only in REVIEW, before the owned initial snapshot freezes.
- Installed FolderPublicationGate in RepositoryOperationCoordinator and connected it to the real private journal at Android application startup. Existing repository/file/discovery/clone-publication/move/removal services therefore check unfinished ancestor/descendant workflows inside their leases. The owned publication's coroutine permit is restricted to its exact path and workflow. A partially initialized ownership marker blocks ordinary operations even before config exists; a pending config marker remains protective if the private journal is unavailable. Journal corruption fails closed and is preserved. Wrong owned-workflow IDs retain the established ownership error; the first core rerun caught that distinction and the implementation now preserves it.
- Added guarded completion: verify the saved remote/main, perform idempotent encrypted account assignment and catalog registration, save COMPLETED durably, then clear pending Git config and remove the owned metadata marker last. Registration refuses another credential policy or catalog owner and retains a workflow receipt for retry. A preferences commit must succeed again before acknowledgment, including when a failed earlier commit updated memory. Failed registration leaves PUBLISHED; failure after durable completion can retry clearing without re-registering or demanding the original HEAD after subsequent local work. Saved URI/folder access still applies.
- Core verification: **367 tests pass**, no failures/errors/skips, including **53 publication/API tests**. Seven new tests cover refreshed exclusions, grant rebinding, registration/clear ordering and retry, pre-metadata journal protection, ancestor/descendant and permit scope, corruption, and real Git pending-marker cleanup with later history preservation. Full debug/AndroidTest/release assembly and vital release lint pass; final build after the catalog preflight takes 30 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-publication-registration-catalog-build.log`). No release APK was installed or published.
- Native publication verification: the final APKs pass **12 focused checks** in 2.467 seconds: 11 publication Git/journal/access/registration tests plus the existing denied-storage case (`/tmp/syncmd-publication-registration-catalog-native.log`). New tests use UUID-scoped app-private/no-backup storage, native fsync, real Git preparation and an owned bare repository, with fake API/account seams. They cover preserving corrupt journal bytes, new-host preparation recovery, a different folder at the same path, missing grants/symlinks, pinned account choice and author despite a browsing switch, sensitive defaults, stale catalog rejection, encrypted binding conflicts/idempotence, real local publication, repeat registration without duplicates, and preserving later history after completion. Real GitHub API creation, Android HTTP/TLS publication and the production UI are not covered by these fixtures.
- Broader regression evidence covers 48 existing repository/account/move/storage cases across runs. An initial 38-case invocation passed 37 cases and failed one setup precondition because a move case requires shared-storage permission. After granting it in a fresh process, that case passed. A separate storage-screen case then hit a Compose SnapshotStateObserver NPE during its asynchronous update (`/tmp/syncmd-publication-registration-storage-native.log`). The unchanged isolated case passes twice (`/tmp/syncmd-storage-observer-repro-{1,2}.log`), and the unchanged complete 10-case storage suite passes in 53.181 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-storage-observer-suite-repro.log`). This is retained as an unresolved observer/test flake, not a claimed production fix. Initial logs remain available. The broader first-run log is `/tmp/syncmd-publication-registration-regression-native.log`.
- The dedicated GitSync AVD was initially stopped; Argent booted gitsync-paid-review-20261026 as emulator-5554 in this session. Other devices were untouched. Temporary all-files access was restored to its original default; final checks run with that default. Device checks confirm font scale 1.0, no adb reverse rules and no remaining owned publication fixture directories in app files/no-backup storage. Build counts, APK/mapping hashes, log paths and the qualification caveat are retained in `/tmp/syncmd-publication-registration-proof.json`. git diff --check passes.

### Folder publication: moved and restored folder recovery (2026-10-28)

Unprepared reviews still require the original path and directory identity; completed records cannot be reassigned.
- Reconnection holds the selected directory lease, rechecks platform access, rejects other pending publication ownership and registered destinations, and atomically compares/replaces the private journal. It changes only path, directory identity and URI binding. The reviewed files, selected paths, author, account, destination, visibility, prepared OID, phase and remote receipt remain frozen. No account lookup, repository creation, push, commit, working-file write or index replacement occurs during reconnection.
- Retained source copies are never deleted or silently unlocked. Their pending ownership markers continue to block ordinary Git operations. Cross-volume/removable-media behavior and a reviewed retained-copy cleanup flow remain qualification/follow-up work; local copy tests demonstrate changed-inode handling, not physical-volume acceptance.

The final Java 17 offline run passed **375 core tests**, including **61 publication/API tests**, with no failures/errors/skips. Debug, AndroidTest, release assembly and release vital lint passed (`/tmp/syncmd-publication-reconnect-build.log`). An initial fixture counted read-only receive-pack advertisements as pushes; its counter was corrected to count actual push calls before the successful runs.

A new native copy case proves changed-inode rebinding, missing-grant rejection, registered-destination rejection, persisted private progress and retained-original protection.

### Pull All, launcher widget and Quick Settings (2026-10-28)

- Added a production home → Pull All route and scrollable per-repository results, with retry, empty/settings-failure states, individual updated/current/blocked/failed/LFS-attention outcomes and stop feedback. Activity recreation retains its ViewModel; leaving the foreground or route cancels remaining work. A stopped in-flight repository may have finished; the UI explicitly asks users to inspect its status before retrying. Requests are not persisted for automatic replay after process death; cold-process qualification remains open.
- `PullAllRunner` has only the pull repository interface. It cannot stage, commit or push. It serializes duplicate batches globally, holds each canonical repository lease, revalidates catalog membership after waiting, requires an exact top-level working copy, blocks dirty/detached/in-progress/unconfigured/local-only upstream states and uses the existing fast-forward-only pull path. Other failures do not prevent later repositories from running. Local-only ahead commits remain local. Foreground pull transport now has a 30-second timeout; cancellation does not pretend that blocking network I/O stopped immediately.
- Added an Android RemoteViews home widget and one-shot Quick Settings tile. Both open the app and request one run through an immutable PendingIntent targeting a non-exported, no-history activity with separate task affinity. The exported OAuth activity accepts no Pull All action extras. The tile requires `BIND_QUICK_SETTINGS_TILE`, unlocks before running and uses the API 34+ PendingIntent overload with an older-API fallback, following [Android's tile guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/quicksettings-tiles) and [TileService API contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/service/quicksettings/TileService). Pull All is independent of background/automatic-push consent.

Native fixtures prove real local fast-forward, dirty bytes and remote preservation, retry, rapid duplicate/stop, background cancellation, empty/corrupt settings, changed registration, recognized widget metadata, private entry/protected tile and immutable PendingIntent. ### Per-repository action runtime and native controls (2026-10-28)

- Added home → Repository actions with explicit repository selection, read-only current-state review, Status/Pull/Push/Sync choices, redacted endpoints, branch/upstream/OID, editable commit message, write-scope confirmation, progress/stop feedback and structured results. Status reads local Git state without contacting a remote. Selection controls expose selected state; action choices expose radio roles. Long content is scrollable and result OIDs selectable. Activity recreation retains results; route exit/backgrounding cancels active work.
- `RepositoryActionRunner` holds one repository lease through each complete action, revalidates catalog membership and requires the reviewed directory identity, branch, HEAD, upstream, endpoint pair and credential revision. Active conflicts/operations, stale plans and unconfigured upstreams stop mutations. Read-only review performs no fetch. Pull/Sync fetch live branch advertisements and inspect ancestry; dirty files block incoming checkout, divergence never auto-merges/rebases, and push-only refuses incoming commits before staging. Dirty equal-head and local-ahead Sync can publish local edits, matching iOS's up-to-date pull classification.
- Push/Sync resolve the repository author once, use the established reviewed-LFS staging/commit path and iOS-style eight-pass settle window, then fetch/recheck ancestry before a non-force acknowledged push. Every current non-ignored change is included; no selected-file claim is made. Existing ahead commits can upload without another commit. A failed/uncertain upload reports a saved local OID, keeps the commit and requires an explicit new review; a lost reply followed by matching live remote history becomes No Changes without another push or commit. Stale outcomes omit an unknown current OID instead of presenting the reviewed OID as current. JGit push transport now has a 30-second inactivity timeout; Stop does not claim blocking transport stopped immediately.
- The Android production service uses the same repository-specific credential resolver/revision and author resolver as existing Git controls. No new default-account fallback, force path, conflict resolution, rebase or branch switch is introduced by these actions. The exported OAuth entry remains unchanged; no external action URI or intent command has been enabled in this step.
- Core verification: **389 tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips, including **8 new action cases** with owned local/bare Git fixtures. Coverage includes network-free Status with no upstream, non-ignored staging and exact author/message/OID, behind/diverged pre-staging rejection, equal-head dirty Sync and incoming-dirty preservation, clean fast-forward Sync, local-ahead/no-change behavior, changed branch/endpoint/account/catalog rejection, saved-commit retry and lost-reply reconciliation. The first new-fixture run exposed a missing peer `origin` in test setup; the fixture's upstream was configured before the successful runs. Final Java 17 offline debug, AndroidTest and release assembly plus release vital lint passed (`/tmp/syncmd-repository-actions-build.log`); the keyboard-test-only compilation also passed (`/tmp/syncmd-repository-actions-test-build.log`).
- Native targeted run: **7 tests passed** in 25.485 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-repository-actions-native.log`), including 6 real screen/ViewModel/local-Git fixtures and production entry/status/recreation/hardware Back. These exercise Status and dirty up-to-date Pull, typed message and keyboard close, push confirmation/cancel, correct author and acknowledged local-bare upload, failed upload/new review/idempotent retry, clean Sync without needless push, duplicate/Stop and dark large-text layout.
- Final-build regression: **27 native tests passed** in 62.323 seconds on dedicated API 35 `emulator-5554`: all 6 action-screen cases (now also asserting selected semantics), 6 Pull All cases and all 15 file/navigation cases (`/tmp/syncmd-repository-actions-regression.log`). Only debug/test APKs were installed. No live GitHub creation/push, release install or store publication occurred. Owned action fixture directories were removed; real system font scale remains 1.0 and shared-storage permission remains default.
- Inspected screenshots `/tmp/syncmd-repository-actions-status-light.png`, `/tmp/syncmd-repository-actions-status-dark-xl.png` (Compose font scale 1.6), `/tmp/syncmd-repository-actions-pushed-light-final.png` and video contact frames. The finalized untracked native-flow recording `.argent/recordings/screen-recording-emulator-5554-1793194310306.mp4` is 29.656 seconds, includes fixture transitions/blank test activities and predates the final semantics-only UI change and transport timeout. It is visual workflow evidence, not a release performance claim.
- **This is the shared runtime and native control surface for automation, not completion of external automation parity.** Per-repository launcher shortcuts, `x-callback-url` delivery/results, integration consent and interrupted-process handling still need connecting and qualification. Android's [ShortcutInfo contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/pm/ShortcutInfo.Builder) permits shortcut intents to target a private activity; that is the next integration seam. Obsidian credential handoff, push-triggered/background sync, localization and all other open matrix requirements remain active. Broader authentication/storage/LFS failure, cancellation-after-commit, screen-reader, older-device and release motion/performance qualification also remains open.


### Per-repository launcher shortcuts (2026-10-28)

- Connected Android launcher pinning for reviewed Status/Pull/Push/Sync actions, including explicit recurring authorization, launcher approval state, refresh, revocation and forgetting disabled records. Push/Sync consent includes staging, commit and upload scope. Each tap opens the foreground action screen and requests one run; busy requests are rejected rather than queued.
- Launcher intents contain only an opaque UUID and target a non-exported trampoline. Private records are excluded from cloud/device backups and pin directory identity, catalog registration, branch/upstream/endpoints and credential/account assignment. Fresh HEADs are allowed; changed bindings, corrupt records, replaced folders, revoked grants and unapproved/unpinned shortcuts fail closed. Authorization is rechecked under the repository lease and at runner mutation checkpoints.
- Verified managed relocation transfers the shortcut's directory/catalog and re-encrypted credential binding before acknowledging the move. Cross-volume originals remain explicit recoveries; migration does not remove those copies or journals. Legacy catalog edge cases and broader storage/transport/provider qualification remain open.

Debug/test/release assembly and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-shortcuts-verified-build.log`); subsequent changes affect instrumentation only and its APK rebuilds pass. Sixteen backend/native screen cases pass: real Git/private-store/Keystore fixtures with a fake launcher cover binding/revocation/corruption, immutable account ownership, fresh-HEAD repeated actions, move migration, consent cancellation and dark/160% font UI.
- Real Pixel Launcher test passes on dedicated API 35 emulator-5554 (`/tmp/syncmd-shortcuts-home-current-native.log`, 12.927 seconds). It approves the actual platform dialog, finds the unique fixture across launcher pages, taps its icon, observes production Status execution exactly once, recreates the activity without replay, revokes authorization and removes its own icon using launcher accessibility actions. A leaked owned move destination was identified by its exact UUID-bearing local remote and removed; new fixtures use UUID destination names and clean owned staging/recovery/destination paths. A broader combined run later exposed incomplete launcher cleanup: a successful accessibility action did not prove that the launcher had unpinned the icon. Native HOME navigation uses a system HOME key, and home-page discovery uses bounded accessibility scrolling.

### Reviewed x-callback-url requests and Android result delivery (2026-10-28)

- Added the production `syncmd://x-callback-url/{status,pull,push,sync}` entry, separate from OAuth. Accepts `repo` (exact working-folder basename), optional `message`, `x-success`, `x-error`, and an untrusted/unused `x-source` label. Duplicate folder names require explicit selection; display aliases never silently select a different folder. Preparation inspects local Git only; every run requires visible approval. Unknown actions/missing repositories produce reviewable errors. Malformed, duplicate/coerced, oversized and incompatible inputs are rejected without selecting or running a repository.
- Native Automation request route shows the action, exact selected folder, branch/HEAD/upstream/endpoints, write scope, supplied message and return destinations. Execution pins the whole reviewed request (including action/message/return URLs), folder identity/catalog, Git plan, credential revision and verified account binding. Rechecks happen inside the directory lease and runner mutation checkpoints. Cancellation/backgrounding stops queued/running work; duplicate/new requests cannot replace the open request or queue later writes. Activity recreation retains one in-memory state, and intent data is scrubbed after receipt. No request/result is persisted for automatic process replay.
- Mirrors the iOS wire results: `status=ok/error`, action, status branch/SHA/change count, pull `updated`, sync `pull_updated`/`push_skipped`, and retained local `sha`/`commit_saved` on failed upload. A push with no changes is an error; a sync with no changes succeeds. Shared action results now retain an actual pull-updated receipt through later failures. Protocol errors use bounded static messages rather than exception text, tokens, remote credentials or local paths.
- Return is an explicit user action after completion. It opens a newly constructed browsable ACTION_VIEW intent, preserving caller state/fragment while replacing reserved result fields; x-error falls back to x-success. Local/executable/recursive destinations and credential-bearing URL authorities are rejected. Failed receiver launch retains the result for explicit retry without repeating Git. No callback URI is parsed as an Android intent or receives URI grants/extras. This follows [Android deep-link guidance](https://developer.android.com/training/app-links/create-deeplinks) and [Intent/filter semantics](https://developer.android.com/guide/components/intents-filters).
- Core: **405 tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips, including 15 new protocol/real-Git cases covering Unicode/percent/plus handling, duplicates/unsafe URLs, response mapping/partial completion, ambiguous folders, no preparation mutation/network, real pull/push, stale replay and changed account/catalog/endpoints. Debug/test/release assembly and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-callback-native-build.log`, 1m 49s).
- Seven native screen cases pass using UUID-owned real Git stores and fake account/return bindings: consent before mutation or redirect, cancellation/index preservation, dark/160% font write/retry, duplicate folder choice, cancellation while waiting for the lease, busy request preservation, changed ownership, uncertain upload retention and invalid requests. Four production transport cases pass (`/tmp/syncmd-callback-transport-ready-native.log`, 14.758 seconds): actual cold/warm implicit Android link entry, success/cancellation return to the separate test APK process, scrubbed intent/recreation/no repeat, missing receiver retry and background cancellation. No live Git host is used.
- Initial transport verification exposed test-only issues: ActivityScenario ignores lifecycle events once the app scrubs the launch intent; tests now observe actual activity identity without retaining the URI in production. [AndroidX source](https://github.com/android/android-test/blob/main/core/java/androidx/test/core/app/ActivityScenario.java) and device logs confirm this. The standalone receiver uses Android/JDK-only Java because instrumentation removes duplicate target-app Kotlin runtime classes. Tests wait for its visible window before sending hardware Back. These failed runs are not counted as passing evidence.
- Inspected `/tmp/syncmd-callback-write-review-dark-xl.png`: write scope and supplied commit message wrap at 160% font size and both approval/cancel controls remain reachable. Complete release motion/performance, screen-reader, landscape, device/API/storage/auth/LFS and fresh-process interruption qualification remain open.
- **This is reviewed callback delivery, not complete hands-free automation parity.** Persistent/revocable preauthorized callback links, automatic return after approved runs, actual process-death/restart tests, Obsidian connection/credential handoff and every other open parity category remain in the full objective.

### Reusable authorized callbacks and automatic result return (2026-10-28)

- Added an explicit Create authorized automation link consent flow from an ordinary reviewed callback. It fixes the repository identity/registration, branch/upstream, remote endpoints, credential/account assignment, action, commit message and success/error destinations. A link may authorize future local changes; issuance itself performs no Git mutation. Only HEAD and the display alias may change without renewed consent. The dialog explains bearer access, future changes, return policy and revocation before copying.
- Uses a random 256-bit token in `syncmd://authorized-callback/<token>`. Only its SHA-256 digest is persisted in the private, backup-excluded grant store. The public link contains no repository metadata or return query; sensitive clipboard metadata hides the preview. Token-bearing wrapper strings are redacted. A separate no-history entry activity scrubs the incoming URL before opening MainActivity, rejects history restoration and keeps bearer data out of MainActivity task intents and navigation arguments.
- Grants are checked again under the repository lease, with fresh planning and repeated revocation checks at execution checkpoints. Revocation while waiting stops before preparation; revocation during staging stops subsequent commit/push and preserves work already completed. Changed branches, remotes, registrations, credentials or account assignments require renewed consent. Managed moves rebind only the verified same working copy/ownership receipt. Authorized links are listed by public ID, action, location and creation time in Repository actions, with confirmed revoke and explicit forget after revocation.
- Ordinary reviewed runs can opt into automatic result return. Issued links retain their reviewed return preference. Return dispatch requires the app to be foreground and the grant still enabled; backgrounding cancels execution and never queues a later redirect. Typed preparation failures also reach the reviewed error destination. Invalid/revoked links cannot redirect or prevent a subsequent valid incoming link from running. Receiver launch failure leaves an explicit result-delivery retry without repeating Git.
- Current product build passed core/debug/test/release assembly and release vital lint (`/tmp/syncmd-authorized-callback-error-build.log`, 33 seconds); the **405 core tests** have zero failures/errors/skips. - Full targeted native regression: **62 tests passed**, 89.116 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-authorized-callback-final-regression.log`). ### Obsidian companion credential connection (2026-10-28)

- Added Settings → Obsidian connections with repository-specific Prepare and confirmed Forget controls. Preparing writes the companion’s public request/connection descriptors; it does not export a credential. GitHub account clones, URL clones using a GitHub account and history clones prepare metadata after checked registration. Existing/imported vaults can prepare explicitly. Shared working copies remain in their chosen folder. Forget disables future native handoffs and explicitly explains that credentials already imported into Obsidian must be removed/revoked separately.
- Added the production `syncmd://obsidian/prepare` and `/connect` routes, with duplicate/unknown parameter rejection, fixed return routing, UTF-8/plus-safe vault names, activity-intent scrubbing and history-restoration suppression. No caller-selected return URL is accepted. The activity-owned ViewModel supports automatic foreground return and explicit delivery retry without re-export. Background/cancellation stops pending work and never queues an automatic redirect on resume; activity recreation and Back preserve normal navigation.
- Matches the existing companion’s P-256 ECDH, HKDF-SHA256 (`gitsync-obsidian-v1`), AES-256-GCM, request-ID AAD, X9.63 public key and nonce/ciphertext+tag envelope. Seven-day requests and five-minute credential payloads follow the iOS protocol. Credentials never enter callbacks or private binding records. Only public metadata and ciphertext are written into `.git/gitsync-obsidian`. Config and JSON reads are bounded, strict UTF-8, regular-file/no-follow reads; ambiguous origins, config includes/rewrites, duplicate origin URLs and alternate push URLs are refused. Response publication uses a synced temporary file and atomic replacement.
- A private backup-excluded binding fixes the registered folder, metadata identity, original GitHub HTTPS remote, canonical selected account and encrypted credential-record revision. Shared/public vault metadata alone cannot authorize export. The host rechecks folder access, registration, account/credential state and cancellation inside the repository lease immediately before publishing. It consumes native authorization before ciphertext publication, preventing repeated export. Reconnect renews a consumed/expired request for the same binding; a branch checkout does not change vault permission. Browsing-account switches never replace the vault’s account, and removal of the original account fails closed.
- Core verification: **411 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips. Six new tests include independent Bouncy Castle HKDF decryption, matching/authenticated request IDs, expiry, authorization failure, malicious/changed remote config, unsafe metadata files and key/request validation. Debug/AndroidTest and release builds plus release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-final-build.log`, 51 seconds). JSON is encoded explicitly, without adding a reflective persisted schema or release-only crypto dependency. No release app was installed or published.
- Interoperability uses the **actual existing companion source**, not a rewritten decoder. `tools/testing/obsidian_compat.mjs` invokes its `decrypt()` and `installCredentials()` with envelopes from both JVM and the real Android crypto provider. Both pass request binding, expiry, password/username installation and author-config checks (`/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-jvm-compat.log`, `/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-native-compat.log`). Companion `connection.js` SHA-256 is `b1a9fb75aeef5cac8f5faad434fdf1fb319da2c06f67b1891bd1491173c82a53`. All keys/tokens in these fixtures are synthetic; no live GitHub call is made.
- Final broad native regression: **86 tests passed**, 194.447 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-final-regression.log`), including 11 Obsidian cases, ten history clones, 16 production navigation cases and all previous callback/shortcut/Pull All checks. Evidence covers one-use export, reconnect, account isolation/sign-out, changed credentials/remote/catalog, branch checkout, copied public metadata, Forget cancellation/confirmation, clone registration, delivery-only retry, cancellation while waiting for a lease, and the actual Settings route with recreation and hardware Back.
- A separately compiled additional case passed against the same product APK in an actual user-visible app-owned **Android/media vault** with unchanged default storage permission (2.276 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-app-media-native.log`). Ciphertext/request/recipient writes succeed on the shared-storage filesystem while preserving HEAD/index. ### Android Background Sync (2026-10-28)

- Compared the current iOS `BackgroundSyncCoordinator`, `PremiumRuntime`, `RepoAssistSettings`/health models and Settings integration. Its feature flag is currently false. Added Android Settings → Background Sync with **both automatic modes off by default**, explicit per-checkout inclusion, independent automatic pull and automatic commit/push consent, and a native publishing confirmation that explains retained commits/uploads after opt-out. No existing repository is silently enrolled or enabled.
- Enrollment reviews and pins canonical folder identity, catalog registration, checked-out branch, configured remote/upstream, fetch/push URLs, encrypted credential-record revision and verified account assignment. Each pass plans a fresh HEAD. Production enrollment validates HTTPS/SSH endpoints without embedded credentials. Account assignment is included in an opaque connection revision, independently of the credential-record hash, so a formerly anonymous legacy checkout gaining a default account requires fresh review. Browsing-account changes keep the repository's original ownership. Changed/remapped folders, branches, connections, missing access and disconnected credentials fail closed instead of switching branches or choosing another account.
- Shared Git reconciliation implements pull-only, commit/push-only and combined mode. It never automatically merges diverged histories, force-pushes or switches branches. Pull-only preserves drafts; push-only refuses incoming commits; combined mode refuses an incoming checkout that would replace local edits. Existing per-repository author identities, SSH trust and LFS policy remain in the shared action runner. Consent generations, connection/account revisions, connectivity, Wi-Fi and external power are rechecked under the repository lease and before mutation. A failed upload retains its new local commit, and the next pass can publish it without another commit.
- WorkManager owns a unique 15-minute periodic request with a 15-minute initial delay and connected-network constraint. Android controls actual execution timing; the screen explains delay/system restrictions and force-stop behavior. App startup/catalog refresh restore scheduling; foreground resume and Run now enqueue one unique on-demand check. The production Hilt worker awaits installed storage/credential guards and serializes background passes. Stop cancels current/on-demand and periodic work, then reschedules a future periodic check; changing policy/consent invalidates old work before rescheduling. Wi-Fi and charging checks are per repository, so an excluded/deferred checkout cannot authorize another operation.
- Added bounded, checked, backup-excluded private policy/health storage with no credential values. Health records distinguish never/current/updated/waiting/changed/unavailable/attention/failure/stopped, preserve last-success history across deferral and advance last-transfer only for actual data movement. Native controls support reviewed re-inclusion, exclusion, saved-policy removal, opening the repository, live health refresh, confirmed corrupt-state reset and cancellation on backgrounding of unfinished reviews. Reset is bound to the exact corrupt value **and its type**, refuses healthy/changed data, disables both modes and keeps repository/account/other feature data.
- Core verification: **432 tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips. Ten new cases use real disposable Git repositories to cover defaults/exclusion, pull-only fast-forward without publishing drafts, push-only and retry idempotence, incoming/dirty preservation, divergence, branch/credential/catalog/account changes, network/power deferral, queued opt-out and local-commit retention after an interrupted upload. The queued revocation check waits for a captured configuration instead of sleeping. Final product debug/test/release assembly and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-background-account-build.log`, 1 minute 54 seconds); the last additional commit-retention case is test-only and passes in `/tmp/syncmd-background-retained-commit-core.log`.
- Focused native verification: **eight tests pass**, 20.285 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-background-native.log`). Seven isolated namespace cases cover persistence/defaults, stale credential/account enrollment, actual worker pull/push/disabled behavior, conditions/exclusion, type-aware corrupt reset, native Cancel/Allow publishing, reviewed Wi-Fi/power inclusion, Run now callbacks, exclusion, and dark 160% recovery. One additional test uses the **actual WorkManager database/scheduler and production Hilt worker**: its periodic request has the expected interval/constraint, one on-demand job creates and acknowledges exactly one commit in an owned local bare remote, health is updated, opt-out cancels future work and a new unconsented draft stays private. A no-credentials policy and local author prevent use of any saved GitHub identity. Its temporary global catalog/policy snapshots, owned credentials/workspaces and scheduled work are restored/removed after the worker releases its lease. System font scale and storage/notification permissions are not changed. No live GitHub commit/push, cloud change, release installation or publication is part of this phase.

Three lifecycle cases cover dispatch, cancellation and destroyed/replaced entries. No exception suppression or dependency upgrade is used.

No earlier preference values were invented. The encrypted account store and unrelated working-copy files were not targeted by that fixture. The subsequent baseline protects the post-cleanup state; it cannot recover the earlier lost RAM snapshot.
