The safer recovery helper stores typed maps under AES-GCM with an Android Keystore key **inside the debug app's private no-backup directory**. Two synthetic tests cover cross-instance round-trip and corrupt-ciphertext rejection. Capture/restore/audit/one-off repair are maintenance operations and do not add functional coverage. `tools/testing/run_native_regression.py` enforces the dedicated AVD, automatic-sync-off preconditions, scoped process stops and restoration in a finally block; preference values and journal bytes are never exported.

### Pull upstream identity and local-content protection (2026-10-28)

- Reproduced five real-Git failures in `SafePullIntegrationTest` before changing production code (`/tmp/syncmd-safe-pull-red-confirmed.log`): planning fetched origin despite a configured upstream/release; a deleted remote branch left an apparently executable stale-tracking plan; detached HEAD was accepted by planning; a branch switched during credential resolution could receive the incoming fast-forward; and a clean Git status allowed an ignored local file to be overwritten. An initial fixture error creating its exclude file was corrected before the authoritative red run; it is not counted as product evidence.
- `LocalPullRepository` now resolves the exact local branch and its single configured remote upstream. Planning and execution share a snapshot of full branch ref, local commit, configured remote branch/tracking mapping, config SHA-256, working-folder/Git-metadata directory identities and connection revision. The Android binding combines encrypted credential-record revision with verified repository account ownership. Snapshot checks occur before/after fetch and immediately before checkout; detached/unborn branches, active Git operations, missing/ambiguous upstreams and changed identities fail without a working-tree update. These checks cover the observed fetch/authentication window; they do not claim an atomic lock against every external tool throughout checkout.
- Fetch targets only the configured upstream branch and safe remote-tracking destination, without implicit tag fetching. Fresh advertised OID must match the fetched tracking ref; a cached ref cannot stand in for a deleted upstream. Standalone Fetch follows the configured remote as well. Pull computes fresh ancestry and includes the exact fetched OID in an FF_ONLY merge, never creates an implicit merge/rebase/commit, and reports applied commit count with mergeRequired=false. Already-ahead/up-to-date local branches remain unchanged. Cancellation propagates rather than becoming an ordinary connection error. Existing post-update LFS hydration/attention behavior is retained.
- Before fast-forward, inspection protects both ordinary untracked and ignored files, including directory/file prefix collisions and nested ignored contents. Planning marks an ignored-content collision blocked; execution rechecks clean status and identity. Unrelated ignored files remain allowed. Pull access requires the exact working-copy root with its own unlinked metadata; parent-repository discovery and shared/external metadata cannot bypass the repository lease through a subdirectory.
- **440 core tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips, including eight new real-Git cases. They cover custom remote/different upstream name, deleted upstream and exact HEAD/index preservation, branch switch during authentication, detached/active-merge rejection, ignored file and nested-directory collision, unrelated ignored content and explicit retry, local commit/config/credential revision changes, and subdirectory rejection. Product debug/test/release assembly and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-safe-pull-build.log`, 1 minute 49 seconds). Three additional Android filesystem fixtures are built in `/tmp/syncmd-safe-pull-native-build.log`; final native evidence follows when complete.
- Explicit reviewed merging of a remote branch remains open, as do stale-result guards in the pull ViewModel, wider SSH/LFS/provider transport replay, external-tool concurrency, older Android/device and release accessibility/performance qualification. No language coverage, live account, cloud relay or store state changes in this phase. Full parity remains active.

- Final current-product native regression: **118 distinct checks pass**, 245.228 seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-wtqr1t5c/regression.log`). This includes all three new `SafePullDeviceTest` methods on API 35: configured upstream/release while origin is unavailable; exact local ignored-content/HEAD/index preservation and explicit successful retry; and a branch switch during credential resolution without updating either local branch. These invoke the production JGit repository implementation on real Android storage with synthetic no-credentials fixtures; they do not independently exercise a new review screen or live SSH/LFS server. Existing 115 native checks also pass on the updated product. Capture/restore/audit maintenance calls add no distinct functional coverage.
- Private baseline restoration succeeds and removes its journal. Final audit finds zero owned safe-pull workspaces, zero owned callback fixtures and zero pending private recovery files (`/tmp/syncmd-safe-pull-final-audit.log`). The catalog remains the post-incident baseline of zero entries; this does not reconstruct the earlier lost RAM snapshot. Font scale stays 1.0, storage access remains default and notifications remain denied with unchanged sensitivity flags. All build/install/instrumentation handles are terminal, the debug app is stopped and scoped Argent cleanup reports no live helpers. Source/APK/test-log hashes are in `/tmp/syncmd-safe-pull-proof.json`. No real remote mutation, cloud change, release installation or publication occurred. Full parity remains active and incomplete.


### Reviewed remote merging, publishing and production conflict editing (2026-10-28)

- Added the production Git → Merge with remote route using the configured upstream (including differently named remote branches supported by the shared pull engine). Review fetches first and shows local/incoming IDs and counts, the exact local/upstream branch names, redacted fetch/push addresses and the resolved merge author. Explicit native confirmations offer **Merge locally** or **Merge and push**. Local-only merging never publishes; merge-and-push uses the existing exact-ref, acknowledged, non-force push path without publishing tags. An already-ahead branch can explicitly publish without another merge/commit.
- `RepositoryRemoteMerge` shares the pull engine's exact-root, metadata/folder identity, branch/HEAD/config/credential/account snapshot and ignored/untracked protection. Begin refetches the exact upstream and rejects a changed advertised OID; it revalidates the author and clean/ignored-content state immediately before checkout. A new deterministic red case caught edits arriving during asynchronous author resolution; the final check preserves those edits and refuses checkout. The local merge and its immediate LFS finalization are protected from cancellation. Publishing then runs sequentially under the original repository lease; it does not inherit a child/noncancellable job that would violate the coordinator's ownership guard.
- Divergence creates a normal two-parent merge using the reviewed identity, with no history rewriting. Conflicts remain Git-owned on disk, reload on ViewModel reconstruction, block publishing, and lead back to the existing Git conflict center. No automatic push is resumed after conflict completion. Failed or unconfirmed publication reports the saved local result and offers **Retry push of saved commit**; retry pins the same HEAD/config/folder/connection and never merges or recommits. Changed credentials during push preparation stop publishing while retaining the merged commit. LFS checkout attention is retained separately from successful Git work.
- `RemoteMergeViewModel` gates duplicate mutations before launch, ties confirmation to the reviewed plan object, suppresses stale path/request results and cancels abandoned read-only reviews. Returning from background or recreation invalidates an unsubmitted review rather than authorizing stale input; in-flight local mutations finish safely. The native Material screen supports scrolling, both themes, growing labels, meaningful errors and return to Git. All new UI labels are resources; language coverage remains partial (831 base keys, 157 translated per non-English locale, 674 fallback). The existing inline Git inputs now have accessible labels, including merge commit message; shared account-binding errors refer to the repository rather than incorrectly mentioning shortcuts in other workflows.
- **451 core tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips, including 11 new real-Git merge cases. Coverage includes review without checkout, exact merge parents/author/config preservation, local-only versus acknowledged publishing, tag isolation, persistent conflict/manual completion, changed incoming HEAD/index preservation, local HEAD/config/credential/author staleness, late author-resolution edits, ahead-only publishing, changed push credentials, unconfirmed push retention and retry of the exact same commit. Product debug/test/release assembly and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-remote-merge-dialog-fix-build.log`); the final app-only message build also passes (`/tmp/syncmd-remote-merge-message-build.log`). Seven localization tooling tests and both generated-output drift checks pass.
- Device feedback exposed a pre-existing **production conflict editor crash**, first seen as a secondary Compose layout-state failure. An isolated test of the exact dialog/body nesting reproduced the original **infinite maximum height** exception (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-1jnaqoui/regression.log`). File text had its own vertical scroll inside an already scrolling diff panel. Removing that inner scroll lets the bounded panel own scrolling and preserves complete file content. No dependency upgrade, exception suppression or omitted editor interaction is used. Test waits observe actual loaded conflict controls, and whole-file assertions include their original newline. Initial dialog-node image copying could still capture the underlying activity; the final test-only capture helper waits for the focused app window to commit a frame and takes an actual device screenshot instead.
- The actual production route now passes **review → merge-and-push confirmation → persistent conflict → Return to Git → Manual → edit complete ours contents → resolve/stage → enter merge message → complete two-parent merge**, with exact combined file contents, clean Git state and unchanged remote (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-xreh9lwa/regression.log`, 3.936 seconds). The explicit publication request stops at conflicts; completion does not silently publish later.
- The crashed navigation fixture's original preference baseline was successfully restored from the private encrypted journal. A guarded retry removed only the identified UUID-scoped interrupted working copy after checking its directory/metadata ownership and local bare remote. This is the protected post-incident baseline established earlier; it does not recover the older lost RAM snapshot. `--cleanup-navigation-id` accepts only a canonical UUID and one explicit FileNavigation method, and native guards refuse symlink/parent escapes. No preference or journal bytes were exported. Final broad native evidence follows when complete.

### Repository-bound pull/conflict state and callbacks (2026-10-28)

- First reproduced **six failing native regressions** against the previous implementation (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-0n069jmv/regression.log`). Controlled repository responses exposed old pull/conflict results replacing a newer repository, an older refresh clearing the latest busy flag, two simultaneous fetch submissions, an old mutation error leaking to the new repository, and an old conflict editor/draft surviving a path change. These use barriers and coroutine completion, rather than timing sleeps, with owned real Git fixture paths and synthetic delayed service responses. No catalog/account/policy preferences are replaced by these tests.
- Added an immutable `RepositorySelection` identity. Every switch creates a new object, including A → B → A, so callbacks from an earlier visit cannot regain authority merely because the path string matches again. Pull/fetch/planning and conflict edit/resolve/complete/abort callbacks explicitly receive their producing selection. Required parameters caught the second pull entry point in Vault; both Vault and Git now bind callbacks and filter displayed status/errors/attention to the current path. Error/attention acknowledgement is selection-bound as well.
- Pull reads use cancellable jobs plus a monotonically increasing request ID, and publish only for the matching current selection/request. Busy state is set before launch and only the current request may clear it. Pull/fetch mutations are synchronously gated, capture their original path, recheck the selection after acquiring its repository lease, and refresh status inside that same lease. Queued stale work is abandoned before entering the backend; work already inside the backend stays on its original path. A later selection loads fresh data after the existing mutation settles; old errors/attention cannot overwrite it.
- Conflict merge state and conflict rows are read and published as one coherent snapshot under the repository lease. Loading or failed snapshots disable destructive controls; stale rows and editor callbacks cannot act on a different selection. Path changes immediately clear the editor, draft, resolved markers and cached merge state. Same-selection refresh defers while an editor is open; resolution compares the expected conflict with the current backend conflict under the lease, preserves the visible draft on rejection, and explains how to copy it before explicitly closing/reviewing again. This comparison covers the returned conflict versions, not a complete external-filesystem compare-and-swap transaction.
- Conflict mutation gates prevent duplicate resolution/complete/abort submissions. Draft contents are captured before launch, so an in-flight operation cannot pick up a newly selected repository's draft. Completion/abort recheck an active matching merge session before invoking the backend; batch resolution rejects an incomplete returned result instead of claiming all conflicts resolved. Refreshes remove resolved markers for paths that become conflicting again. Operation notices survive queued refreshes until acknowledged; a stale dialog cannot clear another selection's notice. LFS partial-success notices remain supported.
- Rendered feedback: Vault retains only its own cached status while refreshing, shows progress and disables Pull, and provides a native Dismiss action for errors without retrying Git work. Git conflict rows/complete/abort controls follow snapshot readiness and busy state, while the editor field becomes semantically disabled during mutations. Conflict errors remain visible inside the editor without a second overlapping error dialog. A rendered readiness marker lets the production navigation regression wait for actual loaded merge state before finding controls; none of its merge/edit/stage/commit checks were removed.
- **Twelve native state-machine checks pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-3kgxkbh0/regression.log`). Besides the six original failures, they cover round-trip selection tokens, duplicate manual resolution using the captured original draft, queued pull/conflict work behind a lease, changed conflict rejection with draft retention, failed refresh disabling cached destructive actions, and queued refresh/error acknowledgement ownership. Two rendered checks pass for disabled vault Pull/error Dismiss and disabled/re-enabled editor semantics. The full actual remote merge → manual edit → stage → complete path passes after waiting for the coherent merge snapshot (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-t5sx67nd/regression.log`, 10.53 seconds).
- Existing **451 core tests** remain passing with zero failures/errors/skips; this phase changes app ViewModels/UI and tests, not core services. Debug/test/release assembly and release vital lint pass (`/tmp/syncmd-viewmodel-final-build.log`, `/tmp/syncmd-viewmodel-editor-build.log`, `/tmp/syncmd-viewmodel-snapshot-ready-build.log` and final message build `/tmp/syncmd-viewmodel-final-message-build.log`). The final broad current-product native result and cleanup follow when complete.
- Remaining scope includes history/GitHub/settings/clone and other asynchronous flows, stronger external Git/index/worktree session revision binding, wider storage/transport/interrupted-process cases and full accessibility/release/motion qualification. Selection tokens are UI request ownership, not a replacement for backend Git/storage/credential checks. Full parity remains active.

- The first broad run completed **141 checks with one failure** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-e0lpcbck/regression.log`): a fast Commit & Push tap was silently dropped while its source destination was STARTED during a transition. Deterministic lifecycle cases then reproduced dropped forward/Back requests and duplicate queued requests failing to reach their destination (three failures in seven cases, `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-opz0107k/regression.log`). Forward/Back helpers now queue against the producing back-stack entry, wait for it to resume, and leave the lifecycle dispatch turn before navigation. A synchronous destination listener cancels the queued request if that source is left, even when it returns within the same main-loop turn; entry destruction also cancels it. Duplicate taps cannot navigate again after the first leaves their source. No exception suppression or dependency upgrade is used.
- Debug/test/release assembly and vital release lint pass with this navigation fix (`/tmp/syncmd-viewmodel-navigation-fix-build.log`). All **nine focused checks pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-96qae2rm/regression.log`): seven lifecycle cases plus the production partial-diff/commit/push-retry path and reviewed merge/manual-edit/stage/completion path. The protected baseline was restored and its journal removed. Final full-suite results follow below.

- The first completed run with the navigation fix passed **144 of 145 checks**, with one callback-screen test reading delivery counters as soon as DONE was published (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-cd63tr70/regression.log`). The synchronous opted-in return follows that state update in the same main-thread coroutine. The test now waits for returned state and reads counters after the main thread is idle; exactly-once/no-retry assertions remain. This is a test-only correction (`/tmp/syncmd-viewmodel-callback-wait-build.log`); product code is unchanged. Private baseline restoration succeeded.

- Final broad current-product regression: **145 distinct native checks pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-_mgtnuwp/regression.log`), including the twelve new repository-state cases, Vault feedback case, seven lifecycle cases and both production Git workflows. All ten callback-screen cases also pass separately (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-0og9td4a/regression.log`); reruns add no distinct coverage. This is one normal-size dark fixture, not all-screen accessibility or release-motion acceptance. ### History ownership, stash messages and annotated tags (2026-10-29)

- History now exposes one UI state containing selection, inventories, selected commit, readiness, busy state, error and operation revision. Commits/stashes/tags form one backend snapshot under a repository lease. Cancellable latest-read IDs prevent older results or busy completions from replacing newer state. Repository changes clear lists/editor/notices immediately, including A → B → A; failed snapshots disable cached actions. Six regressions first failed against the previous implementation (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-xojvnzfy/regression.log`) and pass after the rewrite (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-qlcidx9v/regression.log`).
- Every mutation receives its producing selection and selected row. Gates are set before launch; queued work rechecks ownership after acquiring the lease; started work retains its original path and arguments. Fresh snapshots compare the reviewed HEAD, stash position plus commit, and tag metadata plus exact ref object ID. Default HEAD tag creation uses the reviewed full commit hash. Notices survive refresh and require their selection to acknowledge them; failed submissions retain form drafts, and success clears only unchanged input belonging to that visit. Operation revisions refresh status/diffs/branches/conflicts after success or partial failure. These are application-owned checks, not complete transactions against external branch/config/index/worktree writers.
- Connected the stash message input to Git and added the iOS optional annotation field to the tag form. Nonempty annotation creates an annotated tag; empty annotation remains lightweight. Inputs and row actions disable during history work. The annotation matches the current iOS catalog and imports into all 25 non-English locales: **158 translated keys of 832 base keys**, **674 English fallback keys**, and three untranslated plural resources. Seven localization tool tests and both generated-output checks pass.
- A real-Git red test proved malformed stash identifiers could silently delete index zero (`/tmp/syncmd-history-identity-red.log`). Apply/pop/drop now require canonical, in-range `stash@{n}`. Apply uses the captured immutable commit ID; pop verifies the slot still matches before removing it, otherwise retaining the stash and reporting that changes were applied. **454 core tests pass**, including malformed-drop preservation, older-stash pop retaining newest, and exact annotated/light tag-object metadata (`/tmp/syncmd-history-core.log`). An external writer racing the final reflog update still needs stronger transaction qualification.
- **Thirteen native state tests pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-m7cg_asu/regression.log`): the original six failures plus round-trip selection callbacks, lease-queued cancellation, shifted stash indices, replaced annotated objects with unchanged peeled targets, failed-snapshot gating, captured annotation/HEAD and exactly-once completion, and changed-HEAD draft retention. The production workflow saves/reapplies a full stash, checks its message, creates the exact annotation/target, publishes only that tag, and verifies the other local tag and remote branch remain unpublished.
- Initial production waits timed out on a disabled **offscreen** cached item. Failure-only model inspection proved ready=true, busy=false, no error and zero active reads; a committed screenshot and visibility assertion proved expanded branch/change cards had moved Stash out of view (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-ifekex2s/regression.log`, `/tmp/history-loading-failure.png`). Parent state-holder and single-state changes alone did not resolve that test assumption. Final tests wait for the actual loaded page, scroll the target into view again, then assert displayed/enabled before action. The controlled case still proves visible disabled input before releasing the lease, then visible enabled input after loading. No Git-content, exactly-once or publication assertion was removed. Temporary tracing, reflection diagnostics and state probe tags were removed.
- Full accessibility/release motion, wider storage/transport/device/cold-process qualification, remaining branch/GitHub/settings/clone asynchronous flows and every other unfinished parity category remain active. No cloud/live-remote/store change or release installation is part of this phase.

- Final focused regression: **15 checks pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-fg5ds5il/regression.log`), comprising thirteen history-state cases and both production loading/workflow cases. Visible controls re-enable after loaded-page synchronization; the original stash message/content, exact annotation/target, selected-tag-only publishing, unchanged remote branch and unpublished other tag checks remain intact. Private baseline restoration succeeds and removes its journal. Debug/test/release assembly and vital release lint pass on the final clean source (`/tmp/syncmd-history-ready-final-build.log`). The final broad result and audit are recorded below.

- Final broad current-product regression: **160 distinct native checks pass**, 302.392 instrumentation seconds (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-afouhfk9/regression.log`), including all 23 production navigation cases, thirteen new history-state cases, localization and prior repository/push/background/callback coverage. This adds 15 distinct checks to the previous 145; focused reruns add no distinct coverage. The private baseline restores successfully and its encrypted journal is removed. All build/install/test handles are terminal.

It is a normal-size fixture, not proof of all locales/large text/landscape/screen readers/release frame rate. ### Repository-bound branch reviews and actions (2026-10-29)

- Seven real-Git native regressions failed against the previous branch ViewModel (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-0od52zmy/regression.log`): a refresh dropped while an older read ran; a queued switch writing after its selection was left; branch creation using a changed HEAD or another current branch at the same commit; merge using a moved source; writes using cached rows after failed refresh; and a completed-checkout/LFS warning overwritten by a following inventory failure. Both preexisting branch checks passed in that red run. All nine pass after the rewrite (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-gzz_qtij/regression.log`).
- Branches now expose one UI state and a new selection identity on every repository change, including round trips. A coherent snapshot reads current branch, inventory and current branch again under one canonical-path lease, rejecting a mixed snapshot if the current branch moved. Latest reads cancel/invalidate older requests instead of discarding the later refresh. Only the producing selection/request may publish state or clear loading. Failed snapshots disable cached actions.
- All create/switch/merge/delete callbacks and error acknowledgements are selection-bound. Mutation gates are set before launch; queued work checks selection again after acquiring the lease. Started work keeps its original path and captured arguments. Before writing, a fresh snapshot checks the reviewed current branch and full HEAD; switch/merge/delete also check the selected local row and target commit. Creation uses the captured full commit ID rather than resolving HEAD later. Successful creation clears only unchanged input from the same visit; failed attempts retain it. Inputs/row actions disable while busy, and stale cached head badges are hidden when the snapshot is unavailable.
- Full commit IDs now replace abbreviated IDs in current/local/remote branch metadata, and detached HEAD retains its resolved full object ID. Two real-Git identity checks failed first (`/tmp/syncmd-branch-identity-red.log`); the full **456-test core suite passes** with the corrected metadata (`/tmp/syncmd-branch-state-build.log`). These snapshots and leases protect app operations; external writers racing the final checkout/ref/merge or changing storage/config/credentials need stronger transactional qualification.

### Ignored-file checkout protection and reviewed merge results (2026-10-29)

- Eight of fourteen initial real-Git checks failed before this change (`/tmp/syncmd-checkout-collision-red.log`): ignored file overwrites, dirty merge acceptance, author-resolution branch/HEAD races, and rejection of a safe three-way result. Existing directory/prefix collision checks already refused unsafe writes; those cases were retained. The current iOS service blocks dirty switches/merges and uses safe checkout, which informed the Android guard.
- Shared checkout protection compares ignored/untracked paths against prospective checkout paths. Divergent merges calculate an in-memory recursive merge index, including conflict stages, so an incoming unchanged file deleted by ours does not unnecessarily block a retained ignored replacement. Up-to-date and rejected fast-forward-only merges avoid speculative checkout. Preflight preserves the actual index, working files and refs; JGit may cache computed Git objects.
- Local switch/merge require a clean SAFE repository. Around author resolution and before mutation they recheck current branch/full HEAD, source ref, index/config digests and working/Git-folder identities. This rejects stale app review while preserving externally changed content. The guard does not claim a complete filesystem/ref transaction against arbitrary writers after the final check. Collision dialogs identify the relative path; typed diagnostics do not persist exception text or file paths.
- All **22 new real-Git cases pass**, including late ignored content, index/config/folder/source-ref changes, active merge preservation, safe deletion/no-op merges and legitimate conflict stages. The full **478 core tests pass**, as do debug/test/release assembly and vital release lint (`/tmp/syncmd-checkout-final-build.log`).
- All **five focused Android checks pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-hla8f520/regression.log`): four device cases across private and owned Android/media storage, plus the actual Git switch/merge collision dialog → browser rename → successful switch/merge recovery workflow. Exact original private contents, index/HEAD and remote refs are asserted. No live remote, cloud change, release installation or store publication is part of this phase. Full parity remains incomplete.

### Real status-card commit and completion metadata (2026-10-29)

- Replaced the Git status card's fixed SHA dash and “just now” with actual data. `RepositoryStatus` carries the full resolved HEAD and an optional recorded completion timestamp. Status scans verify branch/HEAD again after reading working/index changes; mixed reads fail for refresh. The UI shows seven SHA characters, an explicit unborn “No commits” state, a locale-formatted date/time, and “Not recorded” when history is unavailable. Weighted status columns wrap longer values rather than pushing them outside the card.
- `RepositoryActivity` stores a bounded versioned non-secret timestamp in the actual Git directory. Fsynced temporary writes publish by atomic replacement and clean up on failure; unsupported atomic replacement retains the prior record. Reads reject malformed, oversized, nonregular and symlink records. Existing unrecognized records are preserved. This metadata follows relocation and survives service/process reopen without relying on preferences or commit author dates. Failure to save optional display metadata cannot turn a completed Git transaction into a reported failure.
- The shared repository lease records typed confirmed completion of clone, commit, pull, acknowledged push, branch, merge, completed rebase and revert operations. Reads, fetch/planning, returned failures, unsuccessful merges, paused rebases and unconfirmed push retries do not advance it. A completed local commit/merge retains its own time when separate publication fails. No preexisting sync history is fabricated; direct external Git commits update displayed HEAD on refresh without pretending the app synced. This covers the shared Git operation paths; publication-specific direct Git paths and repository-list/vault presentation need further qualification.

### Live repository invalidation and saved vault identity (2026-10-29)

- Added process-local `RepositoryUpdates`, separate from persisted completion time. Observers register before their initial read so a write during that read queues another refresh. Each canonical working root has only its current subscriber channels; each channel conflates pending signals, and cancellation removes empty root entries. Alias paths share signals, unrelated roots do not, and no path or work is retained without subscribers. No timer, remote fetch, credential read or analytics export is added.
- The operation coordinator invalidates diagnosed operations in finally, including returned/thrown failures and cancellation, while keeping the existing success-only timestamp rules. Publication finish and pending-guard clearing also invalidate their root. Observers read through the repository lease, so queued refreshes see completed app transactions. This lets a completed local mutation update displayed HEAD even if optional timestamp persistence failed, without inventing a successful sync time.
- Repository-card subscriptions remain lifecycle-owned and now refresh on live signals. Vault/Git observe only while RESUMED and refresh their existing selection-owned ViewModels; pending mutation/editor safeguards remain intact. Initial path binding during navigation remains explicit. Ordinary reads have no diagnostic mutation label and do not generate a refresh loop. External tools do not emit this process-local signal; their changes still refresh on resume. Cross-process storage/device/performance qualification remains open.
- Vault headings now use the selected saved repository's alias/name, with a folder-name fallback only when no saved identity is available. List and vault share one display-identity helper. Owners derive from saved HTTPS/SSH/SCP remote namespaces or supported legacy identifiers, never a private filesystem directory. User info and query tokens are not part of the displayed owner. The existing list behavior of preferring a nonblank alias is retained.

### Reviewed credential-bearing URL migration and guarded transport (2026-10-29)

- Added a production **Credentials in remote URLs** review in Repository connection. Review rows show cleaned addresses and fetch/push roles without extracted usernames/tokens. Supported repository-local HTTPS username/password pairs move into verified Android Keystore-backed encrypted storage before checked Git config publication. Every remote URL list/order and separate fetch/push endpoint is retained; index, HEAD/refs and working contents stay in place. A normal connection edit remains a separate action with its existing fetch/push address review.
- Core planning exposes only cleaned addresses, config revision and metadata identity. Save rereads under the repository lease and Git config lock, verifies revision/metadata again at publication, fsyncs the sanitized prepared config and never creates a plaintext credential backup. Credentials are percent-decoded strictly (literal plus retained); repository paths retain escaped slashes. Config reads are bounded at 8 MiB. Incomplete/conflicting credentials, insecure/query/fragment URLs, password-bearing SSH, includes and inherited remote URL values require explicit manual review; no auth scheme or missing token is guessed. Existing Git locks and external config changes are preserved.
- Repository credentials now support multiple exact endpoint-bound policies in the existing encrypted preference record. Existing secure credentials/account references win at their endpoint. Differing incoming URL credentials are retained as an encrypted inactive alternative; an explicit editor selection swaps the active and alternative policies without copying account tokens. A third differing alternative blocks migration before config publication rather than discarding a credential. Public-access policies retain recovered credentials encrypted and inactive; keeping public access preserves them, and selecting a saved endpoint can reactivate that endpoint without enabling other inactive bindings. Explicit public access/removal clears stored bindings. Origin edits retain secondary bindings. Whole-policy relocation re-encrypts for destination/retained paths with existing AAD checks; no token is copied into Git config, navigation or review state. Migrated usernames remain hidden because a URL username may itself be a token.
- The connection ViewModel captures migration/config/credential revisions, gates duplicate submissions, and rejects queued writes after its selection generation changes. Existing corrupt-record repair through explicit new/public credentials remains available. Migration and normal multi-endpoint edits prepare one complete encrypted record in memory before its preferences commit; failed config publication or preference persistence restores the exact prior encrypted record. A process crash between credential persistence and config publication can leave a checked retry/manual Git-lock recovery requirement; no fully journaled cross-store crash transaction is claimed.
- Transport validation now rejects embedded credentials and unsupported/malformed addresses before calling a credential resolver, including directly constructed scoped providers. This closes the path where a null HTTPS endpoint could omit the HTTP request guard. Core coverage retains valid HTTPS/SSH/local fixture behavior and the no-token-read invariant. The earlier anonymous-provider expectation for HTTP now asserts rejection before token lookup. Discovery redaction uses URI authority parsing before JGit, handles uppercase schemes and password-bearing unsupported userinfo, and preserves `%2F` paths; regression tests caught both the uppercase and unsupported-SSH redaction cases before correction.

### Logical rename conflicts and reviewed filename selection (2026-10-29)

- The iOS conflict model keeps ancestor/ours/theirs paths and its editor chooses the final filename. Android previously treated each unmerged index path independently. A native-Git fixture confirms rename/rename uses stage 1 at the old name and stages 2/3 at the two new names. A separate probe of the app's cached JGit 5.13.5 engine merged that history successfully while retaining both new names (`/tmp/syncmd-rename-engine-probe.log`); fixing only the editor would leave app-created merges incorrect.
- Added graph-verified logical grouping for persisted single-base merge sessions. The model retains each side's path, object ID and file mode, complete guarded text, binary/large-file status and working-file revisions. Any involved path retrieves the same logical conflict. Duplicate ancestor objects and unsupported/multiple-base provenance do not receive a guessed grouping. Ordinary conflict reads skip rename graph work when the index has both sides at its existing path.
- Added rename-aware recursive merge handling for local branch and reviewed remote merges, including their in-core checkout-protection preview. Split rename stages are inserted before JGit publishes its existing locked checkout/index transaction, so the branch cannot silently commit both names. Cross-side tracked-name overlap and ambiguous duplicate ancestry require manual review before the normal tree walk touches files. Recursive virtual-base behavior and replay integration still need dedicated qualification.
- The production Git conflict row shows both side names. The editor offers semantic radio controls for the reviewed names while retaining the text draft; selection belongs to the existing repository/editor context and is captured for submission. Whole-side actions keep that side's name and raw bytes. Manual resolution stages only the chosen name. Exact related paths are cleared from the index, unrelated staged/untracked paths are retained, and obsolete working filenames are removed after successful staging. Late external changes or removal failures leave the extra file in place with a notice. Link parents/targets and unreviewed names are refused; index locks and stale objects/working revisions are checked.
- This is not a journaled multi-file crash transaction. A write/staging failure may leave a prepared working file with the original conflict index; a crash or removal failure after index publication may retain extra untracked names. Rebase/cherry-pick provenance, rename/modify/delete, overlapping renames, symbolic-link/submodule resolution, very large files and broader provider/storage/device/release/accessibility qualification remain open. The full parity goal remains active.
- Initial core grouping/resolution checks passed. The first focused native run passed 22 of 23 cases (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-lwzpf3y8/regression.log`), including binary resolution and large-text light/dark components; the actual manual editor test stopped before opening the editor because the vault's lazy list had not composed Commit & Push. Its helper now waits for loaded vault state and scrolls the lazy list to that action before the controlled callback. Final build, runtime and cleanup evidence follows below.

### Single-sided rename semantics and ordinary conflict write safeguards (2026-10-29)

- Confirmed the baseline engine discrepancies with failing owned fixtures before correction: rename versus deletion silently produced a merge commit, and renamed content edits could conflict at the old path. Ordinary manual conflict writes could follow a replaced file or parent link to another working file. The confirmed red run is `/tmp/syncmd-rename-delete-confirmed-red.log`; rename/edit characterization is `/tmp/syncmd-rename-modify-red.log`. One ignored-file fixture initially lacked its `.git/info` parent; its setup was corrected before using the protection result as evidence.
- Rename/delete now produces stage 1 and the surviving side at the detected new name, on either branch. The merge stays uncommitted and reviewed remote publication stays blocked. Choosing the surviving side retains its exact bytes; choosing the deleted side removes the file. The production conflict row and content panels explain which side deleted it. Binary deletion conflicts do not enable an empty manual editor.
- Detected rename/edit paths and equal rename destinations are merged from the actual ancestor and side blobs. Independent edits combine at the new name; overlapping edits become one complete content conflict there. Unchanged-side shortcuts preserve exact binary bytes, and file-mode changes combine with content changes. The merger retains JGit's existing index lock, checkout metadata, filters and dirty-index/working-file checks. Old tracked names are deleted through the same checkout transaction; unrelated or ignored destination contents are protected by the prospective index review. Ambiguous/overlapping paths and file/directory prefix renames still require manual tools.
- Ordinary conflict mutations now reject existing link targets and parents before mutation. Manual text publication uses bounded strict UTF-8 encoding, a synced sibling temporary, preserved available permissions, a fresh working revision/path check and atomic replacement where supported. This closes the reproduced pre-existing-link writes; no fully atomic external-writer or index/worktree crash transaction is claimed.

### Rename-aware replay and rebase author boundaries (2026-10-29)

- Five owned real-Git replay tests initially failed (`/tmp/syncmd-rebase-rename-red.log`): independent rename edits paused unnecessarily, overlapping edits conflicted at the old filename, and split renames/rename-delete histories could complete without the required conflict. Rebase begin and advance now pass the rename-aware strategy through JGit's existing merge-preserving replay. Existing topology, explicit empty-commit skip and offline abort behavior remain covered.
- Persisted replay conflict grouping uses the picked commit's exact parent. JGit clears `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` on a paused rebase and writes an abbreviated `rebase-merge/stopped-sha`; grouping reads that bounded, nonlinked file, requires a unique commit object and verifies every side path/blob/mode against its trees. A later picked commit after an earlier commit was rewritten is explicitly tested. A single-parent persisted cherry pick can use `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`; multi-parent picks and unsupported replay metadata receive no guessed pairing. This extends the conflict reader, not a new production cherry-pick launcher.
- The production rebase editor now shows upstream/local filenames and reuses the shared semantic radio selector. Text and chosen filename remain bound to the selected conflict and survive a stale-write refusal. Manual resolution submits the chosen name through the existing all-path staging/cleanup service, while the rebase Continue/Skip/Abort controls retain ownership of replay completion. Missing versions show deletion explanations in the editor.

### Recursive merge bases and blocked replay recovery (2026-10-29)

- Clean criss-cross histories now use JGit's active recursive merge walk when reading synthesized base commits. Reopening a separate walk incorrectly looked for a virtual commit in the object database and failed. Persisted split-rename conflicts reconstruct the effective base tree and still require exact path, object and mode matches before grouping. Four real two-base core fixtures cover clean rename/edit merges in either orientation, persisted logical conflict resolution, checkout previews and reviewed local-bare remote completion.
- Blocked checkout paths are captured from each replay merger, including Continue/Skip steps where JGit drops them from its final result. Paused checkout failures persist a bounded, explicit JSON journal under the existing rebase metadata. The production screen shows the paths, disables Continue, and offers **Retry blocked commit** only while the detached HEAD, index, replay plan and progress remain unchanged. Retry requeues the exact original pick once and runs the guarded reset/replay path; it preserves previous manual resolutions and does not publish an artificial retry commit. Malformed, linked, locked, stale or interrupted-publication metadata requires review rather than an automatic retry.
- Core coverage includes ignored intermediate files, directory prefixes and linked parents; previous manual conflict resolution; idempotent queued retry; changed Git progress, index, HEAD or folder identity; and a blocked regular pick after an earlier preserved two-parent merge. That last case does not qualify a blocked two-parent pick itself. Interruption after replay has advanced HEAD still requires manual review. Conflicting/renamed recursive ancestors and preserved-merge branch-switch checkout paths remain qualification work.

### Conflicting virtual bases and preserved-parent checkout protection (2026-10-29)

- A new real criss-cross fixture with conflicting common-ancestor text reproduced a persisted grouping bug: the original merge's virtual stage-one blob used outer `HEAD` / incoming-commit labels, while reconstruction used `OURS` / `THEIRS`. Their object IDs differed, so exact provenance checks correctly refused to combine the three paths. Virtual-base generation and reconstruction now share stable internal ancestor labels, restoring the outer command's labels afterward. Core checks cover clean independent rename edits in both orientations, reopened split-rename grouping/manual completion with exact parents, and ordinary final content conflicts retaining their `HEAD` labels. Renamed recursive ancestors and external virtual bases created with different label conventions remain qualification work.
- A separate preserved-merge fixture reproduced actual ignored-data loss: after a conflict on the second parent, JGit privately checked out the already rewritten first parent, overwrote an ignored file, and a later replayed deletion removed it. This checkout bypasses the configured merger. `RebaseParentCheckouts` now reviews future preserved-merge first-parent destinations before Begin and before/after author resolution on advance. Existing rewritten-parent mappings supply actual target commits; not-yet-rewritten parents conservatively use source-parent paths. This can require moving a blocker before the parent is rewritten even if later manual resolution would remove that destination. Unrelated ignored files remain permitted.
- The guard reads bounded, non-linked todo/mapping metadata and never changes it. A blocked parent checkout leaves the staged resolution, index, HEAD and ignored bytes intact. After moving the blocker, ordinary Continue can finish the preserved merge; no retry journal is needed because preflight refused before advancing the paused step. Core checks cover this recovery, initial refusal before creating rebase state, malformed parent mappings, linked metadata refusal and unaffected unrelated ignored bytes. The fixture explicitly verifies that the first parent was already rewritten before the second-parent conflict, rather than substituting a blocked ordinary pick for the private parent checkout.
- Full build gates pass (`/tmp/syncmd-virtual-base-parent-checkout-build.log`): 568 core tests, debug/test/release assembly and vital release lint. Additional fixture assertions and native checks are being verified below. Existing failed characterization logs are retained at `/tmp/syncmd-conflicting-recursive-characterization.log` and `/tmp/syncmd-preserved-parent-checkout-characterization.log`; only owned synthetic data was used. No cloud changes or external Git writes are involved.
- The strengthened final fixtures pass all **568 core tests**, with no failures/errors/skips (`/tmp/syncmd-parent-checkout-final-test-build.log`); the final instrumentation APK rebuild also passes. Localization generation checks and all seven tooling tests pass, with unchanged 853/158/25/695 key coverage and three untranslated plurals.
- **67 distinct targeted native checks pass** against unchanged final sources/resources/APKs: 19 checkout/rebase cases (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-h51l710h/regression.log`, 97.849s), all 39 production-navigation cases (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-hqcngpkm/regression.log`, 187.237s) and nine remote-merge/shared-media rename cases (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-c9ft9qz6/regression.log`, 43.043s). ### Foreground external-editor refresh (2026-10-29)

- The existing process-local invalidation only reported GitSync operations. A new Android `RepositoryObservations` source combines those signals with coalesced native filesystem events for visible repository visits. Repository cards, vault/Git screens, the file browser and standalone diff preview now observe the same lifecycle-owned source. Ordinary editor drafts retain explicit reload and revision-checked save behavior; observation does not reload or replace an unsaved draft.
- Canonical-path subscribers share one monitor. Native directory watches are pooled by physical directory identity so overlapping repositories and shared parents release independently. The initial implementation's five basic device tests passed, but a new overlapping-root test reproduced lost events after one observer stopped (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-ifsa2c_p/regression.log`); its protected baseline restored. Pooling fixes that case. The implementation follows the [Android 15 FileObserver source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/android15-release/core/java/android/os/FileObserver.java), which stores a single observer callback per native descriptor. No private platform APIs are used.
- Watches cover working directories and relevant Git metadata, including new directory hierarchies and atomic replacements. Git object/log/LFS stores are excluded from recursive monitoring to avoid data-store event storms; refs, HEAD, index and rebase metadata still trigger refresh. Scans are bounded to 2,048 directories / 50,000 entries per repository, plus its parent watch. A 30-second foreground fallback invalidation covers dropped events, native watch limits and filesystems with unreliable notifications. Initial watches are installed before the first subscribed read, pending signals are conflated, filesystem work runs off the main thread and repository access/publication policy is rechecked under the shared lease. Last-subscriber cancellation joins cleanup. No background service, network request, sync, permission grant or file-content snapshot is created.
- Root replacement rebuilds observation; replacement root symlinks are not followed. Read-only Git operations do not create a refresh feedback loop. Diff invalidation closes any open revert confirmation before loading a changed preview, requiring renewed confirmation. Wider filesystem/device performance and accessibility qualification remain open.
- The pooled-source full build gates pass (`/tmp/syncmd-foreground-observer-pooled-build.log`): 568 core tests, debug/test/release assembly and vital release lint. Localization generation checks and seven tooling tests pass with unchanged resource coverage. All 14 initial final-build watcher/overview native checks pass (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-m0po437r/regression.log`), including private/media writes, canonical sharing, nested/atomic writes, bounded fallback, overlapping roots, root replacement, quiet reads, direct external commit display and lifecycle cleanup. A shared app-invalidation collector now publishes to every root subscriber, and filesystem events already covered by a published invalidation are coalesced. Loaded overview cards remain visible during foreground reads; failures still replace old metadata with Unavailable. Full gates pass (`/tmp/syncmd-foreground-observer-final-build.log`). Lifecycle assertions now wait for asynchronous cancellation/cleanup within the existing 10-second action bound; the stopped-overview assertion failure is retained at `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-1n0ge9x6/regression.log`.

## Workflow localization and quantity coverage — 2026-10-29

- Expanded each of the 25 non-English locale resource sets from 158 to **187 strings**, leaving **666 of 853** base keys with English fallback. Added all three LFS extension-review plural resources in every locale. ## Obsidian connection continuity after vault moves — 2026-10-29

- Reproduced the production defect on Android: after a real private-to-app-media vault move, the unchanged companion callback looked up credentials at the old path and failed (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-mmzdktef/regression.log`). - Migration requires a committed move receipt, the old Git metadata identity/catalog registration/credential revision, the same authenticated GitHub account/remote, the destination's recorded root and Git identities, current catalog registration and matching bounded no-follow public handoff metadata. Both repository paths are serialized. It changes only the private binding's path, identity, registration and credential revision; request/connection IDs and the active/consumed flag are preserved. It neither prepares a new request nor writes ciphertext during the move.
- Reviewed the actual companion's `Connection.complete()` implementation: successful import removes request/recipient/response and retains only `connection.json`. A consumed binding therefore accepts that descriptor without a pending request; an active binding still requires its original pending request. Changed request metadata or credentials cannot inherit authorization through a move and require fresh explicit preparation. Ordinary renewal retains the existing connection ID. Repeated move completion cannot reactivate a consumed handoff or modify its response.
- Private binding persistence failure is tested after filesystem/catalog publication. The original private map is restored, the committed move journal remains available, and a new production Settings ViewModel retries migration successfully without exporting into the retained copy. ## Checked clone completion and registration recovery — 2026-10-29

## Recoverable companion preparation after process interruption — 2026-10-29

- Closed the new-preparation ordering gap: previously request/connection files were published before the private binding was saved. Preparation now first plans an immutable request and fingerprints of the four bounded public handoff files, durably reserves that intent in the private connection store, publishes the request/descriptor, and acknowledges private completion. Pending records use schema 2; existing ready/consumed schema-1 bindings retain their encoding and behavior. No token or private key is journaled, and the public wire protocol remains version 1.
- Core publication accepts the original snapshot or a prefix of its own completed writes. It can resume after old recipient/response cleanup, request publication or descriptor publication. Once the new request is visible, recovery preserves a recipient already written by the companion. Foreign file changes, symlinks, changed origin and expired preparations stop publication for review; recovery never silently extends expiry. Canonical root/.git checks, regular-file/size/UTF-8 bounds and atomic file replacement remain in place.
- The Android provider reserves private authorization before publishing and checks catalog registration, Git metadata identity, original account/remote and credential revision at every continuation boundary. Startup Settings recovery finishes only these saved pending preparations; it does not fetch a token remotely or export a credential. A connect callback can finish its matching pending preparation before the ordinary one-time response. Forget removes pending authorization; credential changes block recovery until explicit preparation. Verified vault moves retain matching unpublished plans, and Settings recovery completes them at the destination.
- **575 core tests pass**, including four new preparation tests. One interrupts each of six authorization boundaries and resumes the same plan; another writes a recipient between request and descriptor publication and verifies it survives repeated completion and supports a response. Foreign-edit/symlink preservation and expiry rejection are covered. Debug app/test APK, release APK and release vital lint gates pass (`/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-preparation-build.log`).
- **55 distinct ordinary Android tests pass**: 16 companion cases, nine clone-completion cases, 11 history-clone cases and 19 private/app-media move cases. The device-folder move requiring a shared-storage permission grant was not selected. Broad native log: `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-e39ou722/regression.log` (332.446 seconds). The first case paused result reporting and later passed; the existing run completed without restart. A read-only Java-stack request required root and was not pursued further. No cause or performance fix is claimed.
- **Two actual process-restart workflows pass through four explicit native phases.** One stops after durable private reservation with no public request. The other leaves request/descriptor files and a companion recipient visible while private completion is still pending. The protected runner stops the debug package; each recovery phase asserts a new app PID, loads through production Settings startup, retains the same request ID/recipient, verifies unchanged HEAD/index/working draft, completes one encrypted handoff, rejects duplicate export, and removes its owned fixture. Host workflow metadata and the four phase logs are `/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-preparation-cold-workflows.json` and `/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-preparation-cold-{reserved,published}-{prepare,recover}.log`. The repeated recovery method represents two workflows, not four distinct test methods.
- All five protected baseline cycles were restored and removed. Final audit has **29 zero fields**, including owned cold-preparation workspaces and pending real-store preparation records. Font scale remains 1.0, notification permission remains denied with its existing flags, and debug app is stopped. The unchanged 485-file source/APK receipt and proof are `/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-preparation-receipt.json` and `/tmp/syncmd-obsidian-preparation-proof.json`.
- This recovery starts from an already authorized private preparation for a registered repository. Clone filesystem publication before catalog commit, cold clone UI/state and pending catalog-marker reconciliation, interruption during encrypted response publication, legacy public metadata without a private reservation, installed-companion app switching, wider storage/provider/API behavior and release accessibility/motion/performance remain open. No authority is inferred from old public files alone. The full parity goal remains in progress.


## Ready-clone publication across process restart — 2026-10-29

- Added private no-backup clone receipts before the final staging-directory rename. The receipt freezes destination/parent/root/Git identities, remote, branch, encrypted credential revision, display metadata and a hash of any reviewed history entry. It contains no authentication token or private key. Atomic receipt replacement, strict bounded UTF-8/JSON parsing and no-follow file checks protect metadata; a complete temporary receipt can finish after process interruption. ## Clone catalog acknowledgement and revocation provenance — 2026-10-30

- Closed the newly identified catalog/receipt window for provenance-aware registrations. The first catalog entry and a private registration witness now use one preferences editor commit. The witness binds the private publication UUID, frozen completion hash, canonical path and original catalog date. It contains no token or private key. Pending-marker cleanup and later private-file acknowledgement remain retryable; neither creates a new catalog date.
- Every catalog persistence path revokes a retained witness when its matching path/date disappears or is replaced. Removal, tracking disconnection and move catalog updates therefore cannot leave an old unacknowledged clone authorized to re-add the old entry. A normal manual import/reconnect remains possible, and a fresh publication UUID/frozen working-copy identity can reuse a removed path. An older completion cannot overwrite that entry or regain authority simply because the folder is still present.
- Explicit recovery dismissal checks the exact review and live-owner guard before committing revocation, then unlinks only private metadata. A failed revocation commit leaves that metadata in place. If revocation commits but unlink fails, startup and old completion callbacks still reject the cancelled recovery. A late callback without its private publication is rejected even after obsolete witness metadata is pruned.
- Obsolete witnesses are pruned only after their private publication is gone. Corrupt retained values fail closed for that registration without blocking unrelated catalog changes; an explicit reviewed dismissal can revoke them. Pending clone markers are cleared in the same revocation/pruning commit unless another active publication still needs that frozen snapshot. A complete temporary file or original working copy cannot override revocation in preferences.
- **601 core tests pass**, including eight provenance planning/codec cases and two new reviewed-deletion callback cases. Debug app/test APK, release APK and vital release lint gates pass (`/tmp/syncmd-clone-provenance-authority-build.log`). The later fluent-editor fixture correction passes the same gates (`/tmp/syncmd-clone-provenance-fixture-build.log`), with unchanged production source/resource/debug/release APK inputs. Localization checks and tooling remain unchanged at 870 base keys, 187 translations in each of 25 non-English locales, 683 English fallbacks and three translated LFS plurals.
- The first six Android fault cases passed against the earlier six-case fixture (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-y4hwhifu/regression.log`). Adding the pending-marker case exposed that a delegated editor returned the underlying editor from fluent `remove`/`put` calls, bypassing the commit fault (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-juj6w3fm/regression.log`). The fixture now preserves its wrapper throughout fluent calls. **All seven final new cases pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-4rny9u69/regression.log`), including the unchanged commit-failure assertion. The earlier six are not additional distinct coverage.
- Final affected-workflow, actual process-restart and audit results follow below. No new UI layout, cloud configuration, external Git ref or physical/release installation is changed. Existing legacy journal-only data that lacks the new catalog witness, interruption during other filesystem/Git mutations, wider provider/device/API behavior, localization and release qualification remain open. A history timestamp alone is not cancellation provenance. The complete parity goal remains active.

### Background launcher shortcut execution (2026-10-30)

- Added explicit **Run without opening GitSync.md** consent to launcher shortcut creation. - Added a private, non-sticky Android `dataSync` service for one user tap at a time. It starts a foreground notification before work, has a Cancel action and handles the platform timeout with `stopSelf()`. It never schedules a worker, boot restart, redelivery or retry. Queued receipts can be claimed only in their reserving process; claimed/finished/abandoned/pruned receipts cannot be reused. Both authorization and the new bounded result metadata are excluded from cloud and device-transfer backup. ### Background callback busy and startup-failure returns (2026-10-30)

- Background callback admission now owns the platform service-start boundary. A rejected start consumes its pending request, saves an attention receipt with a typed failure reason when storage is available, and offers the frozen error destination through a private, immutable notification return. A damaged receipt store is preserved and prevents dispatch; an ephemeral one-shot failure return can still be offered while authorization is valid. There is no automatic retry or automatic external return.
- A second callback arriving while the service is busy receives its own `busy` error and saved reason without changing the active request, its notification-return state or its Git work. The saved authorization card explains that the callback did not run and that the link can be used again after the current action finishes. Legacy result receipts retain their exact schema-1 bytes; typed rejection receipts use canonical schema 2. Tokens, callback response data and repository paths stay out of service intents and execution receipts.
- The service retains receipt ownership through actual coroutine completion, protecting confirmed history clearing while result publication is still settling. Stop PendingIntents now use a distinct per-run data URI, preventing identity collisions with a later run. Notification pruning/cancellation failures cannot discard an otherwise valid return.
- **74 distinct native cases pass against the final production inputs.** The broad final run provides 67 non-launcher passes (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-l177zpjm/regression.log`); all seven launcher cases pass in the final focused rerun (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-28a5b9uu/regression.log`). Coverage includes exported busy admission while a real repository lease blocks status, exact HEAD/index/draft/remote preservation, no MainActivity before review, one-shot structured errors, failed platform-dispatch injection, damaged-store preservation, unchanged authorization, legacy receipt bytes, ownership through completion, Stop identity, consent/revocation, successful Status/Pull/Push/Sync and source localization checks.
- The broad final run had one launcher **cleanup** timeout after its product assertions: Move item returned before its transition had settled, and resolving Remove immediately could leave the fixture pinned. The cleanup-only command reproduced the failure. Waiting for launcher idle before resolving Remove recovered only the exact owned shortcut and passed the original push-retry case (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-4bnah65q/regression.log`); the final seven-case rerun passes with all temporary diagnostics removed. No product assertion or unpinned-state check was weakened. Earlier failing runs are not counted as passes. The protected runner now accepts one exact owned shortcut UUID for recovery with a single launcher selector. Argent's gesture control endpoint was unavailable on this emulator; native instrumentation supplied the existing real-launcher QA path.
- A notification-enabled repeat passes at **actual system font scale 1.6**, returning the busy error to the separate caller process once while the first action remains active (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-nz95lsqf/regression.log`). Another notification-enabled repeat returns two independently completed results once each (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-4s0960_t/regression.log`).

### Background callback push and return recovery across real process loss (2026-10-30)

- Added reproducible native/loopback integration coverage for three exact boundaries: a push request received but not applied by the remote, an applied push whose acknowledgement is withheld, and a completed saved result whose caller has not opened its return. Each uses an exported authorized callback link, the production private service, real HTTPS fetch/push and a fresh isolated Git remote. The local draft is committed before the controlled boundary. The host verifies native readiness and remote state, records the live app PID, force-stops only the debug app, observes the original instrumentation handle terminate and recovers in a different PID. The preparation phases end in intentional process crashes; those crashes are not counted as passing JUnit tests.
- **All three final process-loss scenarios pass** in `/tmp/syncmd-callback-interruption-_734vblj/proof.json`. Fresh startup and saved-result review preserve the exact local HEAD/index/draft bytes and do not replay the old execution ID. The first two saved receipts are shown as interrupted; an already completed receipt remains completed. The stale private result-return Activity target has no in-memory response after process loss and cannot replay Git. A fresh explicit invocation publishes the existing commit when the remote lacks it; when the remote already has it, it returns `no_changes` without another receive-pack POST. Each remote contains exactly the initial commit plus one callback commit and passes host `git fsck`. Unexpected Authorization headers remain zero.
- The accepted-push retry initially exposed an incorrect test expectation, not a runtime error: the test expected `status=ok`, while both current iOS `CallbackURLHandler.mapPushResult` and Android's existing protocol tests define a no-change Push as an error. The final fixture asserts the correct error destination and `errorCode=no_changes`, the retained SHA and one-shot delivery. No result/commit/remote assertion was weakened and the earlier failing run is excluded from final passes. The before-publication fixture deliberately discards the paused request after process death; the accepted-publication case covers the other possible remote outcome.
- `tools/testing/callback_git_fixture.py` is an owned temporary HTTPS Git server with pre/post-publication gates. Its three boundaries also pass independently with a real host Git client (`/tmp/syncmd-callback-git-host-proof.json`). Test-process certificate trust is scoped to that loopback certificate, with hostname verification retained. Git transport uses an explicit public credential policy. No GitHub account or external Git service is required by the fixture. `tools/testing/run_callback_interruption_checks.py` serializes the phases and retains original preference/locale recovery encrypted inside app-private no-backup storage; the test bearer handoff also remains private and is removed at cleanup.
- Baselines now support a canonical UUID owner while preserving the existing default suite interface. Capture refuses any unresolved private baseline. The controller records its exact owned reverse rule and holds an evidence-directory lock so recovery cannot run concurrently with its controller. A deliberately staged controller interruption was immediately recovered with `--recover` (`/tmp/syncmd-callback-interruption-1y5f0iap`, `/tmp/syncmd-callback-interruption-recovery-check.log`), proving removal of the owned callback/workspace, restoration of encrypted original maps and removal of the recorded reverse rule. The staging check is separate from the three successful product scenarios.
- **30 compatibility checks pass** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-bn4s9q1k/regression.log`): receipt ownership/persistence, callback authorization, and private snapshot round-trip, locale restoration, corruption rejection and new cross-owner AAD rejection. The legacy default baseline path also captures/restores successfully. The new wrong-owner test copies only its own encrypted synthetic snapshot and proves no preference change before rejection. Native test assembly passes (`/tmp/syncmd-callback-interruption-final-build.log`); Python syntax, ten localization tooling tests, catalog/native-expectation checks and `git diff --check` pass.
- Production behavior required no change: all **404** production/core-source and debug/release APK hashes from the previous authority receipt remain unchanged. No new core or release-build execution is claimed; their prior evidence remains applicable to unchanged inputs. All **523** recorded final source/resource/tool/artifact hashes remain unchanged. Localization stays at 902 base keys, 242 translations per each of 25 locales, 660 English fallbacks and three translated LFS plurals.
- The enabled final audit reports **42 zero counters**, including the two new interruption-workspace/marker fields (`/tmp/syncmd-callback-interruption-final-audit.log`). Recovery checkpoints have no pending cleanup/baseline/reverse work. The last owned fixture and staged-recovery ports were independently verified closed and unforwarded. Font scale remains 1.0, app locale remains system default, and notifications remain denied with the original sensitivity flags. Owned debug/test processes are stopped; scoped Argent cleanup finds no helper. Combined proof/case list: `/tmp/syncmd-callback-interruption-proof.json`; input receipt: `/tmp/syncmd-callback-interruption-final-authority-inputs.json`.
- Full parity remains active. These force-stop/fresh-start cases do not qualify every OS kill mode, interruption inside local index/ref replacement or active replay, or the window between a saved receipt and in-memory response publication. Broader providers/transports/devices, all remaining localization/accessibility/release requirements and every other open matrix item remain in scope. Only owned local Git fixtures were changed; no external Git-service writes, cloud changes, release installation or store publication occurred.

### GitHub session revisions during account loading and PAT validation (2026-10-30)

- Reproduced three production ViewModel failures with real Android auth storage
  and controlled GitHub responses: selecting another account while PAT validation
  is pending leaves its list unloaded; reselecting the same account invalidates
  the old request without replacing it; removing the active account leaves the
  validation spinner busy until the held response returns. All three time out
  before the fix: `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-zsh37a2g/regression.log`.
  Red source/APK hashes: `/tmp/syncmd-github-revision-red-inputs.json`.
- `GitHubViewModel` now invalidates load/validation ownership for every external
  session revision, including unchanged tokens. It cancels superseded jobs
  without waiting for a noncooperating response and immediately loads the selected
  account, or settles signed-out state. Same-account selection keeps the search;
  identity changes clear old account data. A bounded publication marker and an
  owned revision distinguish a successful PAT's own persistence event so its
  profile/repository loading can finish. Existing request/revision/token checks
  still reject stale results.
- Final native cohort passes 32 distinct cases (ViewModel 8, Accounts 15,
  Session 9): `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-r3n44m6s/regression.log`.

  external push is claimed.
- Debug/test/release assembly and release-critical lint pass in

### Repository author save ownership while waiting for another operation (2026-10-30)

### Completed-clone retention after receipt and before publication (2026-10-30)

- Three new real-Git core checks reproduce completed staging deletion after a
  durable publication receipt: an exception immediately after reservation,
  cancellation at the same boundary and an unavailable recovery-ownership check.
  All three fail before retention is implemented:
  `/tmp/syncmd-clone-retention-red-online.log`; source receipt:
  `/tmp/syncmd-clone-retention-red-inputs.json`. An initial offline attempt could
  not resolve missing pinned JVM test dependencies; the online run restored them.
- `SafeCloneService` now accepts a host retention check and preserves its exact
  owned staging folder while a publication receipt needs it, including when
  ownership cannot be read. Default unreceipted failure cleanup, inode checks,
  replacement/symlink protection and occupied-destination preservation remain.
  Both clone entry points consult their private receipt; GitHub transient retry
  also stops once a receipt exists instead of issuing another transfer.
- Added clone-specific credential publication without changing ordinary
  connection-edit rollback. After failure it retains the newly encrypted record
  only if the receipt pins the same canonical destination and exact credential
  revision. Missing, unrelated or older-revision receipts cannot retain a new
  binding. Unreadable private recovery state preserves data for review. URL-clone
  cleanup also keeps its pre-saved public/HTTPS/SSH policy while recovery is
  pending instead of removing the revision required by that receipt.
- Two native red cases proved both clone ViewModels deleted their receipts when
  the owner was cleared, even though publication had failed and staging remained:
  `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-eswaujtb/regression.log` (12 cases, two failures;
  ten pass), with `/tmp/syncmd-clone-retention-native-red-inputs.json`. Ordinary
  owner disposal now deactivates the owner and retains durable metadata. Explicit
  Cancel/reset keeps its existing separate dismissal behavior.
- All **604 core cases pass**, no failures/errors/skips:
  `/tmp/syncmd-clone-retention-core.log` and
  `/tmp/syncmd-clone-retention-core-proof.json`. This is a fresh full execution,
  replacing reuse of the previous 601-case result for these changed core inputs.
  Final Android cohort: **63 native cases pass**, 158.993 seconds:
  `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-2byqa7f_/regression.log`. The recovery class now
  contains 13 cases, including six new checks. Post-receipt error/cancellation
  retains the encrypted account binding; a fresh Settings owner publishes and
  registers the same completed clone with unchanged index and post-receipt draft,
  and exactly one transport call. Both cleared-owner cases also recover through
  Settings once an obstructing foreign destination is removed. Unrelated-path
  and stale-version receipt guards retain ordinary credential rollback. Existing
  completion, catalog provenance, HTTPS/SSH connection and account flows pass.
- Debug/AndroidTest/minified release assembly and release-critical lint pass:
  `/tmp/syncmd-clone-retention-final-build.log`. Ten localization tooling tests,
  generation checks and whitespace checks pass. Resource inputs stay unchanged
  at 911 base / 289 translated / 622 fallback keys per each of 25 locales, plus
  three translated LFS plural resources. No new visible UI layout/copy or motion

  `/tmp/syncmd-clone-retention-final-authority-inputs.json` and
