### Keep completed clones when Git LFS download needs attention (2026-10-31)

- Compared the current iOS `LocalGitService.clone`: after Git clone completes,
  LFS download/trust/local-edit failures are reported separately and keep the
  repository. Android previously returned clone failure for hydration exceptions,
  allowing staging cleanup to discard completed Git history. Three real HTTPS
  regressions fail before the fix: corrupt LFS object data, rejected object
  redirect and a local edit during download. Red log/source receipt:
  `/tmp/syncmd-clone-lfs-red.log`, `/tmp/syncmd-clone-lfs-red-inputs.json`.
- Git checkout errors still fail cloning. Exceptions only from the subsequent
  hydration phase now keep the completed checkout, pointer files and local edits;
  cancellation is rethrown. No remote response text is logged by the new warning.
  Normal publication and checked registration then preserve the completed clone.
  Core tests exercise real download retry without changing HEAD, redirect refusal
  without leaked credentials and concurrent-edit preservation. The malformed
  batch test now requires clone preservation plus an explicit hydration failure,
  unchanged pointer/index/HEAD and no corrupt cache publication; invalid data is
  still rejected rather than turning a successful Git clone into deleted history.
- `CloneCompletion` inspects current LFS file state after checked registration.
  Missing/modified file contents, or unavailable inspection, show a fixed warning
  and wait for Review Git LFS or Continue. No duplicate persisted warning flag is
  needed. Review delivery uses the existing resumed-owner navigation gate, and

### Edit an existing local branch’s upstream sync target (2026-10-31)

- Compared iOS Settings’ editable repository Branch field with Android’s current
  controls. Android already followed configured upstreams but had no production
  editor. Added Repository connection → Upstream branch and its app navigation
  route, with a separate screen so an upstream save cannot commit address or
  credential fields from the connection form. The screen shows the local branch,
  configured remote choices and an editable remote-branch name, with Save/Reload.
- `RepositoryUpstream` captures canonical root/git identities, current local branch
  and config revision. Save holds the repository lease and Git config lock,
  validates the target/ref/fetch mapping and rechecks the reviewed state before
  commit. Only that branch’s remote/merge settings change. Files, index, local refs,
  endpoint URLs, fetch rules, author and encrypted connection records are retained.
  Detached HEAD, active Git operations, unknown remotes, invalid/tag refs, absent
  mappings, stale config/branch and external locks reject writes. Successful saves
  invalidate repository readers. The editor does not fetch or test remote access;
  later network operations retain their existing endpoint/account guards.
- The ViewModel rechecks generation after acquiring the lease so a screen switch
  or same-path reload invalidates queued writes. New native cases cover both
  boundaries, detached-HEAD disabled state and the production entry/save. The UI

### Native Git protocol URLs with explicit public access (2026-10-31)

- Added strict `git://` URL parsing to the existing manual-clone and connection flows, including the default port 9418, custom ports, bracketed IPv6 and escaped-path preservation. Userinfo, query/fragment components, invalid ports, slash-only paths, decoded traversal, control characters and backslashes are rejected. HTTPS and SSH retain their existing parsers.
- Native Git uses explicit public access. The clone form disables token, SSH, GitHub-account and saved-secret choices for this protocol, and requires Public access if another method was selected before changing the address. ViewModel, encrypted credential-store and transport-provider guards reject credential-based native Git operations before opening the transport. A `git://github.com:443/...` URL cannot become a GitHub HTTPS credential target; the fallback GitHub token callback is not read. Connection editing restricts native Git to public-access policies, with HTTPS-only eligibility retained for Basic/PAT/GitHub choices.
- Native Git does not imply an HTTPS LFS endpoint. Explicit custom HTTPS LFS endpoints remain subject to the existing endpoint review, and an absent endpoint requests configuration instead of forwarding Git credentials or guessing an HTTPS origin. The existing completed-clone/LFS-review retention flow remains in place. Native-Git-specific LFS device qualification remains open.
- Core result: **618 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips. The new real loopback JGit daemon fixture clones a selected branch, pulls a server-side commit and pushes a local commit anonymously; URL and credential-guard cases also pass. The new valid native-clone regression failed on the previous unsupported-transport guard before implementation. The final slash-only-path correction is included in the fresh full-core run. Core XML and **177 input hashes** are recorded in `/tmp/syncmd-native-git-core-proof.json`.
- Native Android result: **75 tests passed** on the dedicated GitSync API 35 emulator `emulator-5556`, using the final debug/test APKs (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-40pi602u/regression.log`, 124.229 seconds). The production clone form uses a real Git daemon bound to the emulator's loopback interface, with selected-branch checkout, canonical remote address, persisted anonymous policy and catalog registration assertions. A separate test proves token/key saves and all private connection choices cannot replace the public native-Git record. The cohort also covers existing HTTPS/SSH connection behavior, credential migration, history cloning, clone completion/recovery, upstream editing and localization. The earlier 75-test run also passed before replacing the native form's file-backed transport fixture with this actual Android TCP fixture.
- Final Java 17 offline core/debug/test/release/lint-vital build passed (`/tmp/syncmd-native-git-final-build.log`, 2m 28s); the final test-only daemon-fixture build passed (`/tmp/syncmd-native-git-device-transport-build.log`, 23s). The initial combined build caught a test assertion API typo, which was corrected before the successful builds. Localization importer/expectation checks, ten localization tooling tests and `git diff --check` pass. The new public-access guidance has assistant-authored translations in all 25 non-English locales: **921 base keys / 299 translated / 622 English fallbacks** per locale, with three translated LFS plurals and 468 independently recorded English source literals. Human linguistic review remains open.

This is synthetic light-mode form evidence, not a new full-screen accessibility, large-text or motion qualification claim.

### Local file repository URLs, source access and saved connections (2026-10-31)

- Connected `file://` URLs to manual clone, saved connection editing and history review. `FileRemote` accepts absolute local paths, the standard `file:/...` spelling and `localhost`, canonicalizes them to `file:///...`, and preserves escaped filesystem names containing spaces, Unicode, `#` and `?`. Nonlocal authorities, userinfo, numeric ports, query/fragment components, root-only paths, decoded traversal, controls and backslashes are rejected. Bare repositories and ordinary working repositories both clone through the production staged-publication path with optional branch selection.
- Added Local access to the clone and connection forms. Tokens, SSH keys, GitHub accounts and saved-secret choices are unavailable for local URLs; changing from private authentication requires explicitly selecting Local access before Clone/Save. ViewModel, credential-store and transport-provider guards reject network credentials for file URLs. The default GitHub token callback is not read. The connection address label now uses the existing translated Repository URL label instead of naming only HTTPS and SSH, and parser failure copy no longer excludes valid local/native protocols.
- File sources use the existing Android repository storage policy. The clone ViewModel checks the source before allocating a target or saving a credential policy, and each file transport provider checks again at construction and configuration. App storage works directly; shared device folders retain the existing storage-permission and saved-folder-grant requirements. This adds no cloud-provider-to-filesystem conversion. Explicit HTTPS LFS endpoints retain their separate review; file remotes do not cause an HTTPS LFS origin or credential to be guessed.
- Core result: **622 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips. Four new tests cover strict parsing, default-token/secret rejection, real bare/working-source clones on a selected branch with escaped filenames, absence of shared-object alternates, unchanged source HEAD/index/refs and cleanup on a missing source/branch. The new parser/credential/clone cases failed on the previous implementation before the feature was added. Existing file-backed lifecycle fixtures now use `Credentials.None`; simulated HTTPS/SSH fixture boundaries still assert the requested network credentials while their local backing transport receives none. Fresh full-core evidence and **179 input hashes** are in `/tmp/syncmd-file-clone-core-proof.json`.

### GitHub shorthand and git+ssh input normalization (2026-10-31)

- Added an explicit `GitRemote.parseInput` boundary for addresses entered in the clone and connection forms. Literal `owner/repo` and `owner/repo.git` expand to the same HTTPS GitHub endpoint without adding a second `.git` suffix. Unicode path segments are escaped as literal repository names; raw control/whitespace characters, traversal-only names and ambiguous delimiters do not become shorthand targets. Fully qualified addresses continue through their existing transport parsers.
- Stored endpoints deliberately retain `GitRemote.parse` semantics. An existing relative filesystem remote named `fixture/notes` remains a local relative transport target and cannot silently become GitHub or trigger the fallback GitHub-token callback. History review of such an unsupported stored address still requires explicit review, including when a default GitHub account exists. Only the user-entered form path expands shorthand; the user sees the canonical destination before Clone/Save.
- Added `git+ssh://` normalization to the existing SSH parser, including case-insensitive scheme input, username, custom port, IPv6 and absolute/home-relative path preservation. The transport safety guard accepts this alias through the same key and host-trust path. Forms persist canonical `ssh://` URLs; already-stored raw aliases remain supported by the core transport callback. Alias parsing does not relax inline-password, query, fragment or traversal rejection.
- Added supported-format help and canonical-address previews to the clone and connection URL fields. The help reuses the complete 25-language iOS catalog entry; the new preview label has assistant-authored translations in all 25 non-English locales. The preview is part of the text field's supporting semantics and appears only when normalization changes the supplied address. URI/URIish syntax failures at the explicit input boundary now return a generic diagnostic instead of repeating malformed inline credentials.
- Core result: **628 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips. Six new input cases cover one-time shorthand expansion, endpoint-bound credential challenges, malformed/nonliteral input, unchanged stored-relative behavior and zero default-token reads, SSH alias equivalence and Unicode segments, and diagnostics that omit synthetic inline secrets. Four of the first five cases failed on the pre-feature delegation boundary before implementation. The existing real loopback SSH fixture additionally clones, fetches and pushes through a raw `git+ssh://` remote with the same pinned host key and SSH identity; ordinary SSH and SSH LFS-authentication coverage remain in that fixture. Fresh XML and **180 core input hashes** are in `/tmp/syncmd-remote-input-core-proof.json`.

The GitHub-account connection case enters shorthand with an existing `.git` suffix and checks explicit account selection and replacement of the old secret. ### HTTP Git transport with release-policy approval pending (2026-10-31)

- Added `HttpRemote` with explicit scheme/host/port/path identity; HTTP defaults to port 80 and HTTPS to 443. `HttpsRemote` remains a secure-only wrapper for automatic GitHub credentials and LFS. Clone/connection parsing, explicit Basic/PAT persistence and inline-URL credential migration now accept HTTP without silently changing it to HTTPS. Userinfo/query/fragment/traversal rejection remains in place; migrated credentials are encrypted separately and local index/HEAD data are preserved.
- Scoped challenge handling now compares scheme as well as host, port and repository. The HTTP request factory checks every request before creation, including redirects with cached Authorization headers; a different scheme, origin, port or repository cannot receive the selected credential. HTTP URLs to github.com, including port 443, cannot read the automatic GitHub token or select a GitHub-account policy. Manual HTTP credentials require the user's explicit repository authentication choice.
- Added the unencrypted-transport notice and HTTP format help to clone/connection forms in all 25 non-English locales. The notice explains that repository data and entered credentials can be read in transit. Existing credentials from an HTTPS endpoint cannot silently become a matching HTTP policy. Current resource totals: **927 base / 305 translated / 622 English fallbacks** per locale, three translated LFS plurals and 468 independently recorded English source literals. Format-list variants derive from the iOS catalog by expanding HTTPS to HTTP/HTTPS; notice translations are assistant-authored. Human linguistic review remains pending.
- **Production networking is unchanged.** Automatic approval review rejected an attempted production-wide cleartext flag because it would persistently weaken app network security beyond repository requests. The rejected command did not execute. The implemented safer alternative is a debug-only Network Security Configuration: base cleartext denied, with exact localhost and 127.0.0.1 exceptions for fixture traffic. The release manifest has no new cleartext/network-security override and its source SHA256 matches the prior phase. Android's domain exceptions and cleartext opt-in are documented in the [official network security configuration guide](https://developer.android.com/privacy-and-security/security-config). The concrete, unapplied production proposal is `/tmp/syncmd-http-production-policy.patch`, adding one `android:usesCleartextTraffic="true"` attribute. That app-wide production setting needs explicit user approval; the debug configuration would remain loopback-only.
- Core result: **634 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips. Four new transport tests cover HTTP normalization, secure-only parser separation, scheme-bound challenges, zero automatic-token reads and pre-request redirect rejection; all four failed before implementation. A new migration case verifies HTTP stays HTTP while removing inline credentials and preserving index/HEAD. The existing real native-Git HTTP backend fixture now runs separately for HTTP and HTTPS, exercising selected-branch clone, fetch/pull/push, expired-credential refresh through connection editing, divergence/rebase with no remote push, public access with zero Authorization headers and redirect escape rejection. Existing HTTPS certificate/redirect checks remain. Fresh XML and **181 core input hashes** are in `/tmp/syncmd-http-core-proof.json`.

The new in-emulator smart-HTTP fixture serves an owned bare repository with JGit UploadPack over a real loopback socket, including a Basic challenge. The production form clones/ registers a selected branch with explicit synthetic credentials; encrypted scheme binding, zero unexpected requests and successful authenticated requests are asserted. Runtime policy assertions prove loopback cleartext allowed and git.example cleartext denied. Separate new cases verify HTTP cannot use GitHub-account access and that connection saves preserve working files/index. The cohort retains the prior HTTPS/SSH/native-Git/file/shorthand, migration, history, clone recovery, upstream, repository-management and localization checks.
- A focused four-case run also passed (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-jpbkxmic/regression.log`). The first 93-case run passed all three new HTTP cases but an existing shorthand-clone case exceeded its 15-second completion wait. That exact case passed in isolation and in the final full cohort without a production behavior change. ### HTTP LFS transport and explicit endpoint review (2026-10-31)

- Added HTTP(S) LFS batch/object transport through the platform HTTP connection implementation. HTTP repositories derive matching HTTP LFS endpoints; explicitly configured HTTP endpoints use the existing separate endpoint review and encrypted, scheme/direction-bound credentials. Signed object-action queries are preserved, while HTTPS batch responses cannot downgrade object transfers to HTTP. Redirect, header, bounded-transfer and SHA256 checks remain enforced.

### Reviewed HTTP endpoints issued by SSH LFS authentication (2026-10-31)

- The current iOS SSH authenticator accepts the server-returned LFS URL and headers (`GitLFSCitadelSSHAuthenticator.swift`, lines 132–145). Android now accepts HTTP URLs from the verified SSH server after a separate repository/SSH-remote/endpoint/direction approval. The response's bounded headers and expiry are checked before review, and expiry is checked again afterward. Private SSH keys stay in the SSH transport; short-lived LFS headers stay in memory and are never stored with approvals. HTTPS SSH responses retain their existing behavior, and HTTPS batch responses still cannot downgrade object transfers to HTTP.
- Added a separate encrypted SSH-authorization permission alongside existing custom endpoint Public/Basic policies. A public or Basic approval cannot authorize server-issued headers; SSH approval cannot become a Basic/public policy. The policies can coexist, and revocation removes that direction's permissions while preserving the other direction. Background operations without the required approval fail before transfer. The foreground review shows the HTTP warning and explains the ephemeral authorization; saved endpoint cards distinguish SSH authorization, separate credentials and public access and show the HTTP warning.
- Core result: **640 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips. The real pinned SSH fixture verifies rejected unapproved HTTP authorization and approved ephemeral headers. New cases cover malformed headers and expired responses before review, cancellation and expiry during review. Existing real HTTP/HTTPS LFS integrity, upload/verify/pull and redirect tests remain green. Core source/XML hashes are in `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-core-proof.json`; the fresh core run is `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-build.log` and is reused unchanged by the final combined build.
- Android result: **113 compatibility tests plus one real SSH/HTTP transport test passed** on the dedicated GitSync API 35 emulator (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-ezzpzg__/regression.log`, 342.293 seconds; `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-native-8e04grqy/regression.log` and its `proof.json`). The transport test checks zero downloads before review, exact hydrated bytes and clean status after approval, a separate upload review, zero uploads after rejection, successful approved upload/verification and encrypted reload/revocation. Independent host inspection verifies the pushed remote HEAD changed and its pointer names the exact uploaded 16,387-byte object. Ephemeral SSH authorization and private key material are absent from persisted approval values. The host fixture uses owned temporary SSH/HTTP servers and exact temporary reverse mappings; it restores the encrypted private baseline and stops/removes those resources in finally.
- Device testing exposed an existing encrypted-store startup bug: `init` reloaded approvals before `keyAlias` had been initialized. Per-record reads later worked, while the initial approval list reported a load error. Moving the alias before `init` fixes initial reloads; regression assertions cover both SSH permissions and existing Basic credentials. The first transport run reached hydration/upload but queried the initial list too early; adding a wait revealed that actual startup failure in the second run. The final passing run includes the production fix and retains the reload assertions. One subsequent host-harness check incorrectly expected three SSH upload authorizations for two attempts; its expectation was corrected to two and the complete fresh fixture run passes. Failed runs restored their private baselines and stopped their owned servers/mappings.
- Java 17 offline core/debug/test/release/lint-vital builds pass (`/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-reload-build.log`). Localization importer/expectation checks, ten tooling tests and `git diff --check` pass. Two new resources have assistant-authored translations in every non-English locale: **929 base / 307 translated / 622 English fallbacks**, three translated LFS plurals and 468 independent English source literals. Human linguistic review and broader screen/accessibility/motion qualification remain open.
- Inspected the synthetic HTTP SSH-authorization review capture in `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-final-visuals`. The protected runners restored/removed their private encrypted baselines. Cleanup: 54 standard and seven additional owned namespace counters all zero, no new LFS endpoint preference files, no LFS screen/store/SSH-HTTP workspaces or preferences. The 38 historical scoped LFS preference filenames remain unchanged. Device settings/permissions are restored and owned processes stopped. No preference or credential values were exported. Cleanup and device proof use the `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-` prefix.
- **Production networking remains unchanged.** The main manifest and exact debug loopback-only network policy match the prior phase; the app-wide cleartext proposal remains unapplied after automatic approval review rejected it and requested explicit authorization. HTTP SSH LFS is qualified in the debug loopback scope; release HTTP enablement and broader provider/storage/API/device/language/accessibility/motion qualification remain open. No release installation, external Git server, real account, cloud project or production relay was changed. The full parity goal remains active.
- Final evidence: `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-proof.json`, `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-final-authority-inputs.json` and `/tmp/syncmd-ssh-http-authority-receipt.json`.

### Commit-revert preflight and author-lookup freshness (2026-10-31)

- Compared the current iOS `LocalGitService.revertCommit` with Android `LocalHistoryRepository.revertCommit`: iOS checks local status before applying a revert; Android previously delegated directly to JGit. The new real-Git regression reproduced a successful revert with unrelated unstaged work and a revert admitted during an existing merge. The targeted red log is `/tmp/syncmd-revert-preflight-red.log`. Both checked-out apps currently retain commit-history/revert service methods without a visible commit-revert caller in their view/screen sources. This phase strengthens that service path and does **not** count a new user-facing history/revert screen as complete.
- `CREATE_NEW_COMMIT` now requires a clean working/index state and no active Git operation before author resolution. It captures the existing branch/HEAD/index/folder/config identity and verifies it after the potentially suspending resolver, then checks working state and operation metadata again immediately before JGit. A second regression failed before that freshness check when an external draft arrived during author lookup (`/tmp/syncmd-revert-author-window-red.log`). Guard rejection preserves the external writer's state instead of applying a revert to it. Application repository serialization remains in force; an atomic transaction with unrelated external writers during JGit's own writes is still not established.
- Fresh full core suite: **650 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips (`/tmp/syncmd-revert-preflight-core-proof.json`: 183 input hashes, 76 result XML files). Four new test methods cover five initial local-change kinds (staged, unstaged, partial staging, untracked and deleted), active merge/revert markers, a valid clean inverse commit with resolved identity and preserved unrelated ignored data, and five mutations during author lookup (working bytes, index, HEAD, configuration and merge metadata). Rejected initial states make zero author-resolver calls and retain checked HEAD/index/config/files/operation markers. The clean case creates exactly one inverse commit. The existing author/merge/stash/tag and broader core regressions remain green.
- Native Android result: **16 tests passed** on the owned API 35 GitSync emulator (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-86u0fyq_/regression.log`, 14.898s). Three new methods exercise the real production HistoryViewModel/service/JGit path with private owned bare remotes: dirty and active-operation rejection, an edit arriving after review while waiting for the repository lease, and a clean local revert that refreshes history without pushing. Working bytes/index/config/local HEAD/remote HEAD remain exact on rejection. The 13 existing history request-ownership, stale-row, duplicate-action and selection regressions also pass. This is ViewModel/runtime evidence, not new screen-navigation evidence.
- Java 17 offline core/debug/test/release/lint-vital build passed (`/tmp/syncmd-revert-preflight-final-build.log`, 3m 35s). The initial preflight build also passed before adding the author-window regression; the final build and native run establish the final artifact authority. Importer/native-expectation and device identity guard checks and diff whitespace checks pass. Localization coverage remains **950 base / 364 translated / 586 English fallbacks**, six translated plurals and 468 independent English literals. The new service diagnostics are English and remain in the runtime-error localization requirement.
- Private baselines were restored without exporting preference/credential values. Final audit reports **54 standard counters plus six new history/revert directory/preference/cache counters, all zero**. Original font/app language, notification flags, storage mode and rotation are unchanged; matching debug/test APK hashes are verified and those packages were force-stopped (`/tmp/syncmd-revert-native-4pdydkbv/proof.json`). No shared-storage permission was granted for these private-file tests. No physical device, release installation, external Git repository, cloud project or production relay was changed. Main/debug manifests and networking configuration remain unchanged.

Production HTTP policy is unchanged. ### Independent Push Sync status, provider failure and unavailable-platform opt-out (2026-11-01)

- Closed the two concrete Push Sync cases left by the preceding phase. Automatic screen entry/resume now reads status without requesting an SDK token or renewing registration. Manual refresh still maintains registration; a typed registration-stage failure permits an independent status read for the captured consent generation while preserving the registration error. A failed status read itself is not immediately retried. Known cards remain available with the error, and a later explicit refresh can renew registration.
- An enabled device can now opt out even when its messaging platform reports unavailable; enabling and GitHub linking remain disabled in that state. Opt-out clears local consent/events and performs the existing device unregister path without requiring SDK configuration. Native tests simulate platform unavailability through the messaging interface; they do not install a separately unconfigured Firebase build or certify later SDK reconfiguration.
- Status reads can pin an expected consent generation. Maintenance, callback and unlink follow-up reads use that pin. Missing-device responses recheck consent before resetting the registration cache and before returning; a delayed 404 from an older generation is rejected without changing replacement registration metadata. Registration fallback cannot read status for replacement consent. A current 404 still clears the old registration and allows explicit re-registration with the same device identity.
- A real 30-second SDK deadline now becomes a registration failure through `withTimeoutOrNull`; actual parent/user cancellation remains cancellation. Native coverage distinguishes the deadline from user-tapped Stop, checks that Stop issues no fallback read, and verifies fresh server-side connection removal after the deadline. ### Pull All entry cancellation and current-summary dispatch (2026-11-01)

- Reproduced five Pull All entry races with production screen/ViewModel/runner fixtures: Back during a pending Home summary read still started foreground work; a superseded empty read started another pull despite newer completed history; the pre-pull recheck found a completed summary but still ran; an older entry request caused a second real local pull after manual Start finished; and a stale manual-start read ignored a newer running batch. The expanded pre-fix cohort had **five failures / seven cases**, **6.908s** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-1_luij4m/regression.log`), while the two deliberate-execution controls passed. Private baseline restoration completed after this and the earlier four-case reproduction.
- PullAllViewModel now owns its pending Home job separately from foreground Git work. Back/Stop and manual Start cancel that pending entry; another Home entry replaces it. Background reads retain their revision alongside the result, so an obsolete empty read cannot authorize automatic work. Automatic Home entry requires a successful current empty result, and a completed/failed/interrupted summary remains available for review. Explicit Pull All Again still runs when no background batch is active; unreadable-summary handling and explicit summary clearing remain separate.
- A second minimal cohort exposed the catalog boundary: the real background store published a completed summary during automatic catalog loading, or a running summary during manual catalog loading. Both still dispatched one pull (**two failures / two cases**, **2.266s**, `/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-r835076s/regression.log`). Eligibility is now checked again after the catalog returns, immediately before runner dispatch. Automatic entry rejects an intervening read revision or any summary; both entry modes respect a newly observed running batch. Work already dispatched remains subject to the existing cancellation/Git-boundary behavior; this does not claim rollback of a completed Git operation.
- Added **nine native regressions** in PullAllScreenTest: seven ownership/read/control cases and two real-store publication cases. Back and Start are exercised through production controls. Delayed read gates make ownership deterministic; positive controls require exactly one real local pull from empty-history Home entry and after explicit saved-summary review. The publication cases require zero pull calls and preserve note bytes, HEAD, index, config, fixture-remote HEAD and the new summary. The first fix's seven focused cases passed (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-6qg1f1zw/regression.log`); the subsequent catalog cases were added before the final dispatch guard. These focused runs are repeated coverage, not additional distinct requirements.
- The final **68-case regression cohort passed**, no failure/skip/assumption statuses, **139.252s** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-arsyluzo/regression.log`). It includes all 19 Pull All screen cases plus the existing Push/background/Pull All store/transport, localization and production Push Settings-route/recreation/Back checks. - The existing real MainActivity **Home → Pull All → recreation → Back → widget execution → saved-summary review → hardware Back** case also passed in **2.738s** (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-5t41sr78/regression.log`; `/tmp/syncmd-pull-review-route-system-proof.json`). Its widget targets only the owned dirty repository fixture. This gives **69 distinct passing native checks in this phase**, with nine new regressions; it is not 69 newly added tests. No external Git/relay/Firebase call or production repository write is involved in the new cases.

### Responsive Pull All widget sizing and shorter German recovery action (2026-11-01)

- Reproduced clipped Arabic widget text at the former advertised **180×72dp** minimum (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-4mg6d2pa/regression.log`). Added compact/expanded native RemoteViews layouts. Both display the shorter translated Pull All title and retain the full accessible action description; expanded layouts also show the fetch/fast-forward scope. The compact minimum is now **180×112dp**, with a **3×2-cell** default. Expanded thresholds are **240×280dp** or **360×192dp**. Existing widgets below the new advertised minimum and arbitrary launcher grids still need upgrade/placement acceptance.
- Android 12+ receives a responsive size-to-layout map; older Android chooses from conservative widget option bounds, and resize callbacks update that selection. Locale refresh uses the same responsive payload. This follows Android's [flexible widget layout guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/appwidgets/layouts). No PendingIntent/execution semantics changed. A native unbound `AppWidgetHostView` checks responsive expansion/shrink-back and legacy options selection without allocating a widget ID, changing launcher bindings or clicking a pull action.
- Android 9 exposed additional real 200% text failures: the full German action/scope combination exceeded the expanded card (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-lcd5qnwv/regression.log`), then the short Hebrew title exceeded the first 96dp compact candidate (`/tmp/syncmd-protected-native-o97z7rp5/regression.log`). The final shorter title and 112dp minimum pass on **API 28 and API 35**: **225 renders per platform / 450 total**, all 25 languages × three font-configuration scales (1, 1.6, 2) × three layout bounds. Assertions verify the actual render font configuration, visible text width, full vertical layout, no ellipsis, accessible description and clickable/focusable root. Width checks exclude trailing whitespace using Android's documented [Layout.getLineMax](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/text/Layout#getLineMax(int)). These local configuration-context renders do not claim three global system-font changes or real launcher placement/gestures.

### Repository action and shortcut confirmation ownership (2026-11-01)

### Authorized-link reset preserves replacement data (2026-11-01)

- Reproduced a remaining destructive-recovery boundary through the production Repository Actions screen and ViewModel: open the unreadable-authorized-links reset dialog, replace the unreadable store, refresh while the dialog remains open and confirm. - The grant store now exposes an opaque recovery fingerprint and requires the exact reviewed fingerprint before clearing unreadable data. The ViewModel loads that version with recovery state; the dialog captures it when opened and passes it explicitly on confirmation. A refresh cannot replace the version retained by an open dialog. Healthy data and replacement unreadable data both fail closed. Fingerprints retain preference types and sort set contents; non-finite float values use string encoding so otherwise recoverable corrupt preferences do not break fingerprinting. Raw authorization data is neither displayed nor included in receipts. Production grant writes retain their existing synchronization; this is not a cross-process filesystem transaction guarantee.

### Existing pinned-shortcut labels follow app language (2026-11-01)

- Extracted one shared label formatter for new and existing repository shortcuts. A serialized IO refresh reads the current locale, compares labels and submits label-only `ShortcutInfo` patches for mutable pinned IDs that match private authorization and the private entry intent. Unknown IDs, unrelated/mismatched intents and immutable shortcuts are ignored. Unchanged labels consume no update call. Unreadable records are preserved without publication; locked-user and platform rate-limit responses request a WorkManager retry. No Git action, account lookup, authorization write, intent replacement or enable/disable operation occurs in production refresh.
- Added private locale/package-replacement broadcast wiring, app-configuration observation and startup recovery. The unique metadata worker also refreshes existing widgets with app resources. Startup keeps an already pending refresh; language changes replace it, and the process mutex ensures an older update completes before a newer locale snapshot is applied. The platform documents effective-locale broadcasts for device and app language changes ([Intent reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/Intent#ACTION_LOCALE_CHANGED)) and background-thread/rate-limit requirements for mutable shortcut updates ([ShortcutManager reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/pm/ShortcutManager#updateShortcuts(java.util.List%3Candroid.content.pm.ShortcutInfo%3E))).
- Six isolated native checks cover all actions/modes, unchanged labels, mismatched/unknown intents, quota/locked responses, corrupt-record preservation, revocation and Unicode boundaries. It verifies unchanged intent stacks, saved authorization, note/index/config/HEAD and file-remote HEAD, with no execution receipt or pending request. Relabeling after revocation retains the disabled state. Cleanup removes only the synthetic pin and restores app locales. This per-app-language method is gated to API 33+; current device evidence is API 35.

### Reviewed discard scope, iOS semantics and complete confirmation paths (2026-11-02, device acceptance in progress)

- Replaced production single-file and Revert All callbacks with an immutable `DiscardReview` from the actual repository. Confirmation consumes that exact review rather than obtaining fresh deletion authority from the current diff. A canonical-root lease plus Git’s real index lock protect validation and mutation; branch/HEAD/index/config/folder identity, operation metadata, checkout attributes and relevant file contents are checked. Single-file fingerprints leave unrelated later unstaged edits alone; all-file reviews include the working-tree scope. Same-size/same-mtime content edits are detected by streaming hashes, not only status timestamps. Metadata is checked again after preparing the index.
- Matched current iOS behavior: Revert All restores tracked files and also removes explicitly reviewed new/untracked files; an unborn branch only clears staging and preserves file contents. Single new-file discard deletes that selected file. Ignored files are retained, with ignored checkout obstructions rejected before mutation. A permitted active merge/cherry-pick/revert is explicitly identified as abandoned by an all-file review. A selected file does not silently abandon the operation. Unsupported rebase/submodule/nested-repository or linked-parent cases require the appropriate existing Git workflow/manual review.
- Checkout works against an owned private index and atomically publishes it while the real `index.lock` remains held through operation-metadata cleanup. This avoids JGit checkout releasing the shared lock too early. Foreign locks are preserved. Successful cases remove private-index/lock artifacts. This is not a power-loss recovery journal or a guarantee against writers that ignore Git’s locks after validation; I/O failures after approved writes can leave partial work and require a fresh review.
- DiffViewModel now owns review requests, rejects duplicate/stale confirmations and queued mutations after repository switches or same-repository re-entry, clears busy state after failures, and refreshes the selected file preview before displaying an operation error. Read-only observation refreshes preserve the frozen review and active mutation owner: the existing observation source emits a 30-second fallback even with no actual edit. Confirmation still revalidates repository state. Screen disposal/new visits dismiss pending review authority.
- The production dialog displays every full relative path, with no six-file cap, basename substitution or ellipsis. One bounded lazy list includes the full scope and both actions, so they can be reached by scrolling; named close/cancel/confirm buttons have at least 48dp targets. Paths retain LTR identifier rendering in RTL layouts. Count text uses an Android plural resource, and labels no longer force default-locale uppercase or use the English `s` suffix. The unborn action says Clear staging; zero applicable changes cannot be confirmed.
- Added five messages (**125 translated string values**) and one plural resource across all 25 locales. Coverage is now **892/1,049**, with **157 English fallback keys** and **seven translated plurals** per language. Import/native-expectation checks and **22 localization tooling tests pass**. Assistant-authored translations still need linguistic review; other model/action/exception text remains outside this batch. The static fallback reference audit is `/tmp/syncmd-localization-remaining-892.json` (152 of 157 fallback keys have production-source references, which can include unused composables).
- **673 core tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips, in 78 suites. **18 new real-Git cases** cover both index/worktree layers, distinct paths, new-file deletion, unrelated later edits, attribute changes, unborn branches, stale content/index/config/branch/operation/folder identity, foreign lock retention, ignored collisions, file/directory obstructions, symlinks, cached LFS restoration, real conflicted-merge cleanup and successful lock/private-index cleanup. A new regression first failed when unrelated later edits were incorrectly part of a single-file fingerprint; the narrowed target/attribute scope now passes. Full current authority is `/tmp/syncmd-reviewed-discard-final-core-proof.json`, with 190 core input hashes and 78 archived XML hashes.
- Final debug, AndroidTest and release APKs plus release lint **passed in 3m 16s**. All **557 frozen input hashes** match. Proof is `/tmp/syncmd-reviewed-discard-proof.json`; build log `/tmp/syncmd-reviewed-discard-qualified-build.log`. Six new native fixtures compile (four ViewModel ownership/error/retry cases and two dialog cases), but **none has executed on the Pixel**. Component-local 25-language/160% layout assertions are authored, not device evidence. Full system-locale, TalkBack, motion and wider filesystem/device acceptance remain separate.
- Installed the release in place on physical Pixel 7 `2C061FDH200CJN`; package/version and same signer/prior APK were checked, and APK readback matches SHA-256 **`91d239803bb67b4e82396200d462ad54d9dcb0b1461825ed7ed4e2613b227ac5`**. Receipt: `/tmp/syncmd-pixel-892-update-proof.json`. No app data was cleared or permissions pre-granted. No emulator or new public repository/commit/push was used in this phase.

### LFS queued request ownership across selection/reload/disposal (2026-11-02)

- Reproduced an actual old-repository write with a production `LfsViewModel`, a real generated Git repository and a held canonical lease: a tracking confirmation waited, the selection changed, and the superseded request still created `.gitattributes` in the first repository. The qualified failing XML is retained; an earlier macOS canonical-path fixture mismatch is not counted as the bug reproduction.
- Each explicit load now creates a fresh request identity, including same-folder reloads. Working-file actions and the shared lock dispatcher check that identity **after acquiring the repository lease**, before entering the mutation/service action. Clearing the ViewModel revokes queued work, including the existing non-cancellable lock path. Accepted tracking/hydration actions retain the lease through post-action file/plan reads; finally blocks clear busy/back flags for the current request. An action that already passed the entry check retains its existing service/cancellation semantics; no rollback or cancellation of an already-started server change is claimed.
- **Eight local JVM tests pass**, zero failures/errors/skips. Five use real temporary repositories to check selected-repository changes, explicit same-folder reload, extension-rule application, cached hydration and one valid duplicate-suppressed tracking action, including exact index/working bytes, HEAD and fresh screen state. ### Remaining Settings confirmation ownership audit (2026-11-02)

- Source inspection found that `SettingsScreen` keeps sign-out confirmation as a Boolean, derives its subject from the current login, and calls parameterless `GitHubViewModel.signOut()`. `GitHubAuthManager.signOut()` removes the account active at execution. The confirmation does not retain the reviewed account/session revision. Account/session-bound confirmation, a store-boundary revision check and stale-confirm regression coverage remain implementation work; this is a source finding, not a claimed device reproduction. The nine untranslated Tide Settings keys also remain open.
